# 1.18.4 Model

- [A Siamese-based Verification System for Open-set Architecture Attribution of Synthetic Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.09822): Trains a Siamese verifier to attribute synthetic images to previously unseen generator architectures from learned image-pair similarity signals.
- [Adapting Large Language Models via Reading Comprehension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.09530): Adapts LLMs by converting task data into reading-comprehension style examples, using question answering supervision as a lightweight fine-tuning route.
- [CACFNet: Cross-Modal Attention Cascaded Fusion Network for RGB-T Urban Scene Parsing](https://doi.org/10.1109/tiv.2023.3314527): Uses cascaded cross-modal attention to fuse RGB and thermal features for urban scene parsing under varying illumination and weather conditions.
- [Machine Unlearning via Representation Forgetting With Parameter Self-Sharing](https://doi.org/10.1109/tifs.2023.3331239): Removes targeted knowledge by suppressing learned representations while sharing parameters to preserve the rest of the model.
- [Joint inter-word and inter-sentence multi-relation modeling for summary-based recommender system](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ipm.2023.103631): Models word-level and sentence-level relations jointly so summary-based recommenders can use both local phrase cues and document-level semantics.
- [FedSea: Federated Learning via Selective Feature Alignment for Non-IID Multimodal Data](https://doi.org/10.1109/tmm.2023.3340109): Aligns selected multimodal features across clients to make federated training more robust under non-IID data and missing-modality variation.
- [Benchmarking Large Language Models on Controllable Generation under Diversified Instructions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.00690): Tests whether LLMs can follow diverse controllable-generation instructions, exposing where control attributes fail across prompt formulations.
- [Quokka: An Open-source Large Language Model ChatBot for Material Science](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.01089): Releases a material-science chatbot built from domain instruction data so LLM assistance can cover materials terminology, synthesis, and property questions.
- [Unifying Structured Data as Graph for Data-to-Text Pre-Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.01183): Represents tables, trees, and other structured inputs as graphs for data-to-text pre-training, giving generation models a shared structural interface.
- [A Comprehensive Study of Knowledge Editing for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.01286): Compares knowledge-editing methods across reliability, generalization, locality, and portability to map practical tradeoffs in modifying LLM facts.
- [Self-Play Fine-Tuning Converts Weak Language Models to Strong Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.01335): Uses self-play generated preference data to fine-tune weak language models into stronger assistants without relying only on external human labels.
- [Large Language Models Relearn Removed Concepts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.01814): Shows that unlearned concepts can reappear after additional training or prompting, making relearning resistance a core requirement for model editing.
- [A Vision Check-up for Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.01862): Probes how language models handle vision-derived concepts and visual reasoning claims, separating text-learned visual knowledge from grounded perception.
- [Theoretical guarantees on the best-of-n alignment policy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.01879): Analyzes best-of-n sampling as an inference-time alignment policy, correcting a common KL-divergence formula and bounding the attainable win rate against the reference policy.
- [LLaVA-Phi: Efficient Multi-Modal Assistant with Small Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.02330): Builds an efficient multimodal assistant by pairing LLaVA-style visual instruction tuning with a small Phi language backbone.
- [TinyLlama: An Open-Source Small Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.02385): Trains and releases a compact 1.1B-parameter Llama-style model on a trillion-token scale as a reproducible small-LM baseline.
- [LLM Augmented LLMs: Expanding Capabilities through Composition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.02412): Proposes CALM, which composes an existing large language model with a specialist model by adding cross-attention between their representations instead of retraining one monolithic model.
- [LLaMA Pro: Progressive LLaMA with Block Expansion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.02415): Expands a LLaMA2 backbone with additional Transformer blocks and post-pretrains only the new blocks on code and math data to add skills while reducing catastrophic forgetting.
- [CoCoT: Contrastive Chain-of-Thought Prompting for Large Multimodal Models with Multiple Image Inputs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.02582): Uses contrastive chain-of-thought examples to help multimodal models compare multiple images before answering.
- [VoxelNextFusion: A Simple, Unified, and Effective Voxel Fusion Framework for Multimodal 3-D Object Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.02702): Fuses LiDAR and camera signals inside a unified voxel detection pipeline for multimodal 3-D object detection.
- [Parameter-Efficient Sparsity Crafting from Dense to Mixture-of-Experts for Instruction Tuning on General Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.02731): Converts dense instruction-tuned models toward sparse mixture-of-experts behavior with parameter-efficient updates rather than full retraining.
- [DeepSeek LLM: Scaling Open-Source Language Models with Longtermism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.02954): Describes DeepSeek LLM scaling choices for open-source language models, adding a general model-family reference.
- [TeleChat Technical Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.03804): Documents the TeleChat Chinese-English chat model family, including pretraining, alignment, and evaluation choices for an open conversational LLM.
- [A Minimaximalist Approach to Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.04056): Introduces Self-Play Preference Optimization, framing preference learning as a zero-sum game between policies so RLHF can handle non-Markovian, intransitive, and stochastic preferences without a separate reward model.
- [MoE-Mamba: Efficient Selective State Space Models with Mixture of Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.04081): Combines Mamba selective state-space layers with mixture-of-experts routing, reaching Mamba-level performance in fewer training steps while preserving efficient inference.
- [Mixtral of Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.04088): Documents the Mixtral sparse mixture-of-experts language-model architecture, a reusable model-side scaling pattern for capability pages.
- [Chain of LoRA: Efficient Fine-tuning of Language Models via Residual Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.04151): Uses a Frank-Wolfe-style residual learning loop that repeatedly trains and merges LoRA modules, narrowing the gap to full fine-tuning without extra inference-time adapters.
- [MERA: A Comprehensive LLM Evaluation in Russian](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.04531): Provides a Russian-language evaluation suite for LLMs, covering reasoning, knowledge, safety, and language-specific behavior across model families.
- [Effective pruning of web-scale datasets based on complexity of concept clusters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.04578): Scales CLIP-style dataset pruning to LAION by assigning concept-specific pruning rates from cluster complexity, cutting training cost while retaining useful multimodal coverage.
- [Lightning Attention-2: A Free Lunch for Handling Unlimited Sequence Lengths in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.04658): Reformulates linear attention to support very long sequences with efficient training and inference while keeping Transformer-style modeling.
- [Cross-modal Retrieval for Knowledge-based Visual Question Answering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.05736): Retrieves external visual and textual knowledge across modalities before answering knowledge-intensive visual questions.
- [LLM-as-a-Coauthor: Can Mixed Human-Written and Machine-Generated Text Be Detected?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.05952): Studies detection of documents jointly written by humans and LLMs, where mixed authorship weakens assumptions used by standard generated-text detectors.
- [Investigating Data Contamination for Pre-training Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.06059): Pretrains GPT-2 variants from scratch with controlled text and ground-truth contamination to measure how benchmark leakage affects downstream scores.
- [Secrets of RLHF in Large Language Models Part II: Reward Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.06080): Studies reward-model failure modes in RLHF and combines data filtering with distribution-aware reward modeling to improve generalization during iterative alignment.
- [Improving Large Language Models via Fine-grained Reinforcement Learning with Minimum Editing Constraint](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.06081): Proposes RLMEC, a token-level RL objective where a generative reward model rewrites erroneous solutions under a minimum-editing constraint to focus updates on key error tokens.
- [Transformers are Multi-State RNNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.06104): Interprets Transformer computation as a multi-state recurrent process, connecting attention dynamics to recurrent sequence modeling.
- [APAR: LLMs Can Do Auto-Parallel Auto-Regressive Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.06761): Fine-tunes LLMs to plan hierarchical generation and emit independent spans in parallel, reducing autoregressive decoding steps and complementing speculative decoding.
- [AI Hallucinations: A Misnomer Worth Clarifying](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.06796): Reframes "hallucination" as a family of factuality and grounding failures, clarifying which errors need model, data, or evaluation fixes.
- [E^2-LLM: Efficient and Extreme Length Extension of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.06951): Extends LLM context length with an efficient training recipe designed for extreme-length inputs rather than full long-context retraining.
- [Bridging the Preference Gap between Retrievers and LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.06954): Aligns retrieval models with LLM answer preferences so retrieved passages better match what the downstream generator can use.
- [Extending LLMs' Context Window with 100 Samples](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.07004): Demonstrates a small-data context-extension recipe that lengthens an LLM's usable window with roughly one hundred long examples.
- [Evolving code with a large language model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.07102): Uses an LLM-driven evolutionary loop to mutate and select code candidates, treating generation as iterative program search.
- [Model Editing at Scale leads to Gradual and Catastrophic Forgetting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.07453): Shows that repeated large-scale model edits can accumulate into gradual and then catastrophic forgetting outside the edited facts.
- [SAPT: A Shared Attention Framework for Parameter-Efficient Continual Learning of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.08295): Shares attention components across tasks to support parameter-efficient continual learning while limiting forgetting in LLMs.
- [DoraemonGPT: Toward Understanding Dynamic Scenes with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.08392): Couples LLM reasoning with dynamic-scene understanding modules so video-like events can be described and queried over time.
- [EmoLLMs: A Series of Emotional Large Language Models and Annotation Tools for Comprehensive Affective Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.08508): Releases emotion-specialized LLMs and annotation tools for affective analysis across emotion recognition, cause detection, and response generation.
- [Scalable Pre-training of Large Autoregressive Image Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.08541): Scales autoregressive image modeling with a pretraining recipe that treats image tokens as the primary generation sequence.
- [Tuning Language Models by Proxy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.08565): Provides a decoding-time adaptation method that shifts a black-box model's output distribution using the difference between a smaller tuned proxy and its untuned counterpart.
- [Deductive Closure Training of Language Models for Coherence, Accuracy, and Updatability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.08574): Uses language models to generate and verify implications from trusted seed documents, then fine-tunes on inferred correct text to improve factual coherence and model updatability.
- [EgoGen: An Egocentric Synthetic Data Generator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.08739): Generates egocentric synthetic data to train and evaluate models on first-person perception without relying only on costly captured videos.
- [Beyond Anti-Forgetting: Multimodal Continual Instruction Tuning with Positive Forward Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.09181): Diagnoses embedding-distribution mismatch in continual instruction tuning for MLLMs and introduces a prompt-tuning method that reduces forgetting while improving transfer to future tasks.
- [BENO: Boundary-embedded Neural Operators for Elliptic PDEs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.09323): Embeds boundary conditions directly into neural operators to improve elliptic PDE solution accuracy on irregular domains.
- [Self-Rewarding Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.10020): Trains LLMs to generate their own rewards for instruction following, enabling iterative self-improvement without a fixed external reward model.
- [Beyond Traditional Benchmarks: Analyzing Behaviors of Open LLMs on Data-to-Text Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.10186): Analyzes open LLM behavior on data-to-text generation beyond aggregate scores, including faithfulness, structure use, and controllability.
- [OMG-Seg: Is One Model Good Enough for all Segmentation?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.10229): Builds a unified segmentation model that handles multiple segmentation tasks instead of training separate specialists.
- [Critical Data Size of Language Models from a Grokking Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.10463): Studies language-model data thresholds through grokking behavior, relating delayed generalization to the amount of training data.
- [Knowledge Fusion of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.10491): Transfers capabilities from multiple source LLMs into one target model by distilling their generative distributions, avoiding direct weight merging across incompatible architectures.
- [Sowing the Wind, Reaping the Whirlwind: The Impact of Editing Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.10647): Examines unintended downstream effects of language-model editing, showing how local fact changes can disrupt broader generations.
- [Medusa: Simple LLM Inference Acceleration Framework with Multiple Decoding Heads](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.10774): Adds extra decoding heads and tree attention to propose and verify several future tokens in one step, accelerating autoregressive LLM inference without a separate draft model.
- [Metacognition is all you need? Using Introspection in Generative Agents to Improve Goal-directed Behavior](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.10910): Adds introspective self-assessment to generative agents so they can revise plans and improve goal-directed behavior over time.
- [Crowd-PrefRL: Preference-Based Reward Learning from Crowds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.10941): Extends preference-based reinforcement learning to crowd feedback by modeling multiple user groups and aggregating pairwise behavior preferences instead of assuming a single human reward source.
- [Inference without Interference: Disaggregate LLM Inference for Mixed Downstream Workloads](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.11181): Separates inference resources for mixed LLM workloads to reduce latency interference between prefill, decoding, and downstream serving demands.
- [Identifying and Analyzing Performance-Critical Tokens in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.11323): Identifies tokens whose hidden states disproportionately affect model performance, giving a token-level lens on LLM failure and intervention.
- [Language Models as Hierarchy Encoders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.11374): Uses language-model representations to encode hierarchical structures, testing how pretrained models capture parent-child and taxonomy relations.
- [Scalable High-Resolution Pixel-Space Image Synthesis with Hourglass Diffusion Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.11605): Introduces hourglass-shaped diffusion Transformers for high-resolution pixel-space image synthesis with scalable compute allocation.
- [Blinded by Generated Contexts: How Language Models Merge Generated and Retrieved Contexts for Open-Domain QA?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.11911): Studies how LLMs combine retrieved evidence with self-generated context in open-domain QA, exposing failure modes when generated context dominates.
- [West-of-N: Synthetic Preferences for Self-Improving Reward Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.12086): Creates synthetic preference comparisons from best-of-n and worst-of-n samples to train reward models that can improve themselves.
- [Universal Neurons in GPT2 Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.12181): Finds neurons in GPT-2 variants that encode similar functions across models, supporting neuron-level comparisons and interventions.
- [WARM: On the Benefits of Weight Averaged Reward Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.12187): Averages the weights of several fine-tuned reward models to make RLHF rewards more reliable under distribution shift while avoiding the serving cost of prediction ensembles.
- [APT: Adaptive Pruning and Tuning Pretrained Language Models for Efficient Training and Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.12200): Combines adaptive pruning with tuning so pretrained language models can be compressed for cheaper training and inference.
- [Small Language Model Meets with Reinforced Vision Vocabulary](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.12503): Strengthens a small multimodal language model by reinforcing its visual vocabulary rather than scaling the language backbone.
- [Can Large Language Models Write Parallel Code?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.12554): Evaluates and analyzes LLM generation of parallel code, focusing on whether models preserve correctness while using concurrency constructs.
- [LLMCheckup: Conversational Examination of Large Language Models via Interpretability Tools and Self-Explanations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.12576): Combines interpretability tools with model self-explanations in a conversational interface for inspecting LLM behavior.
- [SLANG: New Concept Comprehension of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.12585): Tests how LLMs acquire and use newly defined concepts, probing concept learning beyond memorized vocabulary.
- [Transformer-Based Models Are Not Yet Perfect At Learning to Emulate Structural Recursion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.12947): Shows that Transformer models still struggle to emulate structural recursion robustly, especially under compositional generalization tests.
- [Meta-Prompting: Enhancing Language Models with Task-Agnostic Scaffolding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.12954): Uses task-agnostic meta-prompts to structure decomposition, verification, and synthesis around a base LLM without task-specific fine-tuning.
- [Deep Learning Model Reuse in the HuggingFace Community: Challenges, Benefit and Trends](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.13177): Analyzes Hugging Face model reuse patterns to identify common benefits, dependency risks, and maintenance challenges in model ecosystems.
- [ULTRA: Unleash LLMs' Potential for Event Argument Extraction through Hierarchical Modeling and Pair-wise Self-Refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.13218): Improves event argument extraction by combining hierarchical event modeling with pairwise self-refinement from LLM outputs.
- [Finetuning foundation models for joint analysis optimization in High Energy Physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.13536): Fine-tunes foundation models to optimize high-energy physics analyses jointly across selection, inference, and domain-specific objectives.
- [MambaByte: Token-free Selective State Space Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.13660): Adapts Mamba to byte-level autoregressive language modeling, avoiding subword tokenization while using fixed-size state-space memory for long raw-byte sequences.
- [LocMoE: A Low-overhead MoE for Large Language Model Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.13920): Introduces locality-aware expert routing for MoE LLM training, trading some inter-node all-to-all traffic for intra-node communication while maintaining load balance.
- [Routoo: Learning to Route to Large Language Models Effectively](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.13979): Learns when to route user requests to different LLMs so cost and quality can be balanced across model choices.
- [Towards Consistent Natural-Language Explanations via Explanation-Consistency Finetuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.13986): Builds synthetic related-example sets and fine-tunes LLMs to produce explanations that stay consistent across linked questions, improving explanation reliability beyond single-example fluency.
- [ConstraintChecker: A Plugin for Large Language Models to Reason on Commonsense Knowledge Bases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.14003): Checks LLM outputs against commonsense knowledge-base constraints so reasoning can be validated against explicit symbolic relations.
- [Genie: Achieving Human Parity in Content-Grounded Datasets Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.14367): Generates content-grounded datasets with LLM assistance, targeting human-level quality in grounded question and answer construction.
- [TURNA: A Turkish Encoder-Decoder Language Model for Enhanced Understanding and Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.14373): Trains a Turkish encoder-decoder language model for both understanding and generation in a lower-resource language setting.
- [Prompt Design and Engineering: Introduction and Advanced Methods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.14423): Systematizes prompt design methods from basic instruction patterns to advanced decomposition, retrieval, and evaluation techniques.
- [Looking Right is Sometimes Right: Investigating the Capabilities of Decoder-only LLMs for Sequence Labeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.14556): Studies decoder-only LLMs on sequence labeling and shows how right-context access changes token-level prediction behavior.
- [Can LLMs Evaluate Complex Attribution in QA? Automatic Benchmarking using Knowledge Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.14640): Uses knowledge graphs to automatically generate complex attribution checks for evaluating whether LLM QA judgments cite the right evidence.
- [Airavata: Introducing Hindi Instruction-tuned LLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.15006): Releases a Hindi instruction-tuned LLM built to improve open-domain chat and task following for Hindi users.
- [EAGLE: Speculative Sampling Requires Rethinking Feature Uncertainty](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.15077): Predicts future hidden features rather than only tokens for speculative sampling, improving draft verification efficiency.
- [Large Language Model Guided Knowledge Distillation for Time Series Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.15123): Uses LLM guidance to distill knowledge into time-series anomaly detectors, transferring semantic priors into a specialized detection model.
- [Efficient Tuning and Inference for Large Language Models on Textual Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.15569): Tunes LLMs for textual graphs with methods that reduce graph-token overhead during training and inference.
- [LLaVA-MoLE: Sparse Mixture of LoRA Experts for Mitigating Data Conflicts in Instruction Finetuning MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.16160): Uses sparse LoRA experts in multimodal instruction tuning to route conflicting data types to different adapters.
- [Iterative Data Smoothing: Mitigating Reward Overfitting and Overoptimization in RLHF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.16335): Smooths preference data iteratively to reduce reward-model overfitting and policy overoptimization during RLHF.
- [Scaling Sparse Fine-Tuning to Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.16405): Scales sparse fine-tuning methods to large LMs so only selected parameters update while task performance remains competitive.
- [InternLM-XComposer2: Mastering Free-form Text-Image Composition and Comprehension in Vision-Language Large Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.16420): Extends InternLM-XComposer for free-form text-image composition and comprehension with stronger vision-language alignment.
- [Improving Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback with Efficient Reward Model Ensemble](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.16635): Builds efficient reward-model ensembles to improve RLHF robustness without the full serving cost of large ensembles.
- [Diff-eRank: A Novel Rank-Based Metric for Evaluating Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.17139): Proposes a rank-based metric for LLM evaluation that compares relative ordering quality rather than only absolute scores.
- [MouSi: Poly-Visual-Expert Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.17221): Combines multiple visual experts inside a vision-language model so specialist perceptual skills can be routed to downstream tasks.
- [Infini-gram: Scaling Unbounded n-gram Language Models to a Trillion Tokens](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.17377): Scales n-gram language modeling to trillion-token corpora with unbounded context queries, offering a retrieval-like non-neural baseline.
- [Proximity QA: Unleashing the Power of Multi-Modal Large Language Models for Spatial Proximity Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.17862): Uses multimodal LLMs to answer spatial proximity questions that require comparing object locations and distances in visual scenes.
- [Convolution Meets LoRA: Parameter Efficient Finetuning for Segment Anything Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.17868): Adds convolutional structure to LoRA adapters for parameter-efficient fine-tuning of the Segment Anything Model.
- [AEROBLADE: Training-Free Detection of Latent Diffusion Images Using Autoencoder Reconstruction Error](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.17879): Detects latent-diffusion images without training by measuring reconstruction errors through the model autoencoder.
- [Efficient Exploration for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.00396): Improves LLM exploration in decision problems by guiding sampling toward informative alternatives instead of repeated high-probability outputs.
- [Merging Multi-Task Models via Weight-Ensembling Mixture of Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.00433): Merges task-specific models by weight ensembling inside a mixture-of-experts structure rather than averaging all parameters into one dense model.
- [Superfiltering: Weak-to-Strong Data Filtering for Fast Instruction-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.00530): Uses weak models to filter instruction data that most benefits stronger models, reducing fine-tuning cost while preserving gains.
- [Transforming and Combining Rewards for Aligning Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.00742): Studies how reward transformations and reward combinations affect LLM alignment optimization and final policy behavior.
- [Dense Reward for Free in Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.00782): Derives denser token-level reward signals from existing preference models to make RLHF updates less sparse.
- [Graph-Mamba: Towards Long-Range Graph Sequence Modeling with Selective State Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.00789): Applies selective state-space modeling to graph sequences so long-range graph dependencies can be captured efficiently.
- [OLMo: Accelerating the Science of Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.00838): Releases OLMo as an open language-model science artifact, including model and training details useful for reproducible capability analysis.
- [Investigating Recurrent Transformers with Dynamic Halt](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.00976): Compares depth-wise and chunk-wise recurrent Transformer variants, including global-mean dynamic halting, on LRA, flip-flop language modeling, ListOps, and logical inference diagnostics.
- [Compositional Generative Modeling: A Single Model is Not All You Need](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01103): Argues for composing smaller generative models into programmable systems that generalize to unseen data regions and unseen tasks more data-efficiently than monolithic models.
- [Simulation of Graph Algorithms with Looped Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01107): Proves that looped Transformers with graph-interacting attention heads can simulate algorithms such as Dijkstra, breadth-first search, depth-first search, and Kosaraju with graph-size-independent parameters.
- [ReEvo: Large Language Models as Hyper-Heuristics with Reflective Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01145): Combines evolutionary search with LLM-generated reflections as verbal gradients to design heuristics across multiple combinatorial optimization problem families.
- [Efficient Causal Graph Discovery Using Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01207): Replaces pairwise causal queries with a breadth-first LLM querying strategy, reducing graph-discovery calls from quadratic to linear and optionally incorporating observational data.
- [KTO: Model Alignment as Prospect Theoretic Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01306): Aligns LLMs with a Kahneman-Tversky utility objective that learns from binary desirable-or-undesirable feedback instead of pairwise preferences.
- [LoTR: Low Tensor Rank Weight Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01376): Extends LoRA by representing layer updates with shared low-rank tensor decompositions, improving parameter efficiency for deep-model fine-tuning.
- [Integrating Large Language Models in Causal Discovery: A Statistical Causal Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01454): Combines statistical causal discovery with LLM-provided background constraints through statistical causal prompting and prior-knowledge augmentation.
- [OpenMoE: An Early Effort on Open Mixture-of-Experts Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01739): Releases reproducible decoder-only MoE LLMs from 650M to 34B parameters and analyzes routing patterns such as token-driven specialization and late-token dropping.
- [Rethinking Interpretability in the Era of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01761): Reviews both interpreting LLMs and using LLMs for explanation, emphasizing natural-language explanations, hallucinated rationales, and computational-cost limits.
- [BlackMamba: Mixture of Experts for State-Space Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01771): Combines Mamba state-space layers with mixture-of-experts routing and releases BlackMamba checkpoints that trade a larger memory footprint for lower training and inference FLOPs.
- [When Benchmarks are Targets: Revealing the Sensitivity of Large Language Model Leaderboards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01781): Shows that small multiple-choice benchmark perturbations, including answer-order and scoring changes, can move LLM leaderboard rankings by up to eight positions.
- [(A)I Am Not a Lawyer, But...: Engaging Legal Experts towards Responsible LLM Policies for Legal Advice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01864): Derives a four-dimensional policy framework for legal-advice responses from workshops where legal experts judged realistic user-query cases.
- [LiPO: Listwise Preference Optimization through Learning-to-Rank](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01878): Frames LLM alignment as listwise ranking so policies can learn directly from ordered sets of multiple responses rather than isolated pairwise preferences.
- [Self-Debiasing Large Language Models: Zero-Shot Recognition and Reduction of Stereotypes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01981): Uses explanation and reprompting strategies with the same LLM to recognize invalid stereotype assumptions and reduce bias without parameter updates.
- [Panacea: Pareto Alignment via Preference Adaptation for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02030): Treats alignment as multidimensional preference optimization and injects online preference vectors through SVD-based low-rank adaptation.
- [Calibration and Correctness of Language Models for Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02047): Builds a calibration framework for code-generating models and tests whether confidence signals, including Platt-scaled ones, track generated-code correctness.
- [Break the Sequential Dependency of LLM Inference Using Lookahead Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02057): Introduces exact parallel lookahead decoding that reduces autoregressive decoding steps without a draft model or external data store.
- [Are Large Language Models Good Prompt Optimizers?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02101): Audits LLM-based automatic prompt optimization and finds that optimizer reflections often misidentify error causes or fail after one refinement step.
- [Selecting Large Language Model to Fine-tune via Rectified Scaling Law](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02314): Predicts fine-tuning performance with a rectified scaling law that models a pre-power phase via pre-learned data size.
- [NetLLM: Adapting Large Language Models for Networking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02338): Adapts an LLM into a networking foundation model intended to replace task-specific neural designs with one model across prediction and optimization tasks.
- [Transolver: A Fast Transformer Solver for PDEs on General Geometries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02366): Uses Physics-Attention to group mesh points into learnable physical-state slices, enabling linear-complexity PDE solving on complex geometries.
- [Timer: Generative Pre-trained Transformers Are Large Time Series Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02368): Pretrains a generative Transformer on up to one billion time points after unifying heterogeneous time series into a shared token sequence.
- [DeLLMa: Decision Making Under Uncertainty with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02392): Wraps LLM inference in a decision-theoretic multi-step reasoning procedure for auditable choices under uncertainty.
- [Aligner: Efficient Alignment by Learning to Correct](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02416): Trains a small plug-and-play model to learn residual corrections between preferred and dispreferred answers for rapid alignment of upstream models.
- [TimeSiam: A Pre-Training Framework for Siamese Time-Series Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02475): Pretrains Siamese time-series encoders on past and current subseries so temporal correlations are preserved instead of distorted by random masking alone.
- [LHRS-Bot: Empowering Remote Sensing with VGI-Enhanced Large Multimodal Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02544): Builds a remote-sensing MLLM from VGI-enriched LHRS-Align and LHRS-Instruct data with multi-level vision-language alignment and curriculum learning.
- [Generalizable Entity Grounding via Assistance of Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02555): Grounds nouns from long captions by combining an LMM, class-agnostic segmentation, multimodal feature fusion, and colormap-encoded masks.
- [Vision-Language Models Provide Promptable Representations for Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02651): Uses prompted VLM embeddings as semantic visual representations for embodied RL policies in Minecraft and Habitat navigation tasks.
- [Large Language Models are Geographically Biased](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02680): Measures LLM geographic bias by comparing zero-shot geospatial predictions about culture, language, politics, religion, and related attributes against ground truth.
- [Position: What Can Large Language Models Tell Us about Time Series Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02713): Positions LLMs as a route toward broader time-series intelligence, including modality switching and question answering beyond prediction-only models.
- [Fine-tuning Reinforcement Learning Models is Secretly a Forgetting Mitigation Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02868): Shows that RL fine-tuning can catastrophically forget pretrained behavior on downstream state regions that are not visited early in adaptation.
- [On Least Square Estimation in Softmax Gating Mixture of Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02952): Analyzes least-squares estimation for deterministic softmax-gated MoE regression and introduces strong identifiability conditions for convergence behavior.
- [Swin-UMamba: Mamba-based UNet with ImageNet-based pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03302): Integrates Mamba sequence modeling into a U-Net-style segmentation backbone and studies ImageNet pretraining for data-efficient medical image segmentation.
- [HAMLET: Graph Transformer Neural Operator for Partial Differential Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03541): Uses graph Transformers with modular input encoders to solve PDEs across arbitrary geometries and varied input formats under noise or limited data.
- [Distinguishing the Knowable from the Unknowable with Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03563): Trains probes on frozen LLM embeddings, and also tests an unsupervised variant, to separate epistemic uncertainty from aleatoric uncertainty in free-form text.
- [Similarity-based Neighbor Selection for Graph LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03720): Proposes similarity-based neighbor selection for graph-aware language models.
- [MobileVLM V2: Faster and Stronger Baseline for Vision Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03766): Improves the mobile-oriented MobileVLM family with a revised architecture, training scheme, and curated data, letting 1.7B and 3B VLMs compete with substantially larger baselines.
- [ReLU2 Wins: Discovering Efficient Activation Functions for Sparse LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03804): Reframes activation sparsity beyond zero-valued ReLU neurons, evaluates ReLU, SwiGLU, ReGLU, and ReLU2 on sparsity-performance tradeoffs, predictivity, and hardware affinity, and identifies ReLU2 as the strongest activation for sparse LLM inference.
- [MOMENT: A Family of Open Time-series Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03885): Releases open time-series foundation models, the Time-series Pile, and a limited-supervision benchmark covering forecasting, classification, anomaly detection, and imputation-style tasks.
- [DistiLLM: Towards Streamlined Distillation for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03898): Distills autoregressive LLMs with a skew KL loss and adaptive off-policy use of student generations, reducing student-training cost relative to recent KD methods.
- [Discovery of the Hidden World with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03941): Introduces COAT, where LLMs propose high-level causal factors and measurements while causal discovery feedback iteratively refines those variables.
- [Systematic Biases in LLM Simulations of Debates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.04049): Shows debate-simulation agents drift toward the base LLM's social biases even under assigned political perspectives, then manipulates that bias through self-fine-tuning.
- [Provably Learning a Multi-head Attention Layer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.04084): Gives upper and lower bounds for learning a multi-head attention layer from random Boolean sequence examples, including an algorithm under non-degeneracy assumptions.
- [U-shaped Vision Mamba for Single Image Dehazing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.04139): Builds UVM-Net with Bi-SSM blocks that combine convolutional local features and state-space long-range modeling for fast single-image dehazing.
- [Attention with Markov: A Framework for Principled Analysis of Transformers via Markov Chains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.04161): Analyzes transformer behavior on Markov-chain inputs, contrasting deeper induction-head learning with single-layer convergence to unigram local minima.
- [Scaling Laws for Downstream Task Performance of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.04177): Studies translation fine-tuning scaling laws and shows downstream BLEU follows a log-law only when pretraining data is sufficiently aligned with the target distribution.
- [Can Mamba Learn How to Learn? A Comparative Study on In-Context Learning Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.04248): Compares Mamba-style SSMs with Transformers on in-context learning, finding strong regression and sparse-parity behavior but weaker non-standard retrieval, then testing a MambaFormer hybrid.
- [LESS: Selecting Influential Data for Targeted Instruction Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.04333): Selects instruction data with Low-rank gradiEnt Similarity Search, using Adam-aware influence features so a 5% subset can target specific downstream capabilities.
- [Democratizing Large Language Models via Personalized Parameter-Efficient Fine-tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.04401): Proposes One PEFT Per User, storing user-specific behavior and preferences in personal PEFT modules combined with retrieval and profiles for LaMP personalization tasks.
- [TransLLaMa: LLM-based Simultaneous Translation System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.04636): Fine-tunes decoder-only LLMs on causally aligned sentence pairs so a generated wait token controls simultaneous-translation segmentation without a separate policy.
- [Code as Reward: Empowering Reinforcement Learning with VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.04764): Uses VLMs to generate executable dense reward functions as code, avoiding frequent VLM reward queries during reinforcement-learning policy training.
- [Direct Language Model Alignment from Online AI Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.04792): Aligns the current policy with online AI feedback by sampling two live responses each iteration and asking an LLM annotator for the preferred one.
- [Leveraging LLMs for Unsupervised Dense Retriever Ranking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.04853): Introduces LARMOR, which samples target-corpus documents and asks LLMs to create pseudo queries, labels, and reference lists for unsupervised dense-retriever selection.
- [Personalized Language Modeling from Personalized Human Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05133): Proposes P-RLHF, jointly learning a lightweight user model and personalized LLM from explicit and implicit preference feedback.
- [Tag-LLM: Repurposing General-Purpose LLMs for Specialized Domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05140): Learns continuous domain and function tags appended to the embedding layer, enabling zero-shot recombination for specialized scientific and biomedical tasks.
- [ApiQ: Finetuning of 2-Bit Quantized Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05147): Initializes LoRA components while quantizing LLM weights, preserving activation precision and reducing shallow-to-deep activation error during 2-bit fine-tuning.
- [Noise Contrastive Alignment of Language Models with Explicit Rewards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05369): Uses NCA and InfoNCA objectives to align language models directly from scalar reward data as well as preference data, with DPO recovered as a special case.
- [Implicit Diffusion: Efficient Optimization through Stochastic Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05468): Optimizes parameters of stochastic diffusion samplers in a single loop that combines sampling and first-order distribution optimization.
- [Generalized Preference Optimization: A Unified Approach to Offline Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05749): Frames DPO, IPO, SLiC, and new offline-alignment variants as convex-function choices inside a generalized preference-optimization loss family.
- [Let Your Graph Do the Talking: Encoding Structured Data for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05862): Introduces GraphToken, a parameter-efficient prompt extension that learns explicit graph encodings and improves node-, edge-, and graph-level GraphQA reasoning.
- [Generative Echo Chamber? Effects of LLM-Powered Search Systems on Diverse Information Seeking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05880): Runs two user studies showing LLM-powered conversational search can increase biased information querying, especially when the assistant reinforces the user's existing view.
- [Large Language Model Meets Graph Neural Network in Knowledge Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05894): Proposes TOGCL for temporal QoS prediction, combining dynamic user-service invocation graphs, target-prompt graph attention, and Transformer temporal encoding.
- [Time Series Diffusion in the Frequency Domain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05933): Reformulates score-based diffusion for time series in the Fourier domain using mirrored Brownian motions, showing frequency-domain denoising better fits several real-world datasets.
- [SPHINX-X: Scaling Data and Parameters for a Family of Multi-modal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05935): Simplifies the SPHINX multimodal architecture by removing redundant vision encoders and using one-stage training, then scales multilingual MLLMs across TinyLlama, InternLM2, LLaMA2, and Mixtral backbones with OCR-intensive and Set-of-Mark data.
- [Delving into Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning in Code Change Learning: An Empirical Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.06247): Compares adapter tuning and LoRA with full fine-tuning on JIT defect prediction and commit-message generation, finding PEFT strong in cross-lingual and low-resource code-change settings.
- [On the Efficacy of Eviction Policy for Key-Value Constrained Generative Language Model Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.06262): Introduces RoCo, a KV-cache eviction policy using temporal attention scores and robustness measures, plus EasyKV for constrained generative inference.
- [InternLM-Math: Open Math Large Language Models Toward Verifiable Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.06332): Continues pretraining InternLM2 into an open math LLM that unifies chain-of-thought, reward modeling, formal reasoning, data augmentation, and code interpreter traces in a seq2seq format for informal and formal math benchmarks.
- [Large Language Models for Captioning and Retrieving Remote Sensing Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.06475): Builds RS-CapRet by pairing remote-sensing CLIP-style image encoders with a frozen large decoder LM through trained linear connectors for captioning and text-image retrieval.
- [On the Out-Of-Distribution Generalization of Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.06599): Evaluates MLLMs under synthetic, real distribution-shift, medical, and molecular imagery settings, identifying mapping deficiency and brittle in-context adaptation as key OOD limits.
- [Feedback Loops With Language Models Drive In-Context Reward Hacking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.06627): Shows that output-refinement and policy-refinement feedback loops can make deployed LLMs optimize implicit objectives in context while increasing harmful side effects, and argues static datasets miss these reward-hacking dynamics.
- [Corruption Robust Offline Reinforcement Learning with Human Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.06734): Studies offline RLHF under adversarially corrupted preference pairs and gives algorithms with guarantees for recovering near-optimal policies from noisy human feedback.
- [Self-Correcting Self-Consuming Loops for Generative Model Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.07087): Stabilizes recursive training on synthetic data by adding correction functions, including simulator-informed correctors, and validates collapse avoidance on human-motion synthesis with synthetic data ratios up to 100%.
- [Lessons Learned from Mining the Hugging Face Repository](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.07323): Distills Hugging Face repository-mining experience into tooling, replication-package, stratified-sampling, and cohort-study guidance for studying model maintenance and emissions.
- [Model Collapse Demystified: The Case of Regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.07712): Analyzes self-training collapse in high-dimensional regression, derives modified scaling laws under spectral and source conditions, and proposes adaptive regularization as a mitigation.
- [Prismatic VLMs: Investigating the Design Space of Visually-Conditioned Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.07865): Builds a standardized VLM evaluation and training framework to compare image preprocessing, visual backbones, and base versus instruction-tuned language backbones across VQA, localization, and hallucination probes.
- [Scaling Laws for Fine-Grained Mixture of Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.07871): Introduces expert granularity as an MoE scaling variable and derives compute-optimal training configurations over token count, model size, and expert size.
- [Active Preference Learning for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08114): Adds active data acquisition for DPO, selecting prompt-completion preference pairs by combining model predictive entropy with implicit preference-model uncertainty.
- [Eliciting Personality Traits in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08341): Measures personality-trait activation in LLM outputs by prompting Llama-2, Falcon, Mistral, Bloom, GPT, OPT, and XLNet variants with interview and Big Five elicitation prompts.
- [Punctuation Restoration Improves Structure Understanding without Supervision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08382): Uses punctuation restoration as an unsupervised language-model training signal. Core idea: force models to recover structural cues that improve NER, open information extraction, chunking, and POS tagging, giving a model-side route to stronger structure understanding without labels.
- [Higher Layers Need More LoRA Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08562): Proposes MoLA, a parameter-efficient MoE-LoRA scheme that allocates different numbers of LoRA experts per Transformer layer and finds higher layers benefit from more experts.
- [Graph Mamba: Towards Learning on Graphs with State Space Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08678): Adapts selective state-space models to graphs through neighborhood tokenization, token ordering, and graph-specific architecture choices to reduce long-range dependency and over-squashing issues.
- [MaxMin-RLHF: Alignment with Diverse Human Preferences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08925): Learns mixtures of preference distributions with expectation maximization and trains policies with a MaxMin objective that represents diverse users more equitably than a single reward model.
- [AgentLens: Visual Analysis for Agent Behaviors in LLM-Based Autonomous Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08995): Provides a visual analytics system that structures raw LLM-agent execution events into hierarchical temporal summaries and causal traces of agent behavior.
- [Attacking Large Language Models with Projected Gradient Descent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09154): Revisits continuous-relaxation PGD for adversarial prompts and controls relaxation error to attack aligned LLMs with far fewer model calls than discrete optimization.
- [(Ir)rationality and cognitive biases in large language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09193): Evaluates seven LLMs on cognitive-psychology reasoning tasks, separating human-like bias from model-specific irrationality and response inconsistency.
- [Personalized Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09269): Compares personalized fine-tuning and zero-shot reasoning for subjective tasks, showing gains on emotion-recognition and hate-speech datasets across LLM architectures.
- [InfoRM: Mitigating Reward Hacking in RLHF via Information-Theoretic Reward Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09345): Adds a variational information-bottleneck reward model and a Cluster Separation Index signal to filter spurious reward features and detect overoptimization.
- [DoRA: Weight-Decomposed Low-Rank Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09353): Decomposes pretrained weights into magnitude and direction, applying LoRA to directional updates so PEFT narrows the gap to full fine-tuning without extra inference overhead.
- [Transformers Can Achieve Length Generalization But Not Robustly](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09371): Tests integer-addition extrapolation and shows Transformer length generalization depends sharply on data format, positional encoding, initialization, and training order.
- [Rolling Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09470): Uses sliding-window denoising that assigns increasing noise to later temporal frames, improving video prediction and chaotic fluid forecasting when dynamics are complex.
- [The Butterfly Effect of Model Editing: Few Edits Can Trigger Large Language Models Collapse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09656): Shows single and sequential knowledge edits can collapse LLM benchmark performance, validates perplexity as a surrogate warning metric, and releases HardEdit cases.
- [How to Train Data-Efficient LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09668): Compares 19 pretraining data samplers and finds Ask-LLM quality scoring and density-based coverage sampling improve quality-resource tradeoffs, sometimes with 90% data rejection.
- [QuRating: Selecting High-Quality Data for Training Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09739): Trains a QuRater model from pairwise LLM judgments over writing style, expertise, facts and trivia, and educational value, then uses ratings on a 260B-token corpus to select data and build curricula for 1.3B-parameter LMs.
- [All in One and One for All: A Simple yet Effective Method towards Cross-domain Graph Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09834): Proposes GCOPE, which uses graph coordinators to unify multiple graph datasets during pretraining so few-shot target graph tasks can benefit from cross-domain transfer instead of negative transfer.
- [Inadequacies of Large Language Model Benchmarks in the Era of Generative Artificial Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09880): Audits 23 LLM benchmarks with a people-process-technology framework and surfaces issues such as bias, reasoning measurement gaps, prompt-engineering sensitivity, evaluator diversity, and the need for dynamic behavioral profiling.
- [Generative Representational Instruction Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09906): Introduces GRIT, training GritLM to follow instructions for both generation and embedding tasks so one model can serve retrieval and generation, including faster long-document RAG without a separate embedder.
- [Enhancing Large Language Models with Pseudo-and Multisource-Knowledge Graphs for Open-ended Question Answering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09911): Combines pseudo-graph generation with atomic knowledge verification so LLMs can use generated and external knowledge graphs for open-ended QA while generalizing across KG sources.
- [Both Matter: Enhancing the Emotional Intelligence of Large Language Models without Compromising the General Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10073): Adds EiBench and MoEI, using modular parameter expansion plus intra- and inter-module modulation to improve emotion perception, cognition, and expression while limiting general-ability forgetting.
- [ControlLM: Crafting Diverse Personalities for Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10151): Steers personality traits at inference time by applying differential activation patterns from contrasting behavioral prompts, enabling training-free persona control and selective amplification of traits such as conscientiousness.
- [Uncertainty Quantification for In-Context Learning of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10189): Separates in-context-learning uncertainty into demonstration-driven aleatoric uncertainty and model-configuration epistemic uncertainty, then estimates both to flag unreliable LLM responses.
- [Rewards-in-Context: Multi-objective Alignment of Foundation Models with Dynamic Preference Adjustment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10207): Conditions a foundation model on multiple reward values in the prompt and uses supervised fine-tuning so user preference weights can be adjusted dynamically at inference time.
- [Hierarchical State Space Models for Continuous Sequence-to-Sequence Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10211): Stacks structured state-space models into HiSS for continuous sensor-to-signal prediction, targeting long raw sequences, nonlinear drift, and small labeled datasets.
- [DELL: Generating Reactions and Explanations for LLM-Based Misinformation Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10426): Uses LLM-generated news reactions, proxy-task explanations, and expert merging to enrich misinformation detectors with simulated user perspectives and task-specific evidence.
- [Smaller Language Models are capable of selecting Instruction-Tuning Training Data for Larger Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10430): Selects instruction-tuning samples by learning percentage, showing that data hardness transfers across model sizes and that a 350M model can curate hard samples for a 13B model.
- [QDyLoRA: Quantized Dynamic Low-Rank Adaptation for Efficient Large Language Model Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10462): Extends QLoRA with dynamic rank training, letting one quantized fine-tuning run produce multiple LoRA ranks and fit Falcon-40B rank search on a single 32GB V100.
- [Direct Preference Optimization with an Offset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10571): Generalizes DPO with an offset margin so preference pairs with stronger or weaker human preference gaps impose different likelihood-separation requirements during fine-tuning.
- [Can LLMs Speak For Diverse People? Tuning LLMs via Debate to Generate Controllable Controversial Statements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10614): Introduces DEBATUNE, which uses multi-round debates between LLMs with opposing stances to create training data for controllable controversial statements over 710 topics.
- [Network formation and dynamics among multi-LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10659): Builds a framework for studying networks of interacting LLM agents, comparing their preferential attachment, triadic closure, homophily, community structure, and small-world behavior against human network decisions.
- [Humans or LLMs as the Judge? A Study on Judgement Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10669): Measures misinformation oversight, gender, authority, and beauty biases in human and LLM judges without ground-truth labels, then shows the same biases can be exploited to attack LLM-as-judge evaluations.
- [Squat: Quant Small Language Models on the Edge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10787): Designs a quantization-aware training framework for small language models that uses edge-deployable quantization compatible with mobile SIMD hardware rather than GPU-oriented fine-grained schemes.
- [Generative Cross-Modal Retrieval: Memorizing Images in Multimodal Language Models for Retrieval and Beyond](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10805): Trains an MLLM to memorize images as unique identifier strings and retrieve them generatively from text queries through separate memorization and retrieval stages.
- [Quantifying the Persona Effect in LLM Simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10811): Quantifies how demographic, social, and behavioral persona variables affect LLM simulation of subjective annotations, finding modest gains that scale with persona-label correlation.
- [Measuring and Controlling Instruction (In)Stability in Language Model Dialogs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10962): Benchmarks instruction drift through self-chats between instructed chatbots and proposes split-softmax to reduce attention-decay-driven loss of system-prompt behavior.
- [Persona-DB: Efficient Large Language Model Personalization for Response Prediction with Collaborative Data Refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11060): Builds a hierarchical retrieval database for personalization, refining user-history representations so LLMs can predict responses without per-user fine-tuning.
- [Revisiting Word Embeddings in the LLM Era](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11094): Compares LLM-derived word embeddings with Word2Vec, GloVe, SBERT, and USE, showing tighter semantic clustering and different analogy behavior rather than assuming gains come only from scale.
- [Language Models as Science Tutors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11111): Introduces TutorEval for long STEM-textbook QA and TutorChat with 80,000 synthetic textbook dialogues, targeting LM tutoring on long scientific documents rather than short exam questions.
- [Speculative Streaming: Fast LLM Inference without Auxiliary Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11131): Fine-tunes a single target model for future n-gram prediction so it can draft and verify its own continuations, avoiding a separate speculative draft model.
- [TuneTables: Context Optimization for Scalable Prior-Data Fitted Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11137): Compresses large tabular datasets into learned contexts for TabPFN-style prior-data fitted networks, improving large-dataset tabular prediction while tuning fewer than 5% of parameters.
- [Boosting of Thoughts: Trial-and-Error Problem Solving with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11140): Iteratively explores trees of thoughts, asks the model for error analysis, and folds those trial-and-error traces into prompts for solving complex math problems.
- [CoLLaVO: Crayon Large Language and Vision mOdel](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11248): Improves object-level VLM understanding with Crayon Prompt panoptic color maps and Dual QLoRA, preserving basic image grounding during visual instruction tuning.
- [Aligning Large Language Models by On-Policy Self-Judgment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11253): Trains one model to act as both policy and preference judge through Judge-augmented SFT, enabling on-policy alignment without a separate reward model.
- [MoRAL: MoE Augmented LoRA for LLMs' Lifelong Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11260): Combines mixture-of-experts routing with LoRA adapters for lifelong LLM updates from question-answer pairs and evaluates retention on the 5L-bench open- and closed-book settings.
- [Aligning Modalities in Vision Large Language Models via Preference Fine-tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11411): Creates POVID preference data by injecting plausible GPT-4V hallucinations and distorting images, then applies DPO to reduce VLLM hallucination from modality misalignment.
- [When Do LLMs Need Retrieval Augmentation? Mitigating LLMs' Overconfidence Helps Retrieval Augmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11457): Measures LLM knowledge-boundary awareness, reduces overconfidence, and triggers retrieval only when uncertainty indicates external evidence is needed.
- [Large Language Model-driven Meta-structure Discovery in Heterogeneous Information Network](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11518): Introduces ReStruct, an evolutionary HIN meta-structure search method where LLM reasoning scores semantic feasibility and generates explanations for discovered structures.
- [Multi-Task Inference: Can Large Language Models Follow Multiple Instructions at Once?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11597): Defines MTI Bench with 5,000 examples across 25 tasks and tests whether LLMs can complete two or three subtasks in one inference call.
- [Learning From Failure: Integrating Negative Examples when Fine-tuning Large Language Models as Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11651): Reuses failed agent trajectories by labeling them with success or failure prefixes or suffixes, improving fine-tuning for math reasoning, multi-hop QA, and strategic QA.
- [Invertible Fourier Neural Operators for Tackling Both Forward and Inverse Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11722): Builds iFNO with invertible Fourier blocks and a VAE component so one neural operator can share parameters across forward prediction and inverse inference tasks.
- [Towards Theoretical Understandings of Self-Consuming Generative Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11778): Derives total-variation bounds for recursive real-synthetic training loops and identifies a phase transition as synthetic data amounts increase.
- [Enhancing Empathetic Response Generation by Augmenting LLMs with Small-scale Empathetic Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11801): Uses small empathetic models as plugins for emotion prediction and emotion-cause perception, helping LLMs generate more fine-grained empathetic responses.
- [Microstructures and Accuracy of Graph Recall by Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11821): Tests LLM graph recall from text and finds biased microstructures such as excess triangles and alternating two-paths, with recall accuracy depending on narrative domain style.
- [Revisiting Knowledge Distillation for Autoregressive Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11890): Shows larger teacher LMs can hurt students when token teaching modes are ignored and proposes ATKD to adapt distillation behavior per token.
- [DB-LLM: Accurate Dual-Binarization for Efficient LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11960): Introduces dual-binarization for ultra-low-bit LLM quantization, splitting two-bit weights into binary sets to combine bitwise efficiency with representation accuracy.
- [Towards Cross-Tokenizer Distillation: the Universal Logit Distillation Loss for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12030): Uses an optimal-transport Universal Logit Distillation loss so teacher and student LLMs with different tokenizers can still distill from logits.
- [Self-AMPLIFY: Improving Small Language Models with Self Post Hoc Explanations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12038): Applies post hoc explanation methods to small autoregressive LMs to generate their own rationales, then uses those rationales for automated in-context performance gains.
- [Model Tailor: Mitigating Catastrophic Forgetting in Multi-modal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12048): Identifies a small salience- and sensitivity-based model patch and replaces at most 10% of fine-tuned parameters to preserve original MLLM tasks while adapting to new ones.
- [Small Models, Big Insights: Leveraging Slim Proxy Models To Decide When and What to Retrieve for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12052): Uses a slim proxy model to infer what knowledge an LLM lacks, retrieving only missing information instead of asking the full LLM to self-diagnose every query.
- [Pan-Mamba: Effective pan-sharpening with State Space Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12192): Adapts Mamba to pan-sharpening with channel-swapping and cross-modal Mamba modules that fuse low-resolution multispectral and high-resolution panchromatic images.
- [AnyGPT: Unified Multimodal LLM with Discrete Sequence Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12226): Represents speech, text, images, and music as discrete sequences and trains any-to-any multimodal instruction data without changing the base LLM architecture.
- [Plato: Plan to Efficiently Decode for Large Language Model Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12280): Builds semantic-aware parallel decoding by having an LLM plan dependency graphs, decode independent nodes concurrently, and reuse global context and KV caches.
- [LoRA+: Efficient Low Rank Adaptation of Large Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12354): Shows standard LoRA under-trains wide models when adapter matrices share one learning rate and fixes it with separate learning rates for A and B.
- [Turn Waste into Worth: Rectifying Top-k Router of MoE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12399): Rectifies MoE top-k routing with intra-GPU handling for dropped tokens and fill-in replacement for padding tokens, reducing wasted expert computation.
- [IMBUE: Improving Interpersonal Effectiveness through Simulation and Just-in-time Feedback with Human-Language Model Interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12556): Uses LMs to simulate interpersonal-effectiveness practice under the DBT DEAR MAN framework and provide just-in-time feedback closer to expert coaching.
- [Confidence Matters: Revisiting Intrinsic Self-Correction Capabilities of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12563): Identifies model confidence as a latent factor in self-correction and proposes If-or-Else prompting to decide when an LLM should revise its own answer.
- [HyperMoE: Towards Better Mixture of Experts via Transferring Among Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12656): Adds hypernetwork-generated modules that transfer information from unselected experts, improving MoE performance while preserving sparse expert selection.
- [Advancing Large Language Models to Capture Varied Speaking Styles and Respond Properly in Spoken Conversations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12786): Builds StyleTalk and trains LLMs to condition responses on paralinguistic and prosodic speaking style when identical words are spoken differently.
- [Identifying Factual Inconsistency in Summaries: Towards Effective Utilization of Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12821): Injects a task-specific taxonomy of factual inconsistency types into zero-shot and supervised LLM inference for summary faithfulness detection.
- [MoELoRA: Contrastive Learning Guided Mixture of Experts on Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12851): Treats LoRA adapters as mixture-of-experts modules and uses contrastive learning to reduce random routing during parameter-efficient fine-tuning.
- [Stable Knowledge Editing in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.13048): Recasts knowledge editing as knowledge augmentation, using automated semantic paraphrase and related augmentation strategies to improve edit stability without assuming isolated knowledge locations.
- [Towards an empirical understanding of MoE design choices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.13089): Empirically compares MoE routing choices, showing learned and frozen random routers can perform similarly and that token- and sequence-level routing specialize differently.
- [Soft Self-Consistency Improves Language Model Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.13212): Replaces majority-vote self-consistency with likelihood-based soft scoring so sequential agents can choose among sparse, diverse action candidates using fewer samples.
- [Smaug: Fixing Failure Modes of Preference Optimisation with DPO-Positive](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.13228): Introduces DPO-Positive to prevent standard DPO from lowering preferred-response likelihood when only the relative preferred-versus-dispreferred gap increases.
- [ProSparse: Introducing and Enhancing Intrinsic Activation Sparsity within Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.13516): Pushes LLMs toward higher activation sparsity after activation-function substitution, targeting inference acceleration while maintaining comparable model performance.
- [User-LLM: Efficient LLM Contextualization with User Embeddings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.13598): Encodes user timelines with a self-supervised user encoder and injects the resulting user embeddings into LLMs through cross-attention for personalized response prediction.
- [UniGraph: Learning a Unified Cross-Domain Foundation Model for Text-Attributed Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.13630): Uses text-attributed graphs as a unifying representation to train one graph foundation model that transfers across unseen graphs, tasks, feature spaces, and label spaces.
- [Using Large Language Models for Natural Language Processing Tasks in Requirements Engineering: A Systematic Guideline](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.13823): Provides a requirements-engineering guideline for choosing, adapting, and fine-tuning LLM architectures for NLP4RE tasks rather than a new model architecture.
- [Analysing The Impact of Sequence Composition on Language Model Pre-Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.13991): Shows document concatenation can introduce distracting cross-document context during causal masking and proposes intra-document masking plus BM25Chunk sequence construction.
- [Beyond A*: Better Planning with Transformers via Search Dynamics Bootstrapping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14083): Trains Searchformer to imitate A* search dynamics as token sequences, solving unseen Sokoban puzzles with fewer search steps than the planner used for supervision.
- [Comparing Graph Transformers via Positional Encodings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14202): Proves equivalences between absolute and relative graph positional encodings and compares their distinguishing power for graph Transformer design.
- [TinyLLaVA: A Framework of Small-scale Large Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14289): Provides a small-LMM design framework that ablates vision encoders, connectors, language backbones, data, and recipes, showing that data quality and training recipe can let TinyLLaVA-3.1B match or exceed several 7B LMM baselines.
- [A Language Model's Guide Through Latent Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14433): Extends activation-based concept guidance beyond truthfulness to concepts such as appropriateness, humor, creativity, and quality, with a metric that balances elicitation and fluency degradation.
- ["My Answer is C": First-Token Probabilities Do Not Match Text Answers in Instruction-Tuned Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14499): Shows first-token multiple-choice scoring can mismatch generated text answers, refusals, choice distributions, and prompt-perturbation behavior by more than 60% in some settings.
- [Balanced Data Sampling for Language Model Training with Clustering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14526): Introduces ClusterClip sampling, balancing common and rare text clusters during LLM training while clipping repeated clusters to limit overfitting.
- [OmniPred: Language Models as Universal Regressors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14547): Trains language models as universal regressors over textualized parameter-value pairs from Google Vizier, outperforming task-specific regression models across black-box optimization data.
- [Prompting a Pretrained Transformer Can Be a Universal Approximator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14753): Proves prompting and prefix-tuning can make pretrained Transformers approximate sequence-to-sequence functions, including single-head attention and prefix-length bounds.
- [Not All Experts are Equal: Efficient Expert Pruning and Skipping for Mixture-of-Experts Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14800): Adds post-training expert pruning and expert skipping for MoE LLMs, reducing model size and inference cost while preserving task performance.
- [Fine-Tuning Enhances Existing Mechanisms: A Case Study on Entity Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14811): Uses patching and circuit analysis to show math fine-tuning improves an existing entity-tracking mechanism rather than creating a fundamentally new one.
- [Palo: A Polyglot Large Multimodal Model for 5B People](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14818): Trains multilingual VLMs across 10 major languages using semi-automatically translated multimodal instruction data and proposes a multilingual multimodal benchmark.
- [Double-I Watermark: Protecting Model Copyright for LLM Fine-tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14883): Injects fine-tuning watermarks through two backdoor paradigms with triggers in instructions and inputs, enabling verification of customized LLM ownership.
- [Divide-or-Conquer? Which Part Should You Distill Your LLM?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15000): Separates reasoning into decomposition and solving phases, showing decomposition is easier to distill into smaller models and can lower inference cost when paired with larger solvers.
- [Unintended Impacts of LLM Alignment on Global Representation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15018): Audits RLHF and DPO effects on English dialects, multilingual ability, and global opinions, finding alignment-induced disparities despite benchmark gains.
- [Studying LLM Performance on Closed- and Open-source Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15100): Compares LLM coding performance on Microsoft proprietary code versus open-source code, tracing C++ degradation to identifier differences and testing in-context mitigation.
- [Second-Order Fine-Tuning without Pain for LLMs: A Hessian Informed Zeroth-Order Optimizer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15173): Introduces HiZOO, a diagonal-Hessian-informed zeroth-order optimizer that adds one forward pass per step to improve convergence and memory-efficient LLM fine-tuning.
- [Advancing Parameter Efficiency in Fine-tuning via Representation Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15179): Introduces RED, a PEFT method that edits intermediate representations with scaling and bias operations instead of choosing LoRA ranks, adapter sizes, or prompt lengths.
- [GraphEdit: Large Language Models for Graph Structure Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15183): Uses LLM-derived node relationship signals to generate graph structures for GSL, reducing reliance on noisy or sparse explicit graph supervision.
- [Genie: Generative Interactive Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15391): Trains a generative world model from internet videos so users can interact with novel environments from image prompts and learned latent dynamics.
- [Repetition Improves Language Model Embeddings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15449): Tests repetition as a simple embedding-time intervention, showing that repeating input text can sharpen language-model sentence embeddings without changing model weights.
- [Co-Supervised Learning: Improving Weak-to-Strong Generalization with Hierarchical Mixture of Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15505): Uses hierarchical mixture-of-experts co-supervision to improve weak-to-strong generalization, letting weaker and stronger learners share supervision across expert levels.
- [MegaScale: Scaling Large Language Model Training to More Than 10, 000 GPUs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15627): Documents a full-stack LLM training system for more than 10,000 GPUs, co-designing model blocks, optimizer choices, overlap scheduling, kernels, data loading, and network tuning.
- [MambaIR: A Simple Baseline for Image Restoration with State-Space Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15648): Adapts Mamba for image restoration by adding local enhancement and channel attention, preserving linear-complexity long-range modeling while reducing pixel forgetting and channel redundancy.
- [HD-Eval: Aligning Large Language Model Evaluators Through Hierarchical Criteria Decomposition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15754): Aligns LLM evaluators by decomposing evaluation criteria hierarchically, turning broad judgment rubrics into finer criteria for more structured scoring.
- [PRP: Propagating Universal Perturbations to Attack Large Language Model Guard-Rails](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15911): Attacks LLM guard models with a two-step universal prefix, first optimizing the prefix against the moderator and then propagating it into the primary model response across white-box and no-access threat models.
- [How Large Language Models Encode Context Knowledge? A Layer-Wise Probing Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16061): Builds ChatGPT-generated probing datasets and uses V-usable information to measure how LLM layers encode context-supported factual knowledge.
- [PeriodicLoRA: Breaking the Low-Rank Bottleneck in LoRA Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16141): Addresses low-rank bottlenecks in LoRA optimization.
- [COMAE: COMprehensive Attribute Exploration for Zero-shot Hashing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16424): Learns zero-shot hash codes through point-wise, pair-wise, and class-wise attribute consistency constraints that transfer local attribute relationships from seen to unseen classes.
- [Aligning Large Language Models to a Domain-specific Graph Database for NL2GQL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16567): Defines a synthetic data and alignment pipeline for adapting LLMs to domain-specific natural-language-to-graph-query generation when paired NL-GQL data are scarce.
