- [SelectIT: Selective Instruction Tuning for LLMs via Uncertainty-Aware Self-Reflection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16705): Selects high-quality instruction-tuning data using the target LLM's own uncertainty and self-reflection signals, avoiding extra scorer models or external data.
- [Political Compass or Spinning Arrow? Towards More Meaningful Evaluations for Values and Opinions in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16786): Critiques political-compass style value tests for LLMs and proposes more meaningful evaluations of model opinions and value stability across prompts.
- [Nemotron-4 15B Technical Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16819): Reports Nemotron-4 15B, covering the 15B model training recipe, alignment stages, and benchmark results as an open technical reference.
- [Groundhog Grounding Large Language Models to Holistic Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16846): Grounds an MLLM to segmentation by converting masked feature-extractor outputs into visual entity tokens and training on the M3G2 multi-modal multi-grained grounding instruction dataset.
- [When Scaling Meets LLM Finetuning: The Effect of Data, Model and Finetuning Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.17193): Studies how fine-tuning outcomes scale with data size, base-model scale, and tuning method, separating gains from dataset growth, model capacity, and adaptation choice.
- [RIME: Robust Preference-based Reinforcement Learning with Noisy Preferences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.17257): Filters noisy preference labels with a sample-selection discriminator and warm-started reward model so preference-based RL remains stable without expert-clean feedback.
- [Mini-Ensemble Low-Rank Adapters for Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.17263): Proposes MELoRA, splitting low-rank adapters into a mini-ensemble that keeps high-rank expressivity while reducing trainable parameters for PEFT.
- [Data-Efficient Learning via Clustering-Based Sensitivity Sampling: Foundation Models and Beyond](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.17327): Selects small representative training subsets with k-means clustering and sensitivity sampling, with loss-approximation guarantees under embedding-space smoothness assumptions.
- [Investigating Continual Pretraining in Large Language Models: Insights and Implications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.17400): Studies continual domain-adaptive pretraining for LLMs with a benchmark that measures adaptation to new domains while retaining previous knowledge.
- [RAVEL: Evaluating Interpretability Methods on Disentangling Language Model Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.17700): Introduces RAVEL for controlled attribute-value entanglement tests and MDAS to find distributed LLM representations satisfying multiple causal criteria.
- [Massive Activations in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.17762): Identifies rare, extremely large LLM activations that behave like indispensable bias terms, concentrate attention, and must be preserved during quantization.
- [Prediction-Powered Ranking of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.17826): Builds rank-sets from a small pool of human pairwise comparisons and a larger pool of model judgments, quantifying when LLM-produced leaderboard rankings can miss the true Chatbot Arena human-preference ordering.
- [Automated Statistical Model Discovery with Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.17879): Casts statistical model discovery as Box's Loop, where an LM proposes probabilistic-program models and critiques them under domain constraints, matching or extending expert models without a handcrafted model language.
- [Gradient-Free Adaptive Global Pruning for Pre-trained Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.17946): Prunes pretrained language models with a gradient-free adaptive global criterion, targeting compression without backpropagation-based saliency estimation.
- [Hire a Linguist!: Learning Endangered Languages with In-Context Linguistic Descriptions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.18025): Proposes LINGOLLM, a training-free prompting method that supplies dictionaries, grammars, and morphological analyses so LLMs can process unseen endangered languages.
- [MEGAnno+: A Human-LLM Collaborative Annotation System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.18050): Provides an LLM-assisted annotation system with agent management, robust automatic labeling, and human verification for sociocultural or domain-specific NLP labels.
- [Cutting Off the Head Ends the Conflict: A Mechanism for Interpreting and Mitigating Knowledge Conflicts in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.18154): Locates late-layer memory heads and context heads with opposing effects, then mitigates retrieval-memory conflicts through targeted attention-head interventions.
- [LLM Task Interference: An Initial Study on the Impact of Task-Switch in Conversational History](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.18216): Measures how task switches in conversation history can degrade LLM performance, isolating interference caused by conditioning on prior dialogue context.
- [Learning or Self-aligning? Rethinking Instruction Fine-tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.18243): Uses knowledge interventions to separate instruction fine-tuning effects, finding that IFT often changes behavioral alignment more than it adds reliable world knowledge.
- [Large Language Models As Evolution Strategies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.18381): Tests whether LLMs can implement zero-shot black-box evolutionary optimization through prompts that update search distributions from prior evaluation trajectories.
- [RNNs are not Transformers (Yet): The Key Bottleneck on In-context Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.18510): Theoretically shows that chain-of-thought improves RNNs but does not close their in-context retrieval gap with Transformers on algorithmic tasks.
- [Keeping LLMs Aligned After Fine-tuning: The Crucial Role of Prompt Templates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.18540): Shows that preserving chat-style prompt templates during benign fine-tuning helps maintain safety alignment across Llama 2-Chat, Mistral Instruct, and GPT-3.5.
- [Arithmetic Control of LLMs for Diverse User Preferences: Directional Preference Alignment with Multi-Objective Rewards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.18571): Introduces DPA, representing user preferences as directions in a multi-objective reward space so LLM behavior can be arithmetically steered after alignment.
- [Grounding Language Models for Visual Entity Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.18695): Introduces AutoVER, combining an autoregressive MLLM with retrieval-augmented constrained generation and hard-negative contrastive training for visual entity recognition.
- [Controllable Preference Optimization: Toward Controllable Multi-Objective Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.19085): Trains controllable reward and policy models so preference optimization can trade off multiple objectives at inference instead of collapsing to one scalar preference.
- [Teaching Large Language Models an Unseen Language on the Fly](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.19167): Teaches unseen languages at inference by prompting LLMs with grammar sketches, dictionaries, and morphology rather than adding pretraining corpora.
- [Wisdom of the silicon crowd: LLM ensemble prediction capabilities rival human crowd accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.19379): Aggregates forecasts from twelve LLMs on 31 binary questions and compares them with 925 human tournament forecasters, showing crowd-style model averaging can approach human aggregate accuracy.
- [Resonance RoPE: Improving Context Length Generalization of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00071): Modifies rotary position embeddings with a resonance-aware design to improve context-length generalization without full long-context retraining.
- [Gender Bias in Large Language Models across Multiple Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00277): Measures gender bias across multilingual LLM outputs, exposing how bias varies by language rather than assuming English-only behavior transfers.
- [Learning and Leveraging World Models in Visual Representation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00504): Trains visual representations to predict latent world dynamics, using learned world models to improve zero-shot generalization across visual tasks.
- [VisionLLaMA: A Unified LLaMA Interface for Vision Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00522): Builds a LLaMA-style vision backbone that unifies image task interfaces through sequential visual tokens rather than task-specific heads.
- [AtP*: An efficient and scalable method for localizing LLM behaviour to components](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00745): Localizes LLM behaviors to internal components with AtP*, giving a scalable alternative to exhaustive activation patching for mechanistic analysis.
- [UrbanGPT: Spatio-Temporal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00813): Builds UrbanGPT for spatio-temporal prediction by adapting language-model interfaces to urban time-series and spatial signals.
- [DenseMamba: State Space Models with Dense Hidden Connection for Efficient Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00818): Adds DenseSSM connections that feed shallow hidden states into deeper SSM layers, improving RetNet and Mamba-style models while preserving parallel training and efficient inference.
- [CLLMs: Consistency Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00835): Fine-tunes target LLMs to predict Jacobi-decoding fixed points from arbitrary states, making parallel token decoding converge faster.
- [OpenGraph: Towards Open Graph Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.01121): Proposes an open graph foundation model that uses LLM-enhanced graph augmentation to generalize graph learning across unseen graph structures and tasks.
- [The Hidden Attention of Mamba Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.01590): Reinterprets selective state-space layers as attention-driven models, enabling empirical and theoretical comparison between Mamba internals and Transformer self-attention.
- [SynCode: LLM Generation with Grammar Augmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.01632): Constrains LLM decoding with context-free grammars through SynCode, producing syntactically valid JSON, YAML, or code without retraining.
- [How Multimodal Integration Boost the Performance of LLM for Optimization: Case Study on Capacitated Vehicle Routing Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.01757): Improves LLM-based optimization by adding visual encodings of vehicle-routing instances so the model can reason over decision-variable relationships not obvious in text prompts.
- [Not all Layers of LLMs are Necessary during Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.02181): Shows some Transformer layers can be skipped at inference time, reducing LLM compute while preserving much of the original task performance.
- [TPLLM: A Traffic Prediction Framework Based on Pretrained Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.02221): Adapts pretrained LLM representations to low-data traffic forecasting, targeting generalization when historical spatiotemporal traffic records are sparse.
- [Vision-RWKV: Efficient and Scalable Visual Perception with RWKV-Like Architectures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.02308): Adapts RWKV-style sequence mixing to vision with sparse-input handling and global processing, targeting scalable high-resolution perception.
- [Taming Throughput-Latency Tradeoff in LLM Inference with Sarathi-Serve](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.02310): Schedules LLM prefill and decode work in Sarathi-Serve to reduce interference between latency-sensitive and throughput-oriented inference phases.
- [Contrastive Region Guidance: Improving Grounding in Vision-Language Models without Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.02325): Provides training-free visual grounding by contrasting VLM responses with and without region guidance, letting open-source VLMs use bounding-box prompts.
- [RegionGPT: Towards Region Understanding Vision Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.02330): Improves region-level VLM understanding with spatially aware visual encoder modifications and detailed region-caption instruction data.
- [Finetuned Multimodal Language Models Are High-Quality Image-Text Data Filters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.02677): Uses fine-tuned multimodal language models as image-text data filters, replacing simpler CLIP-style scores with instruction-aware filtering judgments.
- [MeanCache: User-Centric Semantic Caching for LLM Web Services](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.02694): Caches LLM web-service responses by semantic query similarity and conversational context, reducing repeated-query inference cost while controlling false hits.
- [Causal Prompting: Debiasing Large Language Model Prompting based on Front-Door Adjustment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.02738): Models prompting biases with a structural causal graph and applies front-door adjustment to debias LLM prompts at inference time.
- [PromptKD: Unsupervised Prompt Distillation for Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.02781): Distills a larger vision-language teacher into a lightweight target model by learning domain prompts from unlabeled images.
- [Evolution Transformer: In-Context Evolutionary Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.02985): Introduces a causal Transformer that learns evolutionary-strategy updates from optimization trajectories and emits improved search-distribution steps in context.
- [Data Augmentation using LLMs: Data Perspectives, Learning Paradigms and Challenges](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.02990): Surveys LLM-based data augmentation from data and learning perspectives, covering generated training examples, downstream reuse of LLM-produced data, controllability, and multimodal augmentation gaps.
- [Feast Your Eyes: Mixture-of-Resolution Adaptation for Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03003): Adds MRA, a dual-pathway high-resolution and low-resolution visual adaptation module that improves fine-grained recognition in MLLMs.
- [Quantum many-body physics calculations with large language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03154): Uses multi-step prompt templates to make GPT-4 derive Hartree-Fock Hamiltonians from theoretical-physics papers, with intermediate correction and automatic step scoring.
- [Mixture-of-LoRAs: An Efficient Multitask Tuning Method for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03432): Combines multiple LoRA adapters as a mixture for multitask tuning, routing task-specific updates without full model fine-tuning.
- [GaLore: Memory-Efficient LLM Training by Gradient Low-Rank Projection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03507): Projects gradients into low-rank subspaces during LLM training, reducing optimizer memory while preserving full-rank weight updates.
- [Multimodal Large Language Models to Support Real-World Fact-Checking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03627): Evaluates GPT-4V and open MLLMs on evidence-free multimodal fact-checking prompts that elicit predictions, explanations, and confidence for misleading image-text claims.
- [ShortGPT: Layers in Large Language Models are More Redundant Than You Expect](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03853): Introduces ShortGPT-style layer removal, identifying redundant LLM layers and shortening the network for faster inference.
- [On the Origins of Linear Representations in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03867): Analyzes why linear representations emerge in LLMs, connecting internal geometry with how models encode semantic features.
- [Learning to Decode Collaboratively with Multiple Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03870): Learns a latent token-level controller that decides when a base LLM should generate and when assistant LMs should contribute during collaborative decoding.
- [SaulLM-7B: A pioneering Large Language Model for Law](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03883): Trains a 7B legal-domain LLM from Mistral on more than 30B English legal tokens and adds legal instruction tuning for document understanding and generation.
- [Stop Regressing: Training Value Functions via Classification for Scalable Deep RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03950): Replaces value-function regression with a classification objective, aiming to make value-based deep RL scale more reliably to high-capacity networks.
- [Effectiveness assessment of recent large vision-language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.04306): Evaluates MiniGPT-v2, LLaVA-1.5, Shikra, and GPT-4V across specialized detection tasks and general multimodal reasoning, attributing weak LVLM performance to limited specialized cognition, hallucination, text-image interference, and robustness gaps.
- [Yi: Open Foundation Models by 01.AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.04652): Reports the Yi language and multimodal model family, spanning 6B and 34B base models, chat variants, 200K-context models, depth-upscaled models, and VLMs.
- [Telecom Language Models: Must They Be Large?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.04666): Studies whether compact language models can serve telecommunications tasks, comparing small LMs with larger LLMs under resource-constrained deployment assumptions.
- [Graph-Oriented Instruction Tuning of Large Language Models for Generic Graph Mining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.04780): Introduces MuseGraph, integrating GNN representations with LLM instruction tuning so one model can handle multiple graph mining tasks and datasets.
- [VLM-PL: Advanced Pseudo Labeling approach for Class Incremental Object Detection via Vision-Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.05346): Uses a VLM to verify pseudo ground-truth labels for class-incremental object detection, reducing forgetting across multi-scenario incremental learning.
- [Is Cosine-Similarity of Embeddings Really About Similarity?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.05440): Analyzes cosine similarity in regularized linear embedding models, showing when normalization helps or hurts semantic-similarity judgments.
- [Unfamiliar Finetuning Examples Control How Language Models Hallucinate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.05612): Finds that unfamiliar fine-tuning examples shape how LLMs answer unfamiliar queries, then uses that effect to train more reliable reward models for RL factuality tuning.
- [ItD: Large Language Models Can Teach Themselves Induction through Deduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.05789): Proposes Induction through Deduction, where LLMs generate and test hypotheses through deductive examples to strengthen their intrinsic induction ability.
- [Algorithmic progress in language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.05812): Fits augmented scaling laws to more than 200 Wikitext and Penn Treebank evaluations from 2012 to 2023, estimating that language-model pretraining algorithms halve compute-to-threshold roughly every eight months.
- [Mipha: A Comprehensive Overhaul of Multimodal Assistant with Small Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.06199): Designs an efficient multimodal assistant around small language models by jointly revising visual representation, language backbone, and optimization choices.
- [Fine-tuning of diffusion models via stochastic control: entropy regularization and beyond](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.06279): Formalizes diffusion-model fine-tuning as entropy-regularized stochastic control and extends it to general f-divergence regularizers for text-to-image models.
- [What Makes Quantization for Large Language Model Hard? An Empirical Study from the Lens of Perturbation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.06408): Interprets LLM quantization as weight and activation perturbations, then studies which perturbation patterns most damage model performance.
- [Unsupervised Real-Time Hallucination Detection based on the Internal States of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.06448): Introduces MIND, an unsupervised detector that uses LLM internal states during generation, and HELM, a benchmark containing model outputs plus inference-time states for hallucination detection.
- [RecAI: Leveraging Large Language Models for Next-Generation Recommender Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.06465): Releases a recommender toolkit with an LLM recommender agent, recommendation-oriented language models, knowledge plugins, explanation modules, and evaluators for conversational and controllable recommendation.
- [Can LLMs Separate Instructions From Data? And What Do We Even Mean By That?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.06833): Formalizes instruction-data separation, introduces the SEP dataset, and shows prompt engineering and fine-tuning often fail to improve separation without reducing utility.
- [Impact of Noisy Supervision in Foundation Model Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.06869): Studies label noise in large-scale supervised and image-text pretraining and proposes mitigation strategies to reduce downstream degradation.
- [Taming Pre-trained LLMs for Generalised Time Series Forecasting via Cross-modal Knowledge Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.07300): Distills cross-modal knowledge from text into temporal tokens to adapt pre-trained LLMs for generalized multivariate time-series forecasting.
- [Lumen: Unleashing Versatile Vision-Centric Capabilities of Large Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.07304): Reworks LMM visual-task adaptation so diverse perception outputs are learned with vision-centric representations rather than forced into language-only formats.
- [Harder Tasks Need More Experts: Dynamic Routing in MoE Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.07652): Dynamically varies the number of activated MoE experts according to input difficulty, replacing fixed top-k routing with confidence-based expert selection.
- [Branch-Train-MiX: Mixing Expert LLMs into a Mixture-of-Experts LLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.07816): Branches a seed LLM into asynchronously trained domain experts, then merges feedforward layers into an MoE and fine-tunes token-level routing.
- [MoPE-CLIP: Structured Pruning for Efficient Vision-Language Models with Module-Wise Pruning Error Metric](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.07839): Compresses CLIP with a module-wise pruning-error metric that avoids costly mask search and preserves vision-language performance on constrained hardware.
- [Beyond Text: Frozen Large Language Models in Visual Signal Comprehension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.07874): Converts images into LLM-vocabulary token sequences through a vision-to-language tokenizer, enabling frozen LLMs to process visual signals without multimodal fine-tuning.
- [On the Societal Impact of Open Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.07918): Maps societal impacts of open foundation models, focusing on how open weights change access, misuse, governance, and accountability tradeoffs.
- [Mechanics of Next Token Prediction with Self-Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.08081): Derives mechanics of next-token prediction under self-attention, using simplified analysis to explain how attention supports sequence prediction.
- [BAGEL: Bootstrapping Agents by Guiding Exploration with Language](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.08140): Converts random or synthetic trajectories into language-described demonstrations through iterative labeler-agent round trips, improving retrieved in-context LM-agent performance on ToolQA and MiniWob++.
- [Can Large Language Models Identify Authorship?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.08213): Evaluates whether LLMs can infer authorship, probing stylometric signals and the limits of model-based attribution.
- [Scattered Mixture-of-Experts Implementation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.08245): Implements ScatterMoE with padding-free GPU kernels and ParallelLinear layers to improve sparse MoE training and inference throughput while lowering memory use.
- [Mastering Text, Code and Math Simultaneously via Fusing Highly Specialized Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.08281): Fuses specialized text, code, and math language models so one model can inherit capabilities without training every skill from scratch.
- [CleanAgent: Automating Data Standardization with LLM-based Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.08291): Combines Dataprep.Clean's declarative column-standardization APIs with LLM agents so users can specify cleaning requirements once and obtain generated standardization code.
- [Gemma: Open Models Based on Gemini Research and Technology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.08295): Reports the Gemma open model family and its Gemini-derived training stack, providing a general foundation-model reference point.
- [Language models scale reliably with over-training and on downstream tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.08540): Extends language-model scaling analysis to over-trained regimes and downstream tasks, showing loss-based scaling can still predict practical task behavior.
- [Human Alignment of Large Language Models through Online Preference Optimisation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.08635): Derives IPO-MD from the link between IPO and Nash Mirror Descent, enabling online preference optimization for LLM alignment.
- [SOTOPIA-π: Interactive Learning of Socially Intelligent Language Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.08715): Combines behavior cloning with self-reinforcement on filtered social-interaction data, using LLM ratings to improve social goal completion while monitoring safety and MMLU retention.
- [Strengthening Multimodal Large Language Model with Bootstrapped Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.08730): Uses model-bootstrapped negative responses and preference optimization to reduce MLLM reliance on pretraining statistics over visual evidence.
- [Simple and Scalable Strategies to Continually Pre-train Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.08763): Combines learning-rate re-warming, learning-rate re-decay, and replay of previous data to match full retraining during continual LLM pretraining.
- [Usable XAI: 10 Strategies Towards Exploiting Explainability in the LLM Era](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.08946): Organizes usable explainability for LLM systems into 10 strategies covering how XAI can explain and improve LLM applications and how LLMs can improve explanation workflows.
- [Towards a theory of model distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.09053): Formalizes PAC-distillation to analyze when a complex model can be replaced by a simpler one and what data or runtime is required.
- [AutoLoRA: Automatically Tuning Matrix Ranks in Low-Rank Adaptation Based on Meta Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.09113): Uses meta-learning to assign LoRA ranks automatically across layers, reducing exhaustive rank search in parameter-efficient fine-tuning.
- [Dial-insight: Fine-tuning Large Language Models with High-Quality Domain-Specific Data Preventing Capability Collapse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.09167): Generates and scores diverse domain-specific prompts to build fine-tuning data that improves specialization while limiting general capability collapse.
- [PYRA: Parallel Yielding Re-Activation for Training-Inference Efficient Task Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.09192): Combines parameter-efficient adaptation with structural re-activation so task tuning can reduce training overhead without sacrificing inference efficiency.
- [Are Vision Language Models Texture or Shape Biased and Can We Steer Them?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.09193): Measures texture versus shape bias in VLMs and tests whether language prompts can steer their visual cue preferences.
- [MambaTalk: Efficient Holistic Gesture Synthesis with Selective State Space Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.09471): Uses Mamba-style state-space blocks with discrete motion priors for low-latency, long-sequence gesture synthesis from speech or text.
- [Easy-to-Hard Generalization: Scalable Alignment Beyond Human Supervision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.09472): Trains reward models on easier problems and uses them for reranking or RL on harder reasoning tasks, enabling alignment beyond direct human supervision.
- [PreCurious: How Innocent Pre-Trained Language Models Turn into Privacy Traps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.09562): Shows how benign pretrained LMs can become privacy traps after downstream use, analyzing memorization and leakage risks in adapted models.
- [ExploRLLM: Guiding Exploration in Reinforcement Learning with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.09583): Combines foundation-model-generated policy code and representations with residual RL to improve exploration and sim-to-real transfer in robot manipulation.
- [Quiet-STaR: Language Models Can Teach Themselves to Think Before Speaking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.09629): Trains language models to generate internal rationales before answering, using self-generated thought traces to improve predictions without external rationale labels.
- [Open-Universe Indoor Scene Generation using LLM Program Synthesis and Uncurated Object Databases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.09675): Generates indoor scenes from open-ended text by having an LLM synthesize programs that retrieve objects from uncurated databases, rather than relying on a fixed scene vocabulary or object taxonomy.
- [EfficientVMamba: Atrous Selective Scan for Light Weight Visual Mamba](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.09977): Adds atrous selective scan and a convolution branch to Visual Mamba blocks, improving lightweight vision accuracy with linear-complexity global modeling.
- [HawkEye: Training Video-Text LLMs for Grounding Text in Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.10228): Trains video-text LLMs for temporal and spatial grounding by aligning text with video evidence, targeting fine-grained grounding rather than only video-level captioning or QA.
- [Dreaming of Many Worlds: Learning Contextual World Models Aids Zero-Shot Generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.10967): Learns contextual world models that predict environment dynamics across many settings, improving zero-shot generalization when context shifts.
- [Source Prompt Disentangled Inversion for Boosting Image Editability with Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.11105): Introduces SPDInv, a fixed-point diffusion inversion method that decouples latent noise from the source prompt to reduce text-driven image-editing artifacts.
- [Spiking Wavelet Transformer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.11138): Combines spiking neural computation with wavelet-style Transformer representations to improve efficient visual sequence modeling.
- [LLM Guided Evolution - The Automation of Models Advancing Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.11446): Uses LLMs to mutate model code and reflect on prior variants through Evolution of Thought, automating evolutionary model design.
- [Arc2Face: A Foundation Model for ID-Consistent Human Faces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.11641): Adapts Stable Diffusion with ArcFace identity embeddings and WebFace42M upsampling to generate diverse face images that preserve subject identity.
- [Crystalformer: Infinitely Connected Attention for Periodic Structure Encoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.11686): Reformulates attention over periodic crystal structures as tractable neural potential summation, enabling property prediction for infinitely repeating atom arrangements.
- [Jetfire: Efficient and Accurate Transformer Pretraining with INT8 Data Flow and Per-Block Quantization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.12422): Introduces Jetfire, using INT8 data flow and per-block quantization to make Transformer pretraining more efficient while controlling accuracy loss.
- [Towards Multimodal In-Context Learning for Vision & Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.12736): Studies and improves how VLMs transfer LLM-style in-context learning when demonstrations include both visual and language examples.
- [Toward Sustainable GenAI using Generation Directives for Carbon-Friendly Large Language Model Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.12900): Introduces Sprout, which assigns generation directives to prompts with an optimizer and quality evaluator to cut Llama 2 inference carbon emissions by more than 40 percent in grid-aware experiments.
- [Dated Data: Tracing Knowledge Cutoffs in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.12958): Defines effective cutoffs for model knowledge by probing versioned resources, then traces mismatches to CommonCrawl temporal bias and deduplication behavior in pretraining datasets.
- [SceneScript: Reconstructing Scenes With An Autoregressive Structured Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.13064): Generates 3D scene models as sequences of structured language commands, trained on the synthetic Aria Synthetic Environments dataset.
- [Evolutionary optimization of model merging recipes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.13187): Uses evolutionary search to discover parameter-space and data-flow model merging recipes from open-source LLMs without heavy additional training.
- [Adaptive Ensembles of Fine-Tuned Transformers for LLM-Generated Text Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.13335): Ensembles specialized Transformer detectors adaptively to improve LLM-generated text detection under both in-distribution and out-of-distribution data.
- [VL-Mamba: Exploring State Space Models for Multimodal Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.13600): Replaces Transformer language backbones in MLLMs with pre-trained Mamba state-space models for linear-scaling multimodal inference.
- [Probabilistic Forecasting with Stochastic Interpolants and Föllmer Processes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.13724): Builds conditional generative forecasters with stochastic interpolants and Föllmer-process dynamics to sample future states of dynamical systems.
- [RAR: Retrieving and Ranking Augmented MLLMs for Visual Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.13805): Adds CLIP-based category memory retrieval and MLLM reranking to improve fine-grained, few-shot, and zero-shot visual recognition.
- [MMIDR: Teaching Large Language Model to Interpret Multimodal Misinformation via Knowledge Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.14171): Distills proprietary LLM rationales into open-source LLMs so they can explain multimodal misinformation judgments from processed visual and retrieved evidence.
- [Reinforcement Learning from Reflective Feedback (RLRF): Aligning and Improving LLMs via Fine-Grained Self-Reflection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.14238): Uses fine-grained self-reflection feedback as an RL signal, improving LLM capabilities beyond surface-level preference alignment.
- [Exploring the Potential of Large Language Models in Graph Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.14358): Explores graph generation with LLMs, testing whether textual graph descriptions and prompts can produce valid graph structures.
- [Detoxifying Large Language Models via Knowledge Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.14472): Proposes DINM, a knowledge-editing detoxification baseline that adjusts toxic parameters within a few tuning steps using SafeEdit attack categories and metrics.
- [Cobra: Extending Mamba to Multi-Modal Large Language Model for Efficient Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.14520): Extends Mamba to multimodal large language models for efficient inference.
- [Implicit Style-Content Separation using B-LoRA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.14572): Trains two LoRA blocks in SDXL to separate style and content, enabling style transfer, text-based stylization, and style-content mixing from a single image.
- [ReAct Meets ActRe: When Language Agents Enjoy Training Data Autonomy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.14589): Uses language agents to autonomously collect and refine their own training data, connecting ReAct-style interaction traces with ActRe data generation for agent improvement.
- [PSALM: Pixelwise SegmentAtion with Large Multi-Modal Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.14598): Extends an LMM with a mask decoder and multimodal input schema so one model can jointly train across referring, panoptic, interactive, and open-vocabulary segmentation.
- [MyVLM: Personalizing VLMs for User-Specific Queries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.14599): Personalizes VLMs with external concept heads and learned concept embeddings that let BLIP-2 and LLaVA recognize user-specific visual concepts.
- [Towards Modeling Learner Performance with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.14661): Fine-tunes LLMs for knowledge tracing, testing whether sequence-modeling ability can predict learner performance trajectories across educational datasets.
- [AutoRE: Document-Level Relation Extraction with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.14888): Introduces AutoRE, an end-to-end document-level relation extractor using the relation-head-facts paradigm and QLoRA tuning without fixed relation-option prompts.
- [LLM2LLM: Boosting LLMs with Novel Iterative Data Enhancement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.15042): Iteratively augments low-data fine-tuning sets by having a teacher LLM generate new examples from the student model's mistakes.
- [Make VLM Recognize Visual Hallucination on Cartoon Character Image with Pose Information](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.15048): Uses pose information to help VLMs recognize visual hallucinations in cartoon-character images, targeting a narrow hallucination-detection setting.
- [Text clustering with large language model embeddings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.15112): Compares LLM-derived embeddings, clustering algorithms, summarization-based dimensionality reduction, and model size choices for organizing uncategorized text datasets.
- [Not All Attention is Needed: Parameter and Computation Efficient Transfer Learning for Multi-modal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.15226): Reduces multimodal transfer cost by selecting only necessary attention components, making MLLM adaptation cheaper in parameters and compute.
- [Spectral Motion Alignment for Video Motion Transfer using Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.15249): Aligns spectral motion representations in diffusion-based video motion transfer, improving how motion patterns move across source and target videos.
- [SiMBA: Simplified Mamba-Based Architecture for Vision and Multivariate Time series](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.15360): Combines Mamba sequence modeling with EinFFT channel modeling to stabilize large SSM vision and time-series backbones across ImageNet, transfer learning, and seven forecasting datasets.
- [Can large language models explore in-context?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.15371): Studies whether LLMs can perform in-context exploration in sequential decision tasks, separating exploration behavior from ordinary next-token prediction or static reasoning.
- [LLaVA-Prumerge: Adaptive Token Reduction for Efficient Large Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.15388): Reduces LLaVA visual-token load by selecting class-token-attended tokens and merging redundant keys, achieving about 14x compression with comparable VQA and reasoning performance.
- [Decoding Compressed Trust: Scrutinizing the Trustworthiness of Efficient LLMs Under Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.15447): Audits trustworthiness of compressed LLMs, measuring how pruning, quantization, or distillation affect safety and reliability signals.
- [Emergent World Models and Latent Variable Estimation in Chess-Playing Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.15498): Trains next-character chess language models and uses probes plus activation edits to show internal board-state and player-skill representations that can change predicted play strength.
- [Contact-aware Human Motion Generation from Textual Descriptions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.15709): Builds RICH-CAT contact-aware motion-text data and CATMO, which jointly generates 3D body motion and contact sequences with VQ-VAE contact and motion latents plus an intertwined GPT.
- [The Frontier of Data Erasure: Machine Unlearning for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.15779): Reviews LLM machine-unlearning methods for unstructured text and structured classification data, emphasizing targeted forgetting, model-utility retention, and over-erasure risks.
- [LLMs Are Few-Shot In-Context Low-Resource Language Learners](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.16512): Studies in-context and cross-lingual prompting across 25 low-resource languages, finding query alignment more effective than label alignment for narrowing language gaps.
- [Concerned with Data Contamination? Assessing Countermeasures in Code Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.16898): Tests recent-data, newly curated, and refactored-code countermeasures on more than two million timestamped Python functions, finding common contamination defenses and perplexity checks can be unreliable for code LMs.
- [Data Mixing Laws: Optimizing Data Mixtures by Predicting Language Modeling Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.16952): Fits data-mixture performance laws from small runs, nests them with model-size and step scaling laws, and uses them to choose RedPajama mixtures that match longer default training.
- [InternLM2 Technical Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.17297): Reports InternLM2 model training, data, long-context support, and alignment results as a technical reference for an open LLM family.
- [PlainMamba: Improving Non-Hierarchical Mamba in Visual Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.17695): Adapts Mamba to vision with continuous 2D scanning, direction-aware updates, constant-width PlainMamba blocks, and no special tokens for scalable recognition.
- [Mechanistic Design and Scaling of Hybrid Architectures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.17844): Uses mechanistic architecture-design proxy tasks, compute-optimal and state-optimal scaling laws, and 500-plus 70M-7B language models to show hybrid sparse topologies can outperform Transformer, Hyena, and Mamba baselines.
- [Quantifying and Mitigating Unimodal Biases in Multimodal Large Language Models: A Causal Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.18346): Uses a causal VQA framework, the 12,000-example MORE dataset, and the CAVE agent framework to measure and reduce language and vision biases in multimodal LLM reasoning.
- [Sequential Recommendation with Latent Relations based on Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.18348): Mines latent item relations from LLM language-knowledge representations with a discrete-state VAE and jointly optimizes relation discovery with sequential recommendation objectives.
- [BLADE: Enhancing Black-box Large Language Models with Small Domain-Specific Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.18365): Pairs a black-box general LLM with a small domain LM trained on vertical-domain data and knowledge instructions, then uses Bayesian optimization to combine them on legal and medical benchmarks.
- [Mini-Gemini: Mining the Potential of Multi-Modality Vision Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.18814): Enhances VLMs with high-resolution visual tokens, curated comprehension and generation data, and VLM-guided generation, scaling dense and MoE backbones from 2B to 34B.
- [XMoE: Sparse Models with Fine-grained and Adaptive Expert Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.18926): Uses small experts and a threshold router so tokens engage only essential MoE parameters, cutting MoE-layer computation by more than 50 percent on language modeling and machine translation.
- [Learning From Correctness Without Prompting Makes LLM Efficient Reasoner](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.19094): Trains efficient reasoning from correctness signals without explicit prompting, using answer correctness to improve reasoning behavior directly.
- [STaR-GATE: Teaching Language Models to Ask Clarifying Questions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.19154): Teaches LMs to ask clarifying questions through STaR-GATE, turning ambiguity handling into a learned generation behavior.
- [Disentangling Length from Quality in Direct Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.19159): Diagnoses DPO's tendency to exploit verbosity through out-of-distribution bootstrapping and adds a regularizer that preserves quality gains while controlling answer length.
- [Make Large Language Model a Better Ranker](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.19181): Introduces ALRO for LLM recommenders, combining soft lambda listwise loss with permutation-sensitive learning to align next-token prediction with candidate ranking order.
- [Are Large Language Models Good at Utility Judgments?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.19216): Benchmarks five LLMs on judging passage utility for open-domain QA, separates utility from relevance, and proposes k-sampling listwise judgments for retrieval-augmented answer generation.
- [Efficient Learning with Sine-Activated Low-Rank Matrices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.19243): Inserts sinusoidal activation into low-rank matrix decompositions, raising effective rank while retaining parameter efficiency across ViTs, LLMs, NeRFs, and 3D shape models.
- [Dataverse: Open-Source ETL (Extract, Transform, Load) Pipeline for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.19340): Provides Dataverse, an open-source ETL pipeline for LLM data preparation, covering extraction, transformation, and loading for training corpora.
- [Checkpoint Merging via Bayesian Optimization in LLM Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.19390): Searches checkpoint-merge weights with Bayesian optimization over shared LLM pretraining trajectories, using a held-out set to improve pretraining at low extra cost.
- [RSMamba: Remote Sensing Image Classification With State Space Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.19654): Adapts Mamba for remote-sensing scene classification with a dynamic multi-path activation mechanism that models non-causal 2D image data while preserving linear complexity.
- [Language Models Learn Rare Phenomena from Less Rare Phenomena: The Case of the Missing AANNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.19827): Studies how LMs infer rare linguistic phenomena from more common ones, using missing AANN constructions as the test case.
- [MambaMixer: Efficient Selective State Space Models with Dual Token and Channel Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.19888): Adds selective token and channel mixers plus weighted early-feature access, yielding ViM2 and TSM2 backbones for vision and time-series forecasting.
- [Transformer-Lite: High-efficiency Deployment of Large Language Models on Mobile Phone GPUs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.20041): Optimizes on-device LLM inference with dynamic-shape symbolic execution, GPU operator priority, FP4 M0E4 quantization, and KV-cache sub-tensor handling for 2B to 14B models.
- [Learn "No" to Say "Yes" Better: Improving Vision-Language Models via Negations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.20312): Creates CC-Neg with true and negated captions and trains CoN-CLIP with modified contrastive loss so VLMs better encode negation and compositional text-image semantics.
- [Gecko: Versatile Text Embeddings Distilled from Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.20327): Distills LLM knowledge into a compact text embedder by generating synthetic query-passage pairs and relabeling positives and hard negatives, improving MTEB retrieval at 256 and 768 dimensions.
- [Comparing Bad Apples to Good Oranges: Aligning Large Language Models via Joint Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.00530): Proposes JPO, optimizing the joint probability of chosen instruction-response pairs over rejected pairs so alignment can use preferences over both prompts and answers.
- [Face It Yourselves: An LLM-Based Two-Stage Strategy to Localize Configuration Errors via Logs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.00640): Localizes configuration root causes from logs with LogConfigLocalizer's two-stage LLM strategy, reporting high accuracy on Hadoop configuration-error cases for end users without source access.
- [Rehearsal-Free Modular and Compositional Continual Learning for Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.00790): Adds modular continual-learning components that compose new language-model skills without rehearsal data, targeting retention and transfer.
- [Do language models plan ahead for future tokens?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.00859): Probes whether language models plan future tokens before emitting them, using prediction behavior to test lookahead representations.
- [Query Performance Prediction Using Relevance Judgments Generated by Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01012): Uses LLM-generated relevance judgments for query performance prediction, estimating retrieval difficulty without relying only on human labels.
- [Large Language Model Evaluation Via Multi AI Agents: Preliminary results](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01023): Evaluates LLM outputs via multiple AI agents, a low-confidence row that is closer to evaluation harness design than pure model training.
- [Green AI: exploring carbon footprints, mitigation strategies, and trade offs in large language model training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01157): Estimates CO2 emissions for major LLM training settings, compares GPU hardware footprints, and analyzes mitigation tradeoffs that preserve model robustness and performance.
- [Large Language Models are Capable of Offering Cognitive Reappraisal, if Guided](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01288): Uses RESORT reappraisal constitutions to guide LLMs in generating cognitive-reappraisal support, then has clinical psychologists evaluate responses to social-media distress posts.
- [LLaVA-Gemma: Accelerating Multimodal Foundation Models with a Compact Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01331): Trains LLaVA-style multimodal models on compact Gemma backbones and ablates connector pretraining, vision-backbone strength, and language-model size effects.
- [Prompt-prompted Adaptive Structured Pruning for Efficient LLM Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01365): Introduces GRIFFIN, a training-free sequence-level FF-expert selection method that exploits structured activation flocking to use about half the feedforward parameters during generation.
- [Are large language models superhuman chemists?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01475): Tests whether LLMs perform beyond expert baselines on chemistry tasks, treating chemistry reasoning as a capability evaluation signal.
- [What Can Transformer Learn with Varying Depth? Case Studies on Sequence Learning Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01601): Designs synthetic sequence tasks showing one attention layer handles memorization, two layers are needed for reasoning and generalization, and three may be needed for contextual generalization.
- [AddSR: Accelerating Diffusion-based Blind Super-Resolution with Adversarial Diffusion Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01717): Speeds diffusion blind super-resolution with adversarial diffusion distillation, prediction-based self-refinement, HR-guided teacher constraints, and timestep-adaptive balancing.
- [Octopus v2: On-device language model for super agent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01744): Compresses function calling into an on-device 2B model, cutting context length and latency so agent-style API routing can run under mobile privacy and cost constraints.
- [Accelerating Transformer Pre-Training with 2: 4 Sparsity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01847): Studies 2:4 sparse Transformer pretraining with flip-rate monitoring, masked gradient decay, dense final tuning, transposable masks, and gated-activation kernel optimizations.
- [HyperCLOVA X Technical Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01954): Documents a Korean- and English-capable LLM family trained on Korean, English, and code data, then instruction tuned with safety-guided human annotations and evaluated across reasoning, coding, math, chat, and harmlessness.
- [Advancing LLM Reasoning Generalists with Preference Trees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.02078): Builds preference trees over reasoning traces so LLMs can learn generalist reasoning behavior from structured comparisons rather than isolated final answers.
- [Mixture-of-Depths: Dynamically allocating compute in transformer-based language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.02258): Caps per-layer token participation with top-k routing so Transformers learn token-level dynamic compute allocation while retaining predictable total FLOPs and faster sampling.
- [Calibrating the Confidence of Large Language Models by Eliciting Fidelity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.02655): Decomposes LLM confidence into question uncertainty and answer fidelity, then evaluates a plug-and-play calibration method on six RLHF-tuned models and four MCQA datasets.
- [Rethinking Kullback-Leibler Divergence in Knowledge Distillation for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.02657): Shows forward and reverse KL behave similarly in long-run LLM distillation, then introduces adaptive KL weighting to balance head and tail distribution learning early in training.
- [Faster Diffusion via Temporal Attention Decomposition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.02747): Accelerates diffusion by decomposing temporal attention, reducing video or temporal generation cost while preserving temporal coherence.
- [Cherry on Top: Parameter Heterogeneity and Quantization in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.02837): Identifies high-impact "cherry" parameters and proposes CherryQ, preserving those parameters in high precision while quantizing the rest for low-bit LLM inference.
- [Toward Inference-optimal Mixture-of-Expert Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.02852): Extends MoE scaling analysis with inference efficiency, showing few-expert models can be serving-optimal even when larger expert counts look loss-optimal under training budgets.
- [Linear Attention Sequence Parallelism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.02882): Parallelizes linear attention across sequences, improving training throughput for long-context linear-attention models.
- [Min-K%++: Improved Baseline for Detecting Pre-Training Data from Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.02936): Detects likely pretraining examples by testing whether tokens form local maxima under the LLM's conditional categorical distribution, improving AUROC on WikiMIA and MIMIR.
- [PiSSA: Principal Singular Values and Singular Vectors Adaptation of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.02948): Initializes LoRA-style adapter matrices from principal singular components of the base weights, speeding convergence and improving PEFT and quantized PEFT across models up to 70B.
- [Uncertainty in Language Models: Assessment through Rank-Calibration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03163): Proposes rank-calibration for comparing LM uncertainty measures by whether higher uncertainty corresponds to lower generation quality without ad hoc correctness thresholds.
- [How Easily do Irrelevant Inputs Skew the Responses of Large Language Models?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03302): Constructs semantically unrelated, partially related, and related distractor passages to test RAG robustness, showing LLMs are easily distracted by highly retrieved irrelevant context.
- [ReFT: Representation Finetuning for Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03592): Introduces ReFT, editing hidden representations rather than full weights to parameter-efficiently steer language-model behavior.
- [Mitigating the Impact of Outlier Channels for Language Model Quantization with Activation Regularization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03605): Uses quantization-aware input regularization and activation-kurtosis output regularization to control outlier channels and obtain competitive W4A4 language models.
- [X-Lifecycle Learning for Cloud Incident Management using LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03662): Trains and applies LLMs over cross-SDLC incident data such as code, configs, monitoring signals, dependencies, and troubleshooting documents for cloud incident detection, root-cause analysis, and mitigation.
- [Direct Nash Optimization: Teaching Language Models to Self-Improve with General Preferences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03715): Optimizes LLM policies toward a Nash equilibrium under general preferences, enabling self-improvement from preference feedback beyond simple pairwise win objectives.
- [GenQREnsemble: Zero-Shot LLM Ensemble Prompting for Generative Query Reformulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03746): Uses zero-shot LLM ensemble prompting to generate multiple query reformulations, improving retrieval-oriented query rewriting without task-specific training.
- [Outlier-Efficient Hopfield Layers for Large Transformer-Based Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03828): Replaces standard feedforward computation with Hopfield-style associative layers designed to handle activation outliers more efficiently in large Transformer models.
- [BiSHop: Bi-Directional Cellular Learning for Tabular Data with Generalized Sparse Modern Hopfield Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03830): Models tabular rows and columns with bi-directional cellular learning and generalized sparse modern Hopfield layers for tabular prediction.
- [Verifiable by Design: Aligning Language Models to Quote from Pre-Training Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03862): Aligns LMs to answer by quoting memorized pretraining passages, making generations more verifiable through explicit source-text reproduction rather than unsupported claims.
- [Simple Techniques for Enhancing Sentence Embeddings in Generative Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.03921): Improves sentence embeddings from generative LMs with lightweight pooling and prompting choices, making decoder-only representations more useful for semantic similarity and retrieval.
- [Label Propagation for Zero-shot Classification with Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.04072): Uses graph-based label propagation over VLM image-text representations to improve zero-shot classification without supervised examples for the target classes.
- [Robust Preference Optimization with Provable Noise Tolerance for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.04102): Derives a noisy-preference-tolerant optimization objective for LLM alignment and analyzes why it remains stable when human or model feedback labels are imperfect.
- [No "Zero-Shot" Without Exponential Data: Pretraining Concept Frequency Determines Multimodal Model Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.04125): Links VLM zero-shot performance to pretraining concept frequency, showing rare concepts require exponentially more data and challenging claims of broad zero-shot generalization.
- [Social Skill Training with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.04204): Uses LLMs for social-skill training simulations, a low-confidence model row because the contribution is mainly an interactive training setup.
- [Sigma: Siamese Mamba Network for Multi-Modal Semantic Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.04256): Builds a Siamese Mamba segmentation network for multimodal inputs, using state-space long-range modeling to fuse modalities for semantic segmentation.
- [Bias Amplification in Language Model Evolution: An Iterated Learning Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.04286): Studies iterative learning loops where language models train on model-generated data, showing how social or distributional biases can amplify across model generations.
- [SELF-(IN)CORRECT: LLMs Struggle with Refining Self-Generated Responses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.04298): Shows that LLMs often fail to identify whether candidate answers are their own generated responses, exposing limits of self-correction and self-discrimination pipelines.
- [Parameter Efficient Quasi-Orthogonal Fine-Tuning via Givens Rotation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.04316): Uses trainable Givens rotations to apply quasi-orthogonal parameter-efficient updates, offering an alternative to low-rank adapters for fine-tuning.
- [Prompt Public Large Language Models to Synthesize Data for Private On-device Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.04360): Prompts public LLMs to synthesize task data for private on-device applications, reducing the need to expose private data during model adaptation.
- [Aligning Diffusion Models by Optimizing Human Utility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.04465): Frames diffusion-model alignment as optimizing human utility, using preference or reward signals to steer generated images toward user-valued outputs.
- [Analyzing LLM Usage in an Advanced Computing Class in India](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.04603): Analyzes how students in an advanced computing course use LLMs, providing classroom evidence about model assistance patterns, learning behavior, and limitations.
- [Multicalibration for Confidence Scoring in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.04689): Applies multicalibration to LLM confidence scoring so probability estimates remain calibrated across many subgroups rather than only on aggregate accuracy.
- [Adapting LLMs for Efficient Context Processing through Soft Prompt Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.04997): Introduces SoftPromptComp, combining natural-language summarization, dynamic soft prompts, and utility-preserving weighting to compress long contexts for efficient LLM processing.
- [FGAIF: Aligning Large Vision-Language Models with Fine-grained AI Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.05046): Aligns LVLMs with AI-generated fine-grained hallucination feedback, training separate dense reward models for existence, attribute, and relation errors and integrating them into PPO.
- [How Bad is Training on Synthetic Data? A Statistical Analysis of Language Model Collapse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.05090): Provides a statistical model of recursive LM training on synthetic data, proving all-synthetic loops collapse and estimating safe real-synthetic mixing levels.
- [Dense Training, Sparse Inference: Rethinking Training of Mixture-of-Experts Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.05567): Rethinks MoE training by keeping dense training signals while using sparse inference, aiming to improve expert utilization and serving efficiency.
- [CodecLM: Aligning Language Models with Tailored Synthetic Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.05875): Aligns language models with tailored synthetic data in CodecLM, using generated task data to steer downstream behavior.
- [Optimization Methods for Personalizing Large Language Models through Retrieval Augmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.05970): Optimizes personal-document retrievers for LLM personalization with downstream task feedback, using reinforcement learning, LLM-to-retriever distillation, and pre/post-generation retriever selection on LaMP.
- [Elephants Never Forget: Memorization and Learning of Tabular Data in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.06209): Studies memorization and learning of tabular data in LLMs, separating useful table learning from verbatim memorization risk.
- [OmniFusion Technical Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.06212): Reports OmniFusion, a multimodal model family and training setup for fusing visual and language capabilities.
- [AgentsCoDriver: Large Language Model Empowered Collaborative Driving with Lifelong Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.06345): Builds collaborative driving agents with observation, reasoning, cognitive memory, reinforcement reflection, and communication modules for negotiation and lifelong learning in traffic environments.
- [Policy-Guided Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.06356): Guides diffusion sampling with policy signals, connecting diffusion generation with reinforcement-learning style policy control.
- [MiniCPM: Unveiling the Potential of Small Language Models with Scalable Training Strategies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.06395): Studies scalable training strategies for small language models through the MiniCPM family.
- [InternLM-XComposer2-4KHD: A Pioneering Large Vision-Language Model Handling Resolutions from 336 Pixels to 4K HD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.06512): Extends InternLM-XComposer2 to dynamic 336-pixel through 4K HD inputs by automatic patch configuration over a 336-by-336 ViT, improving high-resolution LVLM benchmarks.
- [Exploring Concept Depth: How Large Language Models Acquire Knowledge at Different Layers?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07066): Probes concept depth across LLM layers, identifying where different knowledge concepts are acquired and represented.
- [Dynamic Generation of Personalities with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07084): Generates dynamic personalities with LLMs, treating persona construction as a controllable generation and simulation problem.
- [Scaling Laws for Data Filtering—Data Curation Cannot be Compute Agnostic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07177): Derives compute-aware scaling laws for VLM data filtering, modeling quality-subset utility, repetition decay, and data-pool interactions to choose DataComp training pools.
- [BRAVE: Broadening the visual encoding of vision-language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07204): Consolidates features from multiple frozen vision encoders into a compact representation for frozen LMs, improving captioning and VQA while reducing VLM blindness and hallucination.
- [JetMoE: Reaching Llama2 Performance with 0.1M Dollars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07413): Presents JetMoE, a low-cost sparse MoE LLM trained to approach Llama 2 performance with a much smaller budget.
- [From Words to Numbers: Your Large Language Model Is Secretly A Capable Regressor When Given In-Context Examples](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07544): Tests GPT-4, Claude 3, Llama 2, and related models on in-context linear and nonlinear regression, finding sublinear-regret behavior without gradient updates.
- [Measuring Geographic Diversity of Foundation Models with a Natural Language-based Geo-guessing Experiment on GPT-4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07612): Probes GPT-4 geographic representation with DBpedia-based geo-guessing tasks, exposing global feature gaps, regional disparities, and differences between unimodal and multimodal variants.
- [Why do small language models underperform? Studying Language Model Saturation via the Softmax Bottleneck](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07647): Explains small-LM training saturation through a hidden-dimension versus target-rank mismatch in the softmax bottleneck, with late-pretraining representation degeneration below 1000 hidden dimensions.
- [ODA: Observation-Driven Agent for integrating LLMs and Knowledge Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07677): Integrates LLMs with knowledge graphs through an observation-driven agent loop; low-confidence here because the contribution is more harness-like.
- [Applying Guidance in a Limited Interval Improves Sample and Distribution Quality in Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07724): Finds that classifier-free guidance can be restricted to a limited denoising interval to improve both sample fidelity and distribution quality in diffusion models.
- [RecurrentGemma: Moving Past Transformers for Efficient Open Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07839): Releases 2B and 9B Griffin-based language models that combine linear recurrences with local attention and fixed-size state for lower-memory long-sequence inference.
- [On Training Data Influence of GPT Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07840): Introduces GPTfluence, a featurized simulation of GPT training dynamics that estimates individual training-example influence across 14M to 2.8B parameter models and fine-tuning settings.
- [HGRN2: Gated Linear RNNs with State Expansion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07904): Adds state expansion to gated linear RNNs, improving recurrent sequence-model capacity while retaining efficient long-context inference.
- [Rho-1: Not All Tokens Are What You Need](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07965): Introduces Rho-1 data selection, emphasizing that only selected high-value tokens should drive language-model pretraining.
- [Lossless Acceleration of Large Language Model via Adaptive N-gram Parallel Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.08698): Uses an adaptive multi-level n-gram draft module plus original-LLM verification to generate multiple tokens in parallel without retraining or extra GPU memory, reaching up to 3.67x speedup.
- [Game Generation via Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.08706): Generates game rules and levels together from video-game description language, extending LLM procedural content generation beyond level-only tasks with fixed rules.
- [Large Language Model Can Continue Evolving From Mistakes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.08707): Lets LLMs continue improving from mistakes by turning failure cases into new learning signals for self-evolution.
- [CATS: Contextually-Aware Thresholding for Sparsity in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.08763): Replaces standard activations with a nonlinear CATS function for activation sparsity, keeping Mistral-7B and Llama2-7B within 1-2% of base performance at 50% sparsity and adding a GPU kernel for about 15% faster token generation.
- [EIVEN: Efficient Implicit Attribute Value Extraction using Multimodal LLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.08886): Uses a pretrained LLM and vision encoder plus Learning-by-Comparison to extract implicit e-commerce attribute values from image-text product data with fewer labels and less confusion among similar values.
- [Fusion-Mamba for Cross-Modality Object Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.09146): Builds Fusion-Mamba blocks with state-space channel swapping and dual state-space fusion so cross-modal features interact in a hidden state space for object detection on M3FD and FLIR-Aligned.
- [TextHawk: Exploring Efficient Fine-Grained Perception of Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.09204): Builds TextHawk for fine-grained MLLM perception, emphasizing efficient visual-token processing for detailed text-rich and resolution-sensitive multimodal understanding.
- [FreqMamba: Viewing Mamba from a Frequency Perspective for Image Deraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.09476): Views Mamba from a frequency perspective and adapts it to image deraining, using frequency cues in state-space visual restoration.
- [Bridging the Gap between Different Vocabularies for LLM Ensemble](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.09492): Introduces EVA, which learns mappings between overlapping tokens across LLM vocabularies, projects next-token distributions into a shared space, and filters unfaithful generators for step-level ensemble decoding.
- [sigma-GPTs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.09562): Introduces an order-flexible autoregressive modeling variant. Core idea: add output-position encodings so generation order can be changed per sample, enabling conditioning on arbitrary token subsets and sampling multiple tokens with a rejection strategy instead of committing to a fixed left-to-right factorization.
- [LoRA Dropout as a Sparsity Regularizer for Overfitting Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.09610): Adds random noise to LoRA low-rank matrices as sparsity regularization, proves a generalization-error bound, and pairs it with test-time ensembling for better accuracy and calibration across NLP tasks.
- [Learn Your Reference Model for Real Good Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.09656): Proposes Trust Region offline alignment variants such as TR-DPO, TR-IPO, and TR-KTO that dynamically update the reference policy to reduce overoptimization on helpfulness, harmlessness, summarization, AlpacaEval 2, and Arena-Hard.
- [Are Large Language Models Reliable Argument Quality Annotators?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.09696): Compares LLM, expert, and novice labels across an argument-quality taxonomy, finding that LLM annotations are consistent and can improve inter-annotator agreement when added to human labeling.
- [Resilience of Large Language Models for Noisy Instructions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.09754): Evaluates and improves LLM resilience to noisy instructions, focusing on robustness when user prompts contain perturbations.
- [Impact of Preference Noise on the Alignment Performance of Generative Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.09824): Injects controlled preference-label noise into summarization and dialogue alignment experiments, showing 20-40% noisy preferences can sharply reduce win rate and that confidence-based filtering helps under some noise types.
- [Evolving Interpretable Visual Classifiers with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.09941): Uses LLM-guided evolutionary search to mutate concept-bottleneck attribute sets, producing interpretable fine-grained visual classifiers that improve iNaturalist and KikiBouba results without privileged class-name knowledge.
- [ClashEval: Quantifying the tug-of-war between an LLM's internal prior and external evidence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.10198): Builds a 1,200-question RAG stress test with answer perturbations across six domains and measures when models override correct priors with wrong retrieved evidence or correct themselves from retrieved content.
- [From Data Deluge to Data Curation: A Filtering-WoRA Paradigm for Efficient Text-based Person Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.10292): Filters synthetic text-person search pairs by cross-modal relevance, then applies Weighted Low-Rank Adaptation so fewer high-value examples train retrieval models faster on CUHK-PEDES.
- [ControlMTR: Control-Guided Motion Transformer with Scene-Compliant Intention Points for Feasible Motion Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.10295): Generates scene-compliant intention points and driving-control commands, converts controls to kinematic trajectories, and uses them as auxiliary guidance to reduce off-road motion predictions.
- [Efficient Generation of Targeted and Transferable Adversarial Examples for Vision-Language Models via Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.10335): Proposes AdvDiffVLM, which edits diffusion reverse-generation scores with adaptive ensemble gradients and GradCAM-guided masks to create natural targeted adversarial images that transfer to black-box VLMs such as GPT-4V.
- [Unveiling the Misuse Potential of Base Large Language Models via In-Context Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.10552): Shows base LLMs can follow malicious instructions when given carefully designed in-context demonstrations, and introduces risk metrics comparing base-model outputs with maliciously fine-tuned models.
- [HLAT: High-quality Large Language Model Pre-trained on AWS Trainium](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.10630): Reports HLAT, a high-quality LLM pretraining run on AWS Trainium, documenting the hardware-oriented training setup and resulting model quality.
- [Autoregressive Pre-Training on Pixels and Texts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.10710): Pretrains a single autoregressive model on document images with next-patch regression and on text with next-token classification, showing combined pixel-text training improves language and vision-language benchmarks.
- [Is DPO Superior to PPO for LLM Alignment? A Comprehensive Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.10719): Compares DPO and PPO for LLM alignment across controlled settings, clarifying when offline preference optimization matches or lags RLHF.
- [Fewer Truncations Improve Language Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.10830): Introduces Best-fit Packing, a length-aware sequence packing method that eliminates unnecessary document truncations while retaining concatenation efficiency and improving reading comprehension, context following, and program synthesis.
- [Small Language Models Are Good Too: An Empirical Study of Zero-Shot Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.11122): Shows small language models can be competitive zero-shot classifiers, separating classification utility from raw parameter scale.
- [Exploring the Transferability of Visual Prompting for Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.11207): Trains transferable visual prompts on one MLLM with feature-consistency alignment and cross-model regularization so the same prompt parameters improve downstream task accuracy across different MLLMs.
- [Text-Controlled Motion Mamba: Text-Instructed Temporal Grounding of Human Motion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.11375): Defines text-based human motion grounding and builds TM-Mamba, combining temporal global context, language-query control, and spatial graph topology with linear memory for long untrimmed motion sequences.
- [Pack of LLMs: Model Fusion at Test-Time via Perplexity Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.11531): Uses prompt perplexity to estimate each candidate LLM's expertise, then greedily optimizes fusion weights at inference time so arbitrary user-specified LLMs can be combined without a trained fuser.
- [Factorized Diffusion: Perceptual Illusions by Noise Decomposition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.11615): Factorizes diffusion noise to generate perceptual illusions, using noise decomposition as a controllable image-generation mechanism.
- [From Image to Video, what do we need in multimodal LLMs?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.11865): Analyzes what multimodal LLMs need when moving from image to video inputs, identifying temporal and representation gaps.
- [Aligning Language Models to Explicitly Handle Ambiguity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.11972): Aligns LMs to recognize ambiguous prompts and respond with clarification or multiple plausible interpretations instead of forcing a single unsupported answer.
- [Token-level Direct Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.11999): Optimizes preference alignment at the autoregressive token level with forward-KL constraints and a token-based Bradley-Terry reward formulation, improving the alignment-diversity balance over DPO and PPO-style RLHF.
- [Toward Self-Improvement of LLMs via Imagination, Searching, and Criticizing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.12253): Builds AlphaLLM with prompt synthesis, MCTS over language-task search spaces, and three critic models so LLMs can self-improve on mathematical reasoning without extra annotations.
- [Reka Core, Flash, and Edge: A Series of Powerful Multimodal Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.12387): Reports Reka Edge, Flash, and Core models trained from scratch for text, image, video, and audio inputs, with Core evaluated against frontier systems on MMMU, VQAv2, multimodal chat, MMLU, GSM8K, and Perception-Test.
- [MORepair: Teaching LLMs to Repair Code via Multi-Objective Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.12636): Fine-tunes code LLMs on both syntactic code transformations and the logical reason behind each patch, improving function-level and repository-level repair benchmarks over standard fine-tuning and RepairLLaMA.
- [Can LLMs Understand Computer Networks? Towards a Virtual System Administrator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.12689): Evaluates proprietary and open LLMs on topology-aware computer-network questions, showing the best zero-shot model reaches 79.3% average accuracy while complex topologies remain difficult.
- [Ensemble Learning for Heterogeneous Large Language Models with Deep Parallel Collaboration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.12715): Combines heterogeneous LLMs through deep parallel collaboration, using ensemble learning to exploit different model strengths.
- [MoVA: Adapting Mixture of Vision Experts to Multimodal Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.13046): Routes among CLIP, DINOv2, document, chart, and other vision experts with a coarse-to-fine strategy, then fuses their features through a MoV-Adapter for stronger multimodal benchmark generalization.
- [LLM Evaluators Recognize and Favor Their Own Generations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.13076): Shows LLM judges can identify and prefer their own outputs, exposing self-preference bias in model-based evaluation.
- [The Instruction Hierarchy: Training LLMs to Prioritize Privileged Instructions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.13208): Trains LLMs on an instruction hierarchy so system and developer instructions override lower-priority user or tool content during conflicts.
