- [Composing Pre-Trained Object-Centric Representations for Robotics From "What" and "Where" Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.13474): Constructs POCR without new pretraining by combining segmentation-based "where" object tracking with pretrained "what" descriptors, improving imitation policies on simulated and real robot manipulation tasks.
- [Rethink arbitrary style transfer with transformer and contrastive learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.13584): Improves arbitrary style transfer with Style Consistency Instance Normalization, instance-based contrastive learning over styles, and a Perception Encoder for style features to reduce artifacts.
- [Adversarial Representation Engineering: A General Model Editing Framework for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.13752): Introduces ARE, a model-editing framework that learns adversarial representation directions to modify LLM behavior across editing scenarios.
- [How Good Are Low-bit Quantized LLaMA3 Models? An Empirical Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.14047): Benchmarks ten post-training quantization and LoRA fine-tuning methods on LLaMA3 from 1 to 8 bits and LLaVA-Next-8B from 2 to 4 bits, exposing degradation under ultra-low precision.
- [Text-Tuple-Table: Towards Information Integration in Text-to-Table Generation via Global Tuple Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.14215): Generates tables from text by extracting global tuples first, improving text-to-table information integration.
- [Phi-3 Technical Report: A Highly Capable Language Model Locally on Your Phone](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.14219): Describes the Phi-3 family, including a 3.8B phi-3-mini trained on filtered web and synthetic data, aligned for chat and safety, and small enough for phone deployment while approaching larger-model benchmarks.
- [Better Synthetic Data by Retrieving and Transforming Existing Datasets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.14361): Improves synthetic data generation by retrieving existing datasets and transforming them, rather than sampling all examples from scratch.
- [Preference Fine-Tuning of LLMs Should Leverage Suboptimal, On-Policy Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.14367): Analyzes preference fine-tuning objectives and shows on-policy sampling or negative-gradient mode-seeking updates move probability mass more effectively than offline maximum-likelihood objectives.
- [SEED-X: Multimodal Models with Unified Multi-granularity Comprehension and Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.14396): Extends SEED-style multimodal modeling to arbitrary image sizes, aspect ratios, and multi-granularity image generation, then instruction tunes for real-world comprehension and generation tasks.
- [SpaceByte: Towards Deleting Tokenization from Large Language Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.14408): Replaces subword tokenization with a byte-level decoder that inserts larger Transformer blocks after space-like bytes, matching tokenized Transformer performance under fixed compute.
- [Hybrid LLM: Cost-Efficient and Quality-Aware Query Routing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.14618): Routes each query between a small edge-deployable model and a larger cloud model using predicted difficulty and a tunable quality target, reducing large-model calls by up to 40% without quality loss.
- [OpenELM: An Efficient Language Model Family with Open Training and Inference Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.14619): Releases OpenELM with layer-wise parameter scaling plus public training data recipes, logs, checkpoints, evaluation code, and Apple-device conversion tooling.
- [FMint: Bridging Human Designed and Data Pretrained Models for Differential Equation Foundation Model for Dynamical Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.14688): Pretrains a decoder-only transformer on 40K ODEs to learn in-context error correction over coarse solver outputs, improving challenging dynamical-system simulation accuracy by 1-2 orders and speed by 5x.
- [SST: Multi-Scale Hybrid Mamba-Transformer Experts for Time Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.14757): Builds SST with multi-scale hybrid Mamba and Transformer experts for time-series forecasting.
- [Simulating Task-Oriented Dialogues with State Transition Graphs and Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.14772): Simulates task-oriented dialogues with state-transition graphs plus LLM generation; low-confidence here because it is closer to a dialogue simulator.
- [RealTCD: Temporal Causal Discovery from Interventional Data with Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.14786): Discovers temporal causal structure for AIOps root-cause analysis without intervention targets, combining score-based masking and regularization with LLM-guided meta-initialization from system text.
- [Pillars of Grammatical Error Correction: Comprehensive Inspection Of Contemporary Approaches In The Era of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.14914): Compares single GEC systems, ensembling, ranking, and LLM-based variants, reporting 72.8 F0.5 on CoNLL-2014 and 81.4 on BEA with released code, trained models, and outputs.
- [Graph Machine Learning in the Era of Large Language Models (LLMs)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.14928): Surveys Graph ML and LLM interaction, covering LLM-improved graph features, label efficiency, heterophily and OOD handling, plus graph-enhanced LLM pretraining and inference.
- [Transformers Can Represent n-gram Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.14994): Proves that Transformer language models with hard or sparse attention can exactly represent any n-gram language model, giving a lower bound on probabilistic representational capacity.
- [Multi-Head Mixture-of-Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.15045): Introduces multi-head MoE, extending expert routing with multiple heads to increase sparse-model expressivity.
- [Rethinking LLM Memorization through the Lens of Adversarial Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.15146): Reframes memorization as adversarial compression, measuring how compact prompts can force LLMs to reconstruct training-like text.
- [MixLoRA: Enhancing Large Language Models Fine-Tuning with LoRA based Mixture of Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.15159): Inserts LoRA experts into frozen FFN blocks with independent attention-layer LoRA adapters, top-k routing, load-balance loss, and a high-throughput MoE framework for multi-task fine-tuning.
- [How to use and interpret activation patching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.15255): Provides practical guidance for activation patching, clarifying how to use and interpret causal interventions in model internals.
- [Bi-Mamba4TS: Bidirectional Mamba for Time Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.15772): Adapts bidirectional Mamba blocks to time-series forecasting so sequence models can use past and future context.
- [The PRISM Alignment Project: What Participatory, Representative and Individualised Human Feedback Reveals About the Subjective and Multicultural Alignment of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.16019): Releases PRISM, linking 1,500 participants from 75 countries to preferences and fine-grained feedback from 8,011 live conversations with 21 LLMs on subjective multicultural alignment issues.
- [The Ethics of Advanced AI Assistants](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.16244): Defines advanced AI assistants as natural-language agents that plan and execute user-directed action sequences, then maps risks around alignment, manipulation, trust, privacy, social deployment, and evaluation.
- [Andes: Defining and Enhancing Quality-of-Experience in LLM-Based Text Streaming Services](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.16283): Defines QoE for token-streaming LLM services and introduces a token-granularity preemptive scheduler that improves perceived service quality or cuts GPU use in live text generation.
- [List Items One by One: A New Data Source and Learning Paradigm for Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.16375): Trains open MLLMs to enumerate visual tags from Set-of-Mark prompted images using 10K to 30K tagged samples, improving reasoning and reducing object-reference hallucination.
- [Correlation-Decoupled Knowledge Distillation for Multimodal Sentiment Analysis with Incomplete Modalities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.16456): Proposes CorrKD for multimodal sentiment under missing modalities, combining sample-level contrastive distillation, category-prototype distillation, and response-disentangled consistency.
- [REBEL: Reinforcement Learning via Regressing Relative Rewards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.16767): Replaces PPO-style value networks and clipping with regression over relative rewards between completions, supporting offline data and preference patterns that can be intransitive.
- [Weak-to-Strong Extrapolation Expedites Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.16792): Uses ExPO to extrapolate the parameter delta between an SFT model and a partially aligned checkpoint, accelerating preference alignment from early training progress.
- [Make Your LLM Fully Utilize the Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.16811): Trains or prompts LLMs to use full context more effectively, targeting lost-in-the-middle and long-context underuse failures.
- [How far are we to GPT-4V? Closing the gap to commercial multimodal models with open-source suites](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.16821): Introduces InternVL 1.5 with a stronger InternViT-6B vision encoder, dynamic high-resolution tiling up to 4K inputs, and bilingual OCR-oriented data to close open-source MLLM gaps with commercial systems.
- [Machine Unlearning in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.16841): Uses an evaluative model to identify dialogs for unlearning, then combines distance-sensitive negative loss with cluster-mean positive loss to suppress harmful or private responses while preserving utility.
- [Evolve Cost-aware Acquisition Functions Using Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.16906): Combines LLM generation with evolutionary computation to design cost-aware Bayesian-optimization acquisition functions, testing them on synthetic functions and hyperparameter optimization tasks.
- [Automated Data Visualization from Natural Language via Large Language Models: An Exploratory Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.17136): Prompts LLMs to treat structured tables as executable programs for NL2Vis, comparing T5 and GPT-3.5 on nvBench with iterative chain-of-thought, role, and code-interpreter variants.
- [When to Trust LLMs: Aligning Confidence with Response Quality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.17287): Introduces CONQORD, an RL alignment method with quality and order-preserving confidence rewards so verbal confidence tracks answer quality and retrieval-use decisions.
- [Introducing cosmosGPT: Monolingual Training for Turkish Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.17336): Trains monolingual Turkish cosmosGPT models and releases new fine-tuning and evaluation datasets, showing much smaller Turkish-only models can compete with adapted multilingual baselines.
- [Probabilistic Inference in Language Models via Twisted Sequential Monte Carlo](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.17546): Applies twisted sequential Monte Carlo to probabilistic inference in language models, treating generation as guided particle inference.
- [Autonomous LLM-driven research from data to human-verifiable research papers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.17605): Builds data-to-paper, a stepwise LLM-agent platform that raises hypotheses, writes and debugs analysis code, interprets results, and produces traceable manuscripts from annotated data.
- [Empowering Large Language Models for Textual Data Augmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.17642): Automatically generates and selects task-informed augmentation instructions for LLM-based textual data augmentation, improving 26 few-shot classification tasks over manual instruction choices.
- [Temporal Scaling Law for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.17785): Formulates temporal scaling laws for LLMs, modeling how performance changes with time-dependent data and training progress.
- [LLMParser: An Exploratory Study on Using Large Language Models for Log Parsing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.18001): Studies few-shot generative log parsing with Flan-T5, LLaMA, and ChatGLM across 16 systems, reporting about 96% average parsing accuracy for structured log extraction.
- [Generative AI for Low-Carbon Artificial Intelligence of Things with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.18077): Uses LLMs with retrieval-augmented generation to formulate AIoT carbon-optimization problems, then applies diffusion models to identify carbon-reduction strategies.
- [Mamba-FETrack: Frame-Event Tracking via State Space Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.18174): Uses a Mamba state-space model for frame-event tracking, fusing frame and event streams in visual tracking.
- [WorldGPT: Empowering LLM as Multimodal World Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.18202): Builds an MLLM-based world model trained from videos, adding memory, retrieval, and reflection modules and evaluating multimodal dynamics understanding with WorldNet.
- [DPO Meets PPO: Reinforced Token Optimization for RLHF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.18922): Frames RLHF as a token-level MDP, derives token-wise rewards from preference data, and integrates DPO-style signal into PPO for AlpacaEval 2 and Arena-Hard improvements.
- [Who Followed the Blueprint? Analyzing the Responses of U.S. Federal Agencies to the Blueprint for an AI Bill of Rights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.19076): Analyzes U.S. federal agency responses to the October 2022 AI Bill of Rights Blueprint across its five principles, finding uneven uptake and limited direct policy influence.
- [Octopus v4: Graph of language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.19296): Uses functional tokens to route queries through a graph of specialized open models, activating under 10B parameters while reporting 74.8 MMLU accuracy.
- [Countering Reward Over-optimization in LLM with Demonstration-Guided Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.19409): Counters reward over-optimization with demonstration-guided reinforcement learning, anchoring LLM policy updates to demonstrations.
- [Lancet: Accelerating Mixture-of-Experts Training via Whole Graph Computation-Communication Overlapping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.19429): Accelerates MoE training by overlapping whole-graph computation and communication, targeting sparse expert training bottlenecks.
- [More Compute Is What You Need](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.19484): Proposes a unified compute-dependent scaling law, arguing that allocation between model size and data size can be chosen for inference efficiency or data exhaustion rather than a fixed Chinchilla optimum.
- [Extending Llama-3's Context Ten-Fold Overnight](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.19553): Extends Llama-3-8B-Instruct from 8K to 80K context using QLoRA, 3.5K GPT-4 synthetic long-context examples, and an eight-hour run on eight A800 GPUs.
- [Derivative learning of tensorial quantities-Predicting finite temperature infrared spectra from first principles.](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.19674): Combines machine learning with first-principles calculations to predict finite-temperature infrared spectra by learning tensorial derivatives and polarization surrogates for systems such as water and MAPbI3.
- [Naturally Supervised 3D Visual Grounding with Language-Regularized Concept Learners](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.19696): Introduces LARC, a language-regularized neuro-symbolic concept learner that performs 3D visual grounding from naturally supervised scene and question-answer pairs instead of dense object labels.
- [Better & Faster Large Language Models via Multi-token Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.19737): Trains LLMs with multi-token prediction so models learn to predict several future tokens and improve speed or sample efficiency.
- [KAN: Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.19756): Introduces Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks as an alternative neural architecture family that can inform model-side capability discussions.
- [A Primer on the Inner Workings of Transformer-based Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.00208): Provides a technical primer on interpreting decoder-only Transformer LMs, organizing mechanistic-interpretability methods and known internal mechanisms rather than proposing a new model.
- [RLHF from Heterogeneous Feedback via Personalization and Preference Aggregation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.00254): Extends RLHF for heterogeneous and strategic human preferences through personalization and aggregation, using multiple reward models or social-welfare aggregation instead of a single homogeneous reward.
- [A Careful Examination of Large Language Model Performance on Grade School Arithmetic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.00332): Creates GSM1k as a GSM8K-style held-out arithmetic benchmark and uses it to measure overfitting and memorization gaps across leading open and closed LLM families.
- [AdaMoLE: Fine-Tuning Large Language Models with Adaptive Mixture of Low-Rank Adaptation Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.00361): Fine-tunes LLMs with adaptive mixtures of low-rank experts, selecting LoRA-style experts per input or task.
- [Self-Play Preference Optimization for Language Model Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.00675): Uses self-play preference optimization to align language models from model-generated comparisons rather than only human preference data.
- [LoRA Land: 310 Fine-tuned LLMs that Rival GPT-4, A Technical Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.00732): Trains and evaluates 310 four-bit LoRA adapters across 10 base models and 31 tasks, using LoRAX to serve many task adapters on one A100.
- [Modeling Caption Diversity in Contrastive Vision-Language Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.00740): Introduces Llip, which models caption diversity by mixing a set of visual features with text-conditioned weights, improving ImageNet zero-shot classification and MS-COCO retrieval.
- [UniGen: Universal Domain Generalization for Sentiment Classification via Zero-shot Dataset Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.01022): Uses domain-independent zero-shot sentiment data generated by a large model to train a tiny classifier that generalizes across unseen sentiment domains at efficient inference cost.
- [Large Language Models are Inconsistent and Biased Evaluators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.01724): Audits LLM evaluators on SummEval and RoSE, identifying familiarity bias, skewed ratings, anchoring, and low inter-sample agreement, then tests mitigation recipes.
- [ALCM: Autonomous LLM-Augmented Causal Discovery Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.01744): Combines causal structure learning, a causal wrapper, and an LLM-driven refiner to iteratively improve causal graphs over seven benchmark datasets.
- [Real Risks of Fake Data: Synthetic Data, Diversity-Washing and Consent Circumvention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.01820): Analyzes synthetic-data risks around false diversity claims and consent circumvention, arguing that generated data can preserve or hide governance problems in ML training and testing.
- [Optimising Calls to Large Language Models with Uncertainty-Based Two-Tier Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.02134): Uses small-model generation uncertainty to choose routing or cascading to a larger LLM, outperforming fixed small or large model choices in 25 of 27 evaluated setups.
- [What matters when building vision-language models?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.02246): Ablates vision-language model design choices such as data mixture, image encoder, connector, language backbone, and training recipe to identify which components drive performance.
- [PICLe: Eliciting Diverse Behaviors from Large Language Models with Persona In-Context Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.02501): Elicits diverse LLM behaviors with persona in-context learning, conditioning generation on persona examples at inference time.
- [Enhancing Contextual Understanding in Large Language Models through Contrastive Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.02750): Applies inference-time contrastive decoding with adversarial irrelevant passages so generated answers rely more on prompt context and less on conflicting parametric knowledge.
- [Has this Fact been Edited? Detecting Knowledge Edits in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.02765): Detects whether an output relies on edited or pretrained facts by training AdaBoost classifiers over hidden states and probability distributions across four editing methods, two LLMs, and two datasets.
- [WDMoE: Wireless Distributed Large Language Models with Mixture of Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.03131): Splits MoE LLM deployment between a base station gate and device-side experts, selecting experts jointly by model performance and wireless latency.
- [Lory: Fully Differentiable Mixture-of-Experts for Autoregressive Language Model Pre-training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.03133): Introduces Lory, a fully differentiable MoE routing design for autoregressive language-model pretraining.
- [Quantifying the Capabilities of LLMs across Scale and Precision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.03146): Evaluates 7B to 70B open instruction models under quantization across NLU, reasoning, misinformation, and hallucination tests, finding larger models more resilient to four-bit precision.
- [CityLLaVA: Efficient Fine-Tuning for VLMs in City Scenario](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.03194): Fine-tunes urban VLMs with bounding-box preprocessing, video best-view selection, visual prompts, QA sequences, block expansion, and sequential questioning for traffic-safety analysis.
- [MAmmoTH2: Scaling Instructions from the Web](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.03548): Harvests 10 million instruction-response pairs from web pretraining corpora, then uses them to train MAmmoTH2 models that improve MATH, GSM8K, reasoning, and chat evaluations.
- [Generating Probabilistic Scenario Programs from Natural Language](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.03709): Builds ScenarioNL, a chained LLM, compiler, and simulator pipeline that converts crash reports into probabilistic Scenic scenario programs for rare-event simulation.
- [NL2Plan: Robust LLM-Driven Planning from Minimal Text Descriptions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.04215): Uses an LLM to extract PDDL domain and problem files from minimal natural-language descriptions, then delegates solution search to a classical planner across seven domains.
- [Large Language Models Cannot Explain Themselves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.04382): Argues that LLM self-explanations are exogenous text rather than faithful accounts of prediction mechanisms, reframing them as critical-thinking aids rather than model explanations.
- [DeepSeek-V2: A Strong, Economical, and Efficient Mixture-of-Experts Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.04434): Presents DeepSeek-V2, a 236B-parameter MoE with 21B active parameters, Multi-head Latent Attention for compressed KV cache, DeepSeekMoE sparse training, 128K context, and SFT/RL chat alignment.
- [Understanding the Capabilities and Limitations of Large Language Models for Cultural Commonsense](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.04655): Evaluates LLMs on general and culture-specific commonsense benchmarks, showing performance varies by culture, cultural context affects general commonsense, and query language changes outcomes.
- [ADELIE: Aligning Large Language Models on Information Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.05008): Aligns LLMs for information extraction with ADELIE, adapting generation to structured extraction targets.
- [LLMs with Personalities in Multi-issue Negotiation Games](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.05248): Studies personality-conditioned LLM behavior in multi-issue negotiation games, linking persona settings to negotiation outcomes.
- [You Only Cache Once: Decoder-Decoder Architectures for Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.05254): Introduces a decoder-decoder language-model architecture that caches once, reducing repeated KV-cache work during generation.
- [Fishing for Magikarp: Automatically Detecting Under-trained Tokens in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.05417): Detects under-trained tokens in LLM vocabularies, identifying Magikarp-style tokens whose embeddings or behavior remain poorly learned.
- [Vidur: A Large-Scale Simulation Framework For LLM Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.05465): Provides a high-fidelity LLM inference simulator and Vidur-Search for deployment-configuration search, modeling latency under batching, scheduling, and parallelization choices.
- [Large Language Model-Aided Evolutionary Search for Constrained Multiobjective Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.05767): Uses a fine-tuned or prompted LLM as an evolutionary search operator, conditioning on objective values and constraint violations to speed constrained multiobjective optimization.
- [Experimental Pragmatics with Machines: Testing LLM Predictions for the Inferences of Plain and Embedded Disjunctions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.05776): Compares LLM predictions with human experimental-pragmatics paradigms for plain and embedded disjunction inferences, contrasting them with scalar implicatures.
- [Lumina-T2X: Transforming Text into Any Modality, Resolution, and Duration via Flow-based Large Diffusion Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.05945): Uses flow-based large diffusion Transformers to generate multiple modalities from text while varying output resolution and duration.
- [CuMo: Scaling Multimodal LLM with Co-Upcycled Mixture-of-Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.05949): Co-upcycles both the vision encoder and MLP connector into top-k MoE components, balancing experts to scale multimodal LLM capacity on open VQA benchmarks.
- [LLMs for XAI: Future Directions for Explaining Explanations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.06064): Uses LLMs to turn existing XAI algorithm outputs into human-readable narratives, then outlines evaluation, prompting, model-comparison, training, and user-study directions.
- [Transforming the bootstrap: using transformers to compute scattering amplitudes in planar N=4 super Yang–Mills theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.06107): Trains Transformers on language-like scattering-amplitude representations to predict integer coefficients for planar N = 4 super Yang-Mills theory with over 98% accuracy.
- [Learning Latent Dynamic Robust Representations for World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.06263): Adds spatio-temporal masking, bisimulation, latent reconstruction, and a hybrid recurrent state-space model to make visual model-based RL world models robust to irrelevant observation noise.
- [Pruning as a Domain-specific LLM Extractor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.06275): Introduces D-Pruner, a dual unstructured pruning method that preserves general and domain-specific weights for smaller healthcare and legal domain LLMs.
- [Linearizing Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.06640): Uptrains pretrained Transformers into recurrent attention or linear RNN variants at about 5% of pretraining cost, while noting remaining in-context learning and long-context gaps.
- [Word2World: Generating Stories and Worlds through Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.06686): Builds an LLM system that writes game stories, extracts narrative structure, and places tiles to generate playable worlds without task-specific fine-tuning.
- [Aladdin: Joint Placement and Scaling for SLO-Aware LLM Serving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.06856): Predicts query resource needs and jointly chooses placement, worker configuration, and scaling for SLO-aware LLM serving, reducing cluster cost under latency targets.
- [Overcoming systematic softening in universal machine learning interatomic potentials by fine-tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.07105): Fine-tunes universal interatomic-potential models to reduce systematic softening errors in predicted material dynamics.
- [Strategic Data Ordering: Enhancing Large Language Model Performance through Curriculum Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.07490): Orders instruction data by prompt length, attention scores, or loss values for Mistral-7B and Gemma-7B curriculum learning, yielding modest performance gains.
- [Reducing Risk for Assistive Reinforcement Learning Policies with Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.07603): Uses diffusion models to make risky assistive-robot RL policies safer in simulation without extra environment interaction.
- [Localizing Task Information for Improved Model Merging and Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.07813): Localizes task information inside models to guide model merging and compression, preserving task-relevant components.
- [Zero-Shot Tokenizer Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.07883): Transfers tokenizers zero-shot, adapting language models to new token vocabularies without full retraining.
- [MambaOut: Do We Really Need Mamba for Vision?*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.07992): Removes the SSM token mixer from Mamba-style vision blocks, finding it unnecessary for image classification but useful for long-sequence detection and segmentation.
- [Who's in and who's out? A case study of multimodal CLIP-filtering in DataComp](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.08209): Audits DataComp CommonPool CLIP filtering, showing it excludes demographic groups, amplifies underrepresentation, and still admits NSFW or copyrighted image-text pairs.
- [TFWT: Tabular Feature Weighting with Transformer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.08403): Introduces Transformer Feature Weighting for tabular data, learning sample-specific feature importance and using reinforcement-learning fine-tuning on real-world datasets.
- [Understanding the performance gap between online and offline alignment algorithms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.08448): Studies reward-overoptimization regimes and traces online RLHF advantages over offline alignment to the mismatch between pairwise classification and generation objectives.
- [Is Your LLM Outdated? A Deep Look at Temporal Generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.08460): Introduces FreshBench for fresh text and event prediction, measuring temporal bias, future-performance degradation, and adaptation gaps between open and closed LLMs.
- [Risks and Opportunities of Open-Source Generative AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.08597): Frames open-source generative AI policy with a three-stage risk and opportunity analysis, arguing for openness in models and data paired with risk management.
- [CLIP with Quality Captions: A Strong Pretraining for Vision Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.08911): Shows high-quality captions make CLIP pretraining stronger for dense prediction, improving semantic segmentation and depth estimation with far less image-text data.
- [LoRA Learns Less and Forgets Less](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.09673): Shows LoRA learns less and forgets less, analyzing why low-rank adaptation preserves more pretrained behavior than full tuning.
- [Many-Shot In-Context Learning in Multimodal Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.09798): Evaluates GPT-4o and Gemini 1.5 Pro on 14 multimodal datasets with up to 2,000 in-context examples, including batching strategies with up to 50 queries.
- [Learning from Observer Gaze: Zero-Shot Attention Prediction Oriented by Human-Object Interaction Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.09931): Releases the IG gaze dataset with 530K fixations over 740 categories and trains interaction-oriented attention prediction models for HOI interpretability.
- [When Large Language Model Meets Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.10098): Surveys and tests how LLMs meet optimization tasks, covering model use as optimizers, heuristics, or solvers.
- [“Thinking” Fair and Slow: On the Efficacy of Structured Prompts for Debiasing Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.10431): Tests System-2-style Implicative Prompts across many LLMs and datasets, reducing mean social bias while keeping downstream task performance competitive.
- [Lean Attention: Hardware-Aware Scalable Attention Mechanism for the Decode-Phase of Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.10480): Parallelizes decode-phase self-attention over long contexts by treating online softmax as a reduction, reporting speedups over FlashAttention-2 at very long sequence lengths.
- [Language Models can Evaluate Themselves via Probability Discrepancy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.10516): Proposes ProbDiff, a self-evaluation score that compares an LLM's probability distribution on an initial answer against revised answers instead of using GPT-4 or a separate judge.
- [Layer-Condensed KV Cache for Efficient Inference of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.10637): Condenses KV cache across layers to reduce LLM inference memory while keeping useful attention history.
- [ActiveLLM: Large Language Model-based Active Learning for Textual Few-Shot Scenarios](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.10808): Uses strong LLMs to select the most informative few-shot text-classification examples, improving BERT-style classifiers and few-shot learners under cold-start annotation budgets.
- [The Future of Large Language Model Pre-training is Federated](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.10853): Introduces Photon, a reproducible federated pretraining system intended to let institutions jointly train LLMs while keeping local data and compute distributed.
- [Observational Scaling Laws and the Predictability of Language Model Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.10938): Fits scaling laws from roughly 100 public model checkpoints by modeling family-specific compute efficiency and a low-dimensional capability space rather than retraining models at many scales.
- [Learnable Privacy Neurons Localization in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.10989): Learns binary neuron masks that localize PII-sensitive neurons in LLMs, then tests whether deactivating those neurons reduces memorized private-information leakage.
- [OpenRLHF: An Easy-to-use, Scalable and High-performance RLHF Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.11143): Provides OpenRLHF, a scalable framework for supervised fine-tuning, reward modeling, and RLHF training of LLMs.
- [Towards Modular LLMs by Building and Reusing a Library of LoRAs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.11157): Builds reusable LoRA libraries with model-based clustering and routes new inputs through Arrow, a zero-shot adapter selector based on parameter-space similarity.
- [Automated Multi-level Preference for MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.11165): Replaces binary MLLM preferences with automated multi-level comparisons and cross-level hallucination examples, alongside MRHal-Bench for judging visual hallucination severity.
- [Uni-MoE: Scaling Unified Multimodal LLMs With Mixture of Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.11273): Scales a unified multimodal LLM with modality-specific encoders, connector-based shared representations, and sparse MoE routing for efficient multi-modality training and inference.
- [Smooth Kolmogorov Arnold networks enabling structural knowledge representation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.11318): Uses smooth Kolmogorov-Arnold networks to encode structural knowledge, improving interpretability of learned functional representations.
- [FIFO-Diffusion: Generating Infinite Videos from Text without Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.11473): Generates long or infinite text-to-video streams with FIFO diffusion, a training-free temporal generation procedure.
- [VR-GPT: Visual Language Model for Intelligent Virtual Reality Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.11537): Embeds a custom VLM in a Unity VR system with speech input and output so users can receive natural-language guidance during immersive tasks without text overlays.
- [Sociotechnical Implications of Generative Artificial Intelligence for Information Access](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.11612): Surveys systemic risks and mitigation questions for using generative AI in information access, so this row is better treated as a Survey migration candidate.
- [Decoding by Contrasting Knowledge: Enhancing LLMs' Confidence on Edited Facts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.11613): Proposes DeCK, a decoding method that contrasts distributions from old and edited in-context knowledge to overcome stubborn pretrained facts during knowledge editing.
- [Era3D: High-Resolution Multiview Diffusion using Efficient Row-wise Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.11616): Generates high-resolution multiview images from one view by predicting camera parameters and using row-wise attention to avoid dense multiview attention costs.
- [TinyLLaVA Factory: A Modularized Codebase for Small-scale Large Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.11788): Provides a modular factory codebase for small LMMs, with interchangeable vision, connector, language, and training-recipe components for reproducible TinyLLaVA-style models.
- [xFinder: Robust and Pinpoint Answer Extraction for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.11874): Introduces xFinder for robust pinpoint answer extraction from LLM outputs, targeting evaluation parsing failures.
- [Multiple-Choice Questions are Efficient and Robust LLM Evaluators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.11966): Argues multiple-choice questions can be efficient robust LLM evaluators, analyzing their stability as an evaluation format.
- [STYLE: Improving Domain Transferability of Asking Clarification Questions in Large Language Model Powered Conversational Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.12059): Introduces STYLE, a clarification-question strategy that transfers LLM-powered conversational search policies across unseen domains; it is an Agent Harness migration candidate.
- [Imp: Highly Capable Large Multimodal Models for Mobile Devices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.12107): Studies lightweight LMM design choices across architecture, training data, and training strategy, then releases Imp models targeted at mobile-scale multimodal understanding.
- [MoRA: High-Rank Updating for Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.12130): Introduces MoRA, a high-rank parameter-efficient update method designed to overcome LoRA rank limits.
- [Self-HWDebug: Automation of LLM Self-Instructing for Hardware Security Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.12347): Uses known hardware CWE bugs and mitigations to make LLMs synthesize debugging instructions for RTL security verification, so the row likely belongs with agent or security workflows.
- [Diffusion for World Modeling: Visual Details Matter in Atari](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.12399): Studies diffusion-based world modeling in Atari, relevant to model-side environment prediction.
- [Wav-KAN: Wavelet Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.12832): Introduces Wav-KAN, an innovative neural network architecture that leverages the Wavelet Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks (Wav-KAN) framework to enhance interpretability and performance.
- [Reducing Transformer Key-Value Cache Size with Cross-Layer Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.12981): Extends multi-query and grouped-query attention by sharing key-value heads across adjacent layers, further reducing autoregressive KV-cache memory with limited accuracy loss.
- [DuetSim: Building User Simulator with Dual Large Language Models for Task-Oriented Dialogues](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.13028): Builds a task-oriented dialogue user simulator with one LLM generating user turns and a second LLM verifying goal consistency, making it an Agent Harness migration candidate.
- [MeteoRA: Multiple-tasks Embedded LoRA for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.13053): Embeds multiple task LoRA modules in MeteoRA, enabling shared LLM adaptation across tasks.
- [Dataset Decomposition: Faster LLM Training with Variable Sequence Length Curriculum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.13226): Speeds LLM training with dataset decomposition and variable sequence-length curriculum, ordering examples by length and difficulty.
- [Understanding Virtual Nodes: Oversmoothing, Oversquashing, and Node Heterogeneity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.13526): Analyzes virtual nodes through oversmoothing, oversquashing, and node-heterogeneity effects.
- [Dense Connector for MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.13800): Connects MLLMs to multi-layer visual encoder features through Dense Connector and Efficient Dense Connector, improving visual signal use while reducing visual token count.
- [LOGIN: A Large Language Model Consulted Graph Neural Network Training Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.13902): Treats LLMs as consultants for GNN training by prompting over semantic and topological node context, then refining GNNs with LLM-derived complementary signals.
- [Model Editing as a Robust and Denoised variant of DPO: A Case Study on Toxicity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.13967): Introduces ProFS, a tuning-free projection filter that identifies and removes toxic subspaces from model parameters as a sample-efficient alternative to DPO-style alignment.
- [DirectMultiStep: Direct Route Generation for Multi-Step Retrosynthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.13983): Uses transformer MoE models to generate full multistep retrosynthesis routes as one conditional string, avoiding iterative single-step CASP search expansion.
- [Sigmoid Gating is More Sample Efficient than Softmax Gating in Mixture of Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.13997): Compares sigmoid and softmax MoE gating, showing sigmoid gating can be more sample-efficient for sparse experts.
- [MagicPose4D: Crafting Articulated Models with Appearance and Motion Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14017): Generates controllable 4D articulated assets by taking monocular videos or mesh sequences as motion prompts and combining dual-phase reconstruction with appearance control.
- [Large Language Models Can Self-Correct with Minimal Effort](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14092): Tests minimal-effort self-correction in LLMs, examining when lightweight revision prompts actually improve answers.
- [Statistical Advantages of Perturbing Cosine Router in Mixture of Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14131): Analyzes cosine-router MoE estimation and proposes parameter perturbation to improve convergence rates and reduce representation collapse in sparse expert models.
- [Unveiling the Tapestry of Consistency in Large Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14156): Introduces ConBench to probe LVLM answer consistency across different prompt solution spaces, revealing failures as option spaces grow or shift between discriminative and generative forms.
- [DiM: Diffusion Mamba for Efficient High-Resolution Image Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14224): Proposes Diffusion Mamba (DiM), which combines the efficiency of Mamba, a sequence model based on State Space Models (SSM), with the expressive power of diffusion models for efficient high-resolution image synthesis.
- [Dynamic Mixture of Experts: An Auto-Tuning Approach for Efficient Transformer Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14297): Introduces DynMoE, where tokens choose how many experts to activate and training adaptively adjusts expert count across vision, language, and vision-language tasks.
- [Instruction Tuning With Loss Over Instructions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14394): Proposes Instruction Modelling, applying loss to the instruction and prompt tokens during tuning, and shows gains across 21 benchmarks especially with long instructions, short outputs, or low-resource tuning.
- [Mitigating Quantization Errors Due to Activation Spikes in GLU-Based LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14428): Based on our observations, we propose two empirical methods, Quantization-free Module (QFeM) and Quantization-free Prefix (QFeP), to isolate the activation spikes during quantization.
- [Unchosen Experts Can Contribute Too: Unleashing MoE Models' Power by Self-Contrast](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14507): Motivated by these findings, we propose Self-Contrast Mixture-of-Experts (SCMoE), a training-free strategy that utilizes unchosen experts in a self-contrast manner during inference.
- [SearchLVLMs: A Plug-and-Play Framework for Augmenting Large Vision-Language Models by Searching Up-to-Date Internet Knowledge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14554): Proposes a plug-and-play framework, for augmenting existing LVLMs in handling visual question answering (VQA) about up-to-date knowledge, dubbed SearchLVLMs.
- [Surge Phenomenon in Optimal Learning Rate and Batch Size Scaling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14578): Analyzes a surge phenomenon in optimal learning-rate and batch-size scaling, refining training hyperparameter scaling rules.
- [Calibrated Self-Rewarding Vision Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14622): Calibrates self-generated LVLM preference data so preference optimization reflects the target model's own image-text grounding failures rather than external scorer artifacts.
- [Multi-turn Reinforcement Learning from Preference Human Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14655): Extends RLHF from single responses to full multi-turn conversations, with mirror-descent policy optimization and an Education Dialogue environment for long-horizon preference feedback.
- [Sparse-Tuning: Adapting Vision Transformers with Efficient Fine-tuning and Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14700): Combines token sparsification with dense adapters so ViT fine-tuning reduces image and video token redundancy while compensating for information lost at inference.
- [SimPO: Simple Preference Optimization with a Reference-Free Reward](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14734): Introduces SimPO, a reference-free preference optimizer that uses average sequence log probability as the implicit reward plus a target reward margin, outperforming DPO variants on AlpacaEval 2 and Arena-Hard.
- [Mamba-Reg: Vision Mamba Also Needs Registers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14858): To better cope with Mamba blocks' uni-directional inference paradigm, two key modifications are introduced: 1) evenly inserting registers throughout the input token sequence, and 2) recycling registers for final decision predictions.
- [BiMix: A Bivariate Data Mixing Law for Language Model Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14908): Models LLM pretraining loss as a function of both domain mixture proportions and data volume, enabling data-mixture optimization and extrapolation to unseen mixtures.
- [Extracting Prompts by Inverting LLM Outputs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15012): Studies prompt extraction by inverting LLM outputs, treating outputs as evidence for recovering hidden prompts.
- [Grokked Transformers are Implicit Reasoners: A Mechanistic Journey to the Edge of Generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15071): Mechanistically analyzes grokked Transformers as implicit reasoners near the edge of generalization.
- [OptLLM: Optimal Assignment of Queries to Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15130): Predicts per-query model performance with uncertainty estimates and searches Pareto choices that trade accuracy against cost across multiple hosted LLMs.
- [Intelligent Go-Explore: Standing on the Shoulders of Giant Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15143): Replaces Go-Explore's handcrafted state-selection heuristics with foundation-model judgments of interesting states and candidate actions for hard-exploration RL.
- [VB-LoRA: Extreme Parameter Efficient Fine-Tuning with Vector Banks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15179): Shares a global vector bank across LoRA matrices and composes low-rank updates with differentiable top-k admixture, reducing stored adapter parameters for many tasks or users.
- [Extracting Heuristics from Large Language Models for Reward Shaping in Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15194): Extracts heuristics from LLMs for reinforcement-learning reward shaping, using language-model suggestions as auxiliary rewards.
- [Prompt Tuning Strikes Back: Customizing Foundation Models with Low-Rank Prompt Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15282): To address this gap, we propose Low-Rank Prompt Adaptation (LoPA), a prompt-tuning-based approach that performs on par with state-of-the-art PEFT methods and full fine-tuning while being more parameter-efficient and not requiring a server-based adapter.
- [Stacking Your Transformers: A Closer Look at Model Growth for Efficient LLM Pre-Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15319): Standardizes LLM model-growth operators and finds depthwise stacking scalable up to 7B models, matching a 300B-token training loss with 194B tokens and providing timing and growth-factor guidelines.
- [Distinguish Any Fake Videos: Unleashing the Power of Large-scale Data and Motion Features](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15343): Builds GenVidDet with 2.66M real and generated videos and trains motion-aware detectors for AI-generated video authenticity, making it primarily a Bench candidate.
- [Sparse Matrix in Large Language Model Fine-tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15525): Uses sparse matrices in LLM fine-tuning to reduce adaptation cost while retaining task-specific update capacity.
- [Inverse-RLignment: Inverse Reinforcement Learning from Demonstrations for LLM Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15624): Frames alignment from demonstrations as inverse reinforcement learning and optimizes a tailored reward model when preference labels are noisy, expensive, or unavailable.
- [ConvLLaVA: Hierarchical Backbones as Visual Encoder for Large Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15738): Replaces ViT visual encoders in LMMs with ConvNeXt-style hierarchical backbones, improving high-resolution multimodal understanding with multi-scale visual features.
- [Transformers represent belief state geometry in their residual stream](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15943): Shows that Transformer residual streams linearly represent belief-state geometry, including nontrivial fractal structures from hidden-state dynamics in the data source.
- [C3LLM: Conditional Multimodal Content Generation Using Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16136): Uses LLMs for conditional multimodal content generation, connecting text instructions with multimodal generation controls.
- [Mixture of In-Context Prompters for Tabular PFNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16156): Proposes MIXTUREPFN, combining nearest-neighbor in-context sampling with bootstrap fine-tuning on inference-time tabular data for prior-data-fitted networks.
- [Evolutionary Large Language Model for Automated Feature Transformation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16203): Combines an RL-collected feature-transformation database, evolutionary maintenance, and few-shot LLM generation to search feature operations for downstream tabular models.
- [No Two Devils Alike: Unveiling Distinct Mechanisms of Fine-tuning Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16229): Uses logit lens, activation patching, and cross-model probing to show that fine-tuning attacks alter refusal behavior through distinct internal mechanisms.
- [ConStat: Performance-Based Contamination Detection in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16281): Defines contamination as inflated, non-generalizing benchmark performance and detects it from model behavior rather than direct training-data inspection.
- [Training Tensor Attention Efficiently: From Cubic to Almost Linear Time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16411): Derives an almost-linear-time backward pass for tensor attention using closed-form gradients, polynomial approximation, and tensor algebra.
- [The Importance of Directional Feedback for LLM-based Optimizers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16434): Shows that LLM optimizers improve more reliably with directional natural-language feedback, then synthesizes such feedback from optimization traces for tasks from functions to prompts and poems.
- [Provably Mitigating Overoptimization in RLHF: Your SFT Loss is Implicitly an Adversarial Regularizer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16436): Shows SFT loss can act as an adversarial regularizer in RLHF and derives a policy objective that penalizes reward-model overoptimization.
- [On the Algorithmic Bias of Aligning Large Language Models with RLHF: Preference Collapse and Matching Regularization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16455): Identifies preference collapse in standard RLHF and proposes preference-matching regularization to align policies with the reward model's full preference distribution.
- [Cost-Effective Online Multi-LLM Selection with Versatile Reward Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16587): Formulates multi-LLM routing as a cost-aware combinatorial bandit with task-specific reward models for selecting model subsets under heterogeneous pricing.
- [Let Silence Speak: Enhancing Fake News Detection with Generated Comments from Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16631): Generates missing user comments with LLMs to strengthen fake-news detection, adding synthetic social context to classifiers.
- [Limits of Deep Learning: Sequence Modeling through the Lens of Complexity Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16674): Proves limits of one-layer SSMs and finite-precision sequence models on function composition, then tests SSMs and Transformers on multiplication, dynamic programming, and puzzles.
- [Triple Preference Optimization: Achieving Better Alignment using a Single Step Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16681): Proposes TPO, a one-step preference objective intended to preserve both reasoning and instruction following under noisy or small preference datasets.
- [gzip Predicts Data-dependent Scaling Laws](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16684): Proposes a new data-dependent scaling law for LM's that accounts for the training data's gzip-compressibility; its compute-optimal frontier increases in dataset size preference (over parameter count preference) as training data becomes harder to compress.
- [Zamba: A Compact 7B SSM Hybrid Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16712): Combines state-space layers with Transformer attention in a compact 7B hybrid language model for efficient long-context modeling.
- [Entity Alignment with Noisy Annotations from Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16806): Proposes LLM4EA, which uses active selection and noise-aware handling of LLM-generated annotations to align entities across large real-world knowledge graphs.
- [Can We Trust LLMs? Mitigate Overconfidence Bias in LLMs through Knowledge Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16856): Transfers chain-of-thought rationales from larger to smaller LLMs so fine-tuned students improve answer accuracy and confidence calibration.
- [Tokenization Matters! Degrading Large Language Models through Challenging Their Tokenization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17067): To demonstrate this flaw of LLMs, we construct an adversarial dataset, named as ADT (Adversarial Dataset for Tokenizer), which draws upon the vocabularies of various open-source LLMs to challenge LLMs' tokenization.
- [On the Noise Robustness of In-Context Learning for Text Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17264): To circumvent the issue, we propose a simple and effective approach called Local Perplexity Ranking (LPR), which replaces the "noisy" candidates with their nearest neighbors that are more likely to be clean.
- [DoRA: Enhancing Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning with Dynamic Rank Distribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17357): To address this issue, we introduce the Dynamic Low-Rank Adaptation (DoRA) method.
- [Matryoshka Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17430): Inspired by the concept of Matryoshka Dolls, we propose M3: Matryoshka Multimodal Models, which learns to represent visual content as nested sets of visual tokens that capture information across multiple coarse-to-fine granularities.
- [EMR-Merging: Tuning-Free High-Performance Model Merging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17461): Merges models without tuning through EMR-Merging, aiming for high-performance parameter combination without additional training.
- [ClassDiffusion: More Aligned Personalization Tuning with Explicit Class Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17532): Adds semantic-preservation loss to text-to-image personalization tuning so customized concepts retain superclass compositional behavior across prompts.
- [LoRA-XS: Low-Rank Adaptation with Extremely Small Number of Parameters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17604): To address this, we introduce LoRA-XS, a novel fine-tuning method backed by a theoretical derivation.
- [Do Egocentric Video-Language Models Truly Understand Hand-Object Interactions?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17719): Introduces EgoHOIBench and EgoNCE++ to test and improve whether egocentric video-language models distinguish fine-grained hand-object verbs and nouns.
- [LoRA-Switch: Boosting the Efficiency of Dynamic LLM Adapters via System-Algorithm Co-design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17741): Co-designs LoRA-Switch at system and algorithm levels to make dynamic LLM adapter switching more efficient.
- [XL3M: A Training-free Framework for LLM Length Extension Based on Segment-wise Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17755): Based on this, we propose an efficient training free framework, named XL3M (it means extra-long large language model), which enables the LLMs trained on short sequences to reason extremely long sequence without any further training or fine-tuning.
- [Don't Miss the Forest for the Trees: Attentional Vision Calibration for Large Vision Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17820): Calibrates LVLM visual attention so models attend to image evidence rather than over-weighting language priors.
- [RITUAL: Random Image Transformations as a Universal Anti-hallucination Lever in LVLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17821): Uses random image transformations as RITUAL, a training-free anti-hallucination lever for LVLMs.
- [Seeing the Image: Prioritizing Visual Correlation by Contrastive Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17871): Reweights VLM training tokens by visual correlation estimated from contrastive image logits, reducing text tokens that are weakly grounded or contradictory to the image.
- [Self-Guiding Exploration for Combinatorial Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17950): To address this gap, we introduce a novel prompting strategy: Self-Guiding Exploration (SGE), designed to enhance the performance of solving CPs.
- [Knowledge Circuits in Pretrained Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17969): Traces how information heads, relation heads, and MLPs cooperate as knowledge circuits in GPT-2 and TinyLlama, then checks how editing perturbs those circuits.
- [Exploiting LLM Quantization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.18137): Reveals that widely used quantization methods can be exploited to produce a harmful quantized LLM, even though the full-precision counterpart appears benign, potentially tricking users into deploying the malicious quantized model.
- [FinerCut: Finer-grained Interpretable Layer Pruning for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.18218): To address these issues, we propose FinerCut, a new form of fine-grained layer pruning, which in contrast to prior work at the transformer block level, considers all self-attention and feed-forward network (FFN) layers within blocks as individual pruning.
- [Scaling Laws and Compute-Optimal Training Beyond Fixed Training Durations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.18392): Replaces fixed-duration cosine schedules with constant learning rate plus cooldowns, enabling cheaper scaling experiments and compute-optimal training analysis across durations.
- [Why are Visually-Grounded Language Models Bad at Image Classification?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.18415): Our analysis reveals that the primary cause is data-related: critical information for image classification is encoded in the VLM's latent space but can only be effectively decoded with enough training data.
- [ViG: Linear-complexity Visual Sequence Learning with Gated Linear Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.18425): Introduces ViG, using gated linear attention for linear-complexity visual sequence learning.
- [Inference-Time Alignment of Diffusion Models with Direct Noise Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.18881): Aligns diffusion models at inference time through direct noise optimization instead of retraining model weights.
- [Offline Regularised Reinforcement Learning for Large Language Models Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19107): Proposes Direct Reward Optimisation, aligning LLMs from single prompt-response-feedback trajectories instead of scarce pairwise preference quadruplets.
- [Robust Preference Optimization through Reward Model Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19316): Our results show that distilling from such a family of reward models leads to improved robustness to distribution shift in preference annotations, while preserving the simple supervised nature of DPO.
- [Value-Incentivized Preference Optimization: A Unified Approach to Online and Offline RLHF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19320): Introduces VPO, which regularizes learned preference rewards with value-function uncertainty so both online and offline RLHF can use optimism or pessimism under uncertainty.
- [MAP-Neo: Highly Capable and Transparent Bilingual Large Language Model Series](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19327): Releases MAP-Neo, a fully open bilingual 7B LLM trained from scratch on 4.5T tokens, including the cleaned corpus, data-cleaning pipeline, checkpoints, and training and evaluation framework.
- [Preference Learning Algorithms Do Not Learn Preference Rankings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19534): Shows DPO and RLHF policies often fail to rank preferred outputs above rejected ones, then analyzes the gap between objective optimization and observed ranking accuracy.
- [CLIPLoss and Norm-Based Data Selection Methods for Multimodal Contrastive Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19547): Proposes surrogate CLIPLoss and NormSim data-selection metrics for CLIP-style pretraining, selecting image-text pairs without training a new scoring model.
- [Quo Vadis ChatGPT? From Large Language Models to Large Knowledge Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19561): Argues for large knowledge models that combine LLMs with first-principles and technical knowledge for scientific and engineering reasoning.
- [SVFT: Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning with Singular Vectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19597): Fine-tunes pretrained models by updating singular vectors tied to each weight matrix, offering a PEFT alternative to generic low-rank adapter updates.
- [Detecting Hallucinations in Large Language Model Generation: A Token Probability Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19648): Detects hallucinations with supervised classifiers using only four token and vocabulary probability features from evaluator LLMs, reporting gains across three benchmarks with a reproducible lightweight setup.
- [Enhancing Large Vision Language Models with Self-Training on Image Comprehension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19716): Introduces STIC, where LVLMs self-construct preference data from unlabeled images and then improve image comprehension through self-training.
- [LLaMEA: A Large Language Model Evolutionary Algorithm for Automatically Generating Metaheuristics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20132): Uses an LLM-driven evolutionary algorithm, LLaMEA, to generate metaheuristics automatically.
- [XPrompt:Explaining Large Language Model's Generation via Joint Prompt Attribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20404): Introduces a counterfactual explanation framework based on Joint Prompt Attribution, JoPA, which aims to explain how a few prompt texts collaboratively influences the LLM's complete generation.
- [Mind the Inconspicuous: Revealing the Hidden Weakness in Aligned LLMs' Refusal Boundaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20653): Shows repeated end-of-sequence tokens shift harmful prompts toward refusal-boundary weaknesses and boost jailbreak success across many open LLMs.
- [Ovis: Structural Embedding Alignment for Multimodal Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20797): Aligns MLLM visual and textual embeddings by adding a learnable visual embedding table whose indexed combinations mirror token embedding lookup, improving multimodal fusion across benchmarks.
- [Self-Augmented Preference Optimization: Off-Policy Paradigms for Language Model Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20830): Introduces SAPO, an off-policy preference-optimization paradigm that builds self-augmented preference data instead of requiring preexisting paired comparisons.
- [Large Language Models are Zero-Shot Next Location Predictors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20962): Tests more than 15 LLMs on three mobility datasets and finds zero-shot prompting can outperform specialized next-location predictors in some settings.
- [SaySelf: Teaching LLMs to Express Confidence with Self-Reflective Rationales](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20974): Trains LLMs to output calibrated fine-grained confidence and self-reflective rationales by summarizing uncertainty from inconsistent sampled reasoning chains and refining confidence with RL rewards.
- [Code Pretraining Improves Entity Tracking Abilities of Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.21068): Compares base models with code-continued variants and finds code pretraining, unlike math or alignment tuning, consistently improves natural-language entity tracking.
- [What Makes CLIP More Robust to Long-Tailed Pre-Training Data? A Controlled Study for Transferable Insights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.21070): Uses controlled long-tailed pretraining experiments to explain why CLIP-style contrastive learning transfers more robustly than supervised learning under class imbalance.
- [Expert-Token Resonance MoE: Bidirectional Routing with Efficiency Affinity-Driven Active Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.00023): Introduces expert-token resonance routing for MoE, using bidirectional affinity between tokens and experts for active selection.
- [SCALM: Towards Semantic Caching for Automated Chat Services with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.00025): Designs a semantic cache for LLM chat services that identifies reusable response patterns and cache entries beyond exact string matching.
- [Large Language Model Pruning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.00030): Surveys and systematizes LLM pruning methods, covering structured and unstructured compression for large language models.
- [QuanTA: Efficient High-Rank Fine-Tuning of LLMs with Quantum-Informed Tensor Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.00132): Proposes Quantum-informed Tensor Adaptation (QuanTA), a novel, easy-to-implement, fine-tuning method with no inference overhead for large-scale pre-trained language models.
- [μLO: Compute-Efficient Meta-Generalization of Learned Optimizers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.00153): Introduces micro learned optimizers for compute-efficient meta-generalization, reducing the cost of learned-optimizer transfer.
- [LLM-RankFusion: Mitigating Intrinsic Inconsistency in LLM-based Ranking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.00231): Proposes LLM-RankFusion, an LLM-based ranking framework that mitigates these inconsistencies and produces a robust ranking list.
- [Controlling Large Language Model Agents with Entropic Activation Steering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.00244): Introduces EAST, an activation-steering method that changes in-context LLM agents' exploration behavior by modulating uncertainty in their internal representations.
- [HonestLLM: Toward an Honest and Helpful Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.00380): Builds HonestLLM to improve honesty and helpfulness, targeting calibrated truthful response behavior.
- [Automatic Instruction Evolving for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.00770): Proposes Auto Evol-Instruct, which has LLMs design, critique, and iteratively improve instruction-evolution strategies without human-written evolution rules.
- [Is In-Context Learning in Large Language Models Bayesian? A Martingale Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.00793): Shows that the martingale property is a necessary condition for unambiguous predictions in such scenarios, and enables a principled, decomposed notion of uncertainty vital in trustworthy, safety-critical systems.
- [Envisioning Outlier Exposure by Large Language Models for Out-of-Distribution Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.00806): Uses LLMs to envision outlier exposure examples for out-of-distribution detection, generating auxiliary OOD signals.
- [BoNBoN Alignment for Large Language Models and the Sweetness of Best-of-n Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.00832): Analyzes best-of-n sampling as an alignment distribution and derives BoNBoN distillation to approximate best-of-n behavior without drawing many samples at inference.
- [Aligning Language Models with Demonstrated Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.00888): Converts expert demonstrations into iterative pairwise preference comparisons against LLM samples, including checkpoint-to-checkpoint comparisons for alignment.
- [Dragonfly: Multi-Resolution Zoom Supercharges Large Visual-Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.00977): Adds multi-resolution zoom visual encoding to LVLMs so fixed-resolution ViTs can preserve both global context and fine image details.
- [Probing Language Models for Pre-training Data Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01333): Detects whether target texts appeared in pretraining by probing internal activations and introduces ArxivMIA as a contamination-detection benchmark.
- [D-CPT Law: Domain-specific Continual Pre-Training Scaling Law for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01375): Derives D-CPT scaling laws for domain-specific continual pretraining, estimating how data and compute affect adaptation.
- [Sparsity-Accelerated Training for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01392): Accelerates LLM training with sparsity, using sparse computation to reduce pretraining or fine-tuning cost.
- [The Importance of Online Data: Understanding Preference Fine-tuning via Coverage](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01462): Proves that a global coverage condition is both necessary and sufficient for offline contrastive methods to converge to the optimal policy, but a weaker partial coverage condition suffices for online RL methods.
- [The Geometry of Categorical and Hierarchical Concepts in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01506): Studies whether categorical and hierarchical concepts form linear directions and structured geometry in LLM representation spaces.
- [Demystifying AI Platform Design for Distributed Inference of Next-Generation LLM models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01698): Analyzes distributed inference platform design for next-generation LLMs, focusing on serving architecture rather than a new model.
- [DuQuant: Distributing Outliers via Dual Transformation Makes Stronger Quantized LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01721): Introduces DuQuant, distributing activation outliers through dual transformations to improve quantized LLM accuracy.
- [Eliciting the Priors of Large Language Models using Iterated In-Context Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01860): Uses iterated in-context learning as a Markov-chain workflow to sample and study implicit Bayesian priors from LLMs across causal and everyday prediction tasks.
- [Plug-and-Play Diffusion Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01954): Trains a lightweight external guide while freezing a text-to-image diffusion model, reducing classifier-free guidance computation and transferring to fine-tuned variants.
- [FedMKT: Federated Mutual Knowledge Transfer for Large and Small Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.02224): Proposes parameter-efficient federated mutual knowledge transfer between large and small language models while keeping training distributed.
- [Retaining Key Information under High Compression Ratios: Query-Guided Compressor for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.02376): Proposes QGC, a query-guided dynamic context compressor that preserves answer-relevant information at high compression ratios for QA tasks.
- [Towards Efficient Mixture of Experts: A Holistic Study of Compression Techniques](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.02500): Studies MoE compression and proposes aggressive layer, block, and expert-level removal strategies to reduce sparse-model parameter and communication overhead.
- [Scalable MatMul-free Language Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.02528): Builds scalable MatMul-free language models, replacing dense matrix multiplications to reduce compute.
- [Mitigate Position Bias in Large Language Models via Scaling a Single Dimension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.02536): Links lost-in-the-middle behavior to positional hidden states and mitigates it by scaling a single hidden dimension tied to attention-position bias.
- [Adaptive Layer Splitting for Wireless LLM Inference in Edge Computing: A Model-Based Reinforcement Learning Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.02616): Uses model-based reinforcement learning to choose LLM layer split points between edge servers and user equipment for wireless inference.
- [Block Transformer: Global-to-Local Language Modeling for Fast Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.02657): Adds hierarchical global-to-local modeling to autoregressive Transformers to reduce self-attention inference bottlenecks.
- [Scaling Laws for Reward Model Overoptimization in Direct Alignment Algorithms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.02900): Derives scaling behavior for reward-model overoptimization in direct alignment algorithms, linking reward-model quality to policy degradation.
- [Pruner-Zero: Evolving Symbolic Pruning Metric from scratch for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.02924): Uses genetic programming to evolve symbolic post-training pruning metrics for LLMs, reducing dependence on hand-designed pruning heuristics and retraining.
- [Understanding the Impact of Negative Prompts: When and How Do They Take Effect?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.02965): Analyzes negative prompts in diffusion generation, finding delayed effects and latent-space neutralization mechanisms behind concept removal.
- [Large Language Models as Evaluators for Recommendation Explanations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.03248): Evaluates whether LLM judges can assess recommendation explanations using instruction-following and commonsense reasoning signals.
- [SpikeLM: Towards General Spike-Driven Language Modeling via Elastic Bi-Spiking Mechanisms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.03287): Introduces elastic bi-spiking mechanisms so spiking language models can handle both discriminative and generative language tasks with richer binary-event encodings.
- [Wings: Learning Multimodal LLMs without Text-only Forgetting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.03496): Presents Wings, a novel MLLM that excels in both text-only dialogues and multimodal comprehension.
- [LLMEmbed: Rethinking Lightweight LLM's Genuine Function in Text Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.03725): Uses lightweight LLMs as transferable embedding generators for text classification rather than as direct instruction-following classifiers.
- [Credit Card Fraud Detection Using Advanced Transformer Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.03733): Applies an advanced Transformer model to credit-card fraud detection; low-confidence here because it is a narrow domain application.
- [Light-PEFT: Lightening Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning via Early Pruning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.03792): Introduces masked foundation-model early pruning and multi-granularity PEFT pruning to reduce task-specific fine-tuning cost.
- [Improving Alignment and Robustness with Circuit Breakers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.04313): Uses representation-engineering circuit breakers to interrupt harmful generations during decoding, improving alignment robustness beyond refusal training.
- [Simplified and Generalized Masked Diffusion for Discrete Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.04329): Generalizes masked diffusion for discrete data with a simpler training and sampling formulation applicable beyond continuous diffusion settings.
- [PaCE: Parsimonious Concept Engineering for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.04331): Introduces Parsimonious Concept Engineering, an activation-engineering alignment method that steers LLM behavior with compact concept directions.
- [DeepStack: Deeply Stacking Visual Tokens is Surprisingly Simple and Effective for LMMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.04334): Stacks visual tokens from multiple encoder layers into LMM inputs, improving multimodal reasoning with a simple visual-feature aggregation architecture.
- [MoE Jetpack: From Dense Checkpoints to Adaptive Mixture of Experts for Vision Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.04801): Converts dense vision checkpoints into adaptive MoE models through fine-tuning, reusing pretrained weights while adding sparse expert capacity.
- [Do Language Models Exhibit Human-like Structural Priming Effects?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.04847): Shows that these effects can be explained via the inverse frequency effect, known in human priming, where rarer elements within a prime increase priming effects, as well as lexical dependence between prime and target.
- [Towards Semantic Equivalence of Tokenization in Multimodal LLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.05127): To address this, this paper proposes a novel dynamic Semantic-Equivalent Vision Tokenizer (SeTok), which groups visual features into semantic units via a dynamic clustering algorithm, flexibly determining the number of tokens based on image complexity.
- [An Empirical Study on Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning for MultiModal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.05130): Empirically compares parameter-efficient fine-tuning methods for multimodal LLMs across tasks and adapter choices.
- [The Factorization Curse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.05183): Diagnoses a next-token objective failure behind reversal-style knowledge retrieval errors. Core idea: show that learning a joint distribution under one token factorization does not imply reliable retrieval under another, then test factorization-agnostic objectives as a route toward more robust knowledge storage and planning.
- [DiffusionPID: Interpreting Diffusion via Partial Information Decomposition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.05191): Applies partial information decomposition to diffusion models, separating redundant, unique, and synergistic information during denoising.
- [CorDA: Context-Oriented Decomposition Adaptation of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.05223): Introduces CorDA, decomposing context-oriented weights so LLM adaptation can preserve world knowledge while learning new tasks.
- [Improving Logits-based Detector without Logits from Black-box LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.05232): Reconstructs logits-like signals from black-box LLM outputs so logits-based generated-text detectors can operate without API logit access.
- [Federated LoRA with Sparse Communication](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.05233): Combines LoRA adaptation with sparse federated communication so clients can fine-tune language models while exchanging fewer adapter updates.
- [Exploring Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning of Large Language Model on Automated Program Repair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.05639): Compares PEFT strategies for adapting LLMs to automated program repair, measuring repair performance under lightweight fine-tuning.
- [F-LMM: Grounding Frozen Large Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.05821): Grounds frozen multimodal models by adding lightweight trainable components rather than updating the full vision-language backbone.
- [Are Large Language Models Actually Good at Text Style Transfer?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.05885): Benchmarks LLM text-style transfer and analyzes where prompting and model choice succeed or fail across style-control settings.
