- [Turbo Sparse: Achieving LLM SOTA Performance with Minimal Activated Parameters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.05955): Introduces dReLU and a sparsification data mixture so Mistral and Mixtral activate only 2.5B and 4.3B parameters per token while achieving 2-5x decoding speedups.
- [ShiftAddLLM: Accelerating Pretrained LLMs via Post-Training Multiplication-Less Reparameterization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.05981): Reparameterizes pretrained LLM weight matrices into binary matrices with group-wise scaling, replacing multiplications with shifts and adds plus automated bit allocation for low-latency inference.
- [MATES: Model-Aware Data Selection for Efficient Pretraining with Data Influence Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06046): Trains data influence models that track a language model's changing pretraining preferences, selecting web data dynamically instead of relying on static rules or a larger reference model.
- [Verifiable Generation with Subsentence-Level Fine-Grained Citations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06125): Fine-tunes citation-aware generators to attach sources to subsentence spans, making support boundaries more precise than sentence-level citation methods.
- [Language Models Resist Alignment: Evidence From Data Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06144): Uses compression behavior to test whether alignment fine-tuning changes a model's underlying distribution or mainly adds a superficial behavioral layer.
- [PowerInfer-2: Fast Large Language Model Inference on a Smartphone](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06282): Extends smartphone LLM serving with activation sparsity, heterogeneous CPU-GPU-NPU execution, and memory-aware scheduling for models larger than device memory.
- [Self-Tuning: Instructing LLMs to Effectively Acquire New Knowledge through Self-Teaching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06326): Converts new documents into self-teaching instruction data so LLMs explain, question, and rehearse fresh knowledge rather than only continue pretraining on raw text.
- [Can Language Models Serve as Text-Based World Simulators?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06485): Evaluates language models as text-world transition models that predict how actions update symbolic states, probing whether they can replace hand-coded simulators for planning tasks.
- [Skywork-MoE: A Deep Dive into Training Techniques for Mixture-of-Experts Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06563): Reports the Skywork-MoE 146B training recipe, comparing dense-checkpoint upcycling with scratch training and detailing routing, expert, and stability choices.
- [SwitchLoRA: Switched Low-Rank Adaptation Can Learn Full-Rank Information](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06564): Rotates and switches LoRA subspaces during training so low-rank adapters can accumulate full-rank information with lower memory and communication cost.
- [Break the Chain: Large Language Models Can be Shortcut Reasoners](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06580): Tests when chain-of-thought is unnecessary and promotes shorter human-like shortcut reasoning traces for logical and commonsense tasks.
- [Instruct Large Language Models to Drive like Humans](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07296): Adapts LLMs to autonomous-driving motion planning by teaching driving logic and trajectory decisions from human-like scenario instructions.
- [TextGrad: Automatic "Differentiation" via Text](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07496): Treats LLM feedback as textual gradients, enabling backpropagation-style optimization of prompts and modular language-model programs without numeric derivatives.
- [Beyond Model Collapse: Scaling Up with Synthesized Data Requires Reinforcement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07515): Shows synthetic-data scaling needs verification or reinforcement signals, because blindly adding generated samples can trigger model-collapse dynamics.
- [MAP: Low-compute Model Merging with Amortized Pareto Fronts via Quadratic Approximation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07529): Approximates task trade-off Pareto fronts for model merging with a quadratic surrogate, reducing the compute needed to choose merge weights.
- [Autoregressive Pretraining with Mamba in Vision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07537): Applies autoregressive visual pretraining to Mamba backbones, exploiting their recurrent structure to improve vision representations efficiently.
- [An Empirical Study of Mamba-based Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07887): Runs controlled comparisons of Mamba language models against Transformers, isolating scaling, optimization, and long-context behavior in selective state-space LMs.
- [Grounding Multimodal Large Language Models in Actions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07904): Unifies action grounding methods for MLLMs by mapping embodiment-specific action spaces into trainable interfaces over frozen multimodal knowledge.
- [Large Language Model Unlearning via Embedding-Corrupted Prompts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07933): Performs unlearning by optimizing embedding-corrupted prompts that suppress targeted knowledge while reducing collateral damage to retained facts.
- [VisionLLM v2: An End-to-End Generalist Multimodal Large Language Model for Hundreds of Vision-Language Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08394): Unifies visual perception, understanding, and generation in one end-to-end MLLM for hundreds of tasks beyond text-only visual question answering.
- [Discovering Preference Optimization Algorithms with and for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08414): Uses LLMs to search the space of preference-optimization losses, discovering alignment objectives beyond hand-designed DPO-style formulas.
- [Magpie: Alignment Data Synthesis from Scratch by Prompting Aligned LLMs with Nothing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08464): Generates instruction-response alignment data by prompting aligned chat models with empty or minimal inputs, avoiding seed prompts and private data.
- [What If We Recaption Billions of Web Images with LLaMA-3?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08478): Recaptions web-scale image-text pairs with LLaMA-3 to enrich noisy captions for vision-language and text-to-image model training.
- [Beyond LLaVA-HD: Diving into High-Resolution Large Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08487): Studies high-resolution LMM design and proposes better global-local image handling than simple patch slicing and uniform resizing.
- [HelpSteer2: Open-source dataset for training top-performing reward models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08673): Releases updated permissively licensed preference data for reward-model training, including attribute-style judgments for stronger aligned LLMs.
- [Mixture-of-Skills: Learning to Optimize Data Usage for Fine-Tuning Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08811): Learns fine-tuning data mixtures across heterogeneous skills so alignment training balances writing, reasoning, chat, and coding capabilities.
- [Cognitively Inspired Energy-Based World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08862): Replaces next-token or next-frame prediction with an energy-based world model inspired by human predictive processing over latent state constraints.
- [Delta-CoMe: Training-Free Delta-Compression with Mixed-Precision for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08903): Compresses fine-tuned delta weights with mixed precision without retraining, supporting cheaper multi-tenant deployment of many adapted LLMs.
- [Autonomous Multiobjective Optimization Using Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08987): Uses an LLM to autonomously design and adapt evolutionary search strategies for unseen multi-objective optimization problems.
- [ME-Switch: A Memory-Efficient Expert Switching Framework for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09041): Decomposes expert deltas and switches task experts on demand, reducing memory and I/O costs when serving many fine-tuned LLM variants.
- [MMRel: Benchmarking Relation Understanding in Multi-Modal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09121): Provides large-scale relation data and diagnostics for MLLM inter-object relation understanding, making it closer to a benchmark or data row than a model method.
- [Towards Effective Evaluations and Comparisons for LLM Unlearning Methods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09179): Defines evaluation criteria for LLM unlearning that separate forgetting, retention, and robustness, exposing weaknesses in behavior-only comparisons.
- [C2HLSC: Can LLMs Bridge the Software-to-Hardware Design Gap?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09233): Tests iterative LLM refactoring of C programs into HLS-compatible hardware designs for randomness tests, sorting, and AES examples.
- [Unpacking DPO and PPO: Disentangling Best Practices for Learning from Preference Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09279): Separates data, algorithm, and evaluation factors in preference learning to compare DPO and PPO under controlled alignment settings.
- [Understanding Hallucinations in Diffusion Models through Mode Interpolation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09358): Identifies mode interpolation as a diffusion-model hallucination mechanism where samples blend nearby training modes into impossible images.
- [4M-21: An Any-to-Any Vision Model for Tens of Tasks and Modalities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09406): Scales 4M-style masked multimodal modeling to 21 modalities and many tasks, yielding a single any-to-any vision model.
- [Pandora: Towards General World Model with Natural Language Actions and Video States](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09455): Builds a world model that conditions on natural-language actions and video states to forecast future visual states for grounded long-horizon reasoning.
- [Talking Heads: Understanding Inter-layer Communication in Transformer Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09519): Analyzes attention heads that route and inhibit contextual items across Transformer layers, revealing reusable circuits for context retrieval.
- [Bootstrapping Language Models with DPO Implicit Rewards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09760): Reuses the implicit reward induced by a DPO-trained model to relabel samples and bootstrap further alignment without a separate reward model.
- [Nymeria: A Massive Collection of Multimodal Egocentric Daily Motion in the Wild](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.09905): Releases synchronized egocentric video, eye tracking, IMU, and full-body motion data for daily human motion modeling, so the row is data-centric.
- [Regularizing Hidden States Enables Learning Generalizable Reward Model for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10216): Adds hidden-state regularization to reward-model training, improving generalization beyond the preference distribution used for fitting.
- [AutoSurvey: Large Language Models Can Automatically Write Surveys](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10252): Builds an LLM survey-writing pipeline that retrieves literature, organizes outlines, and drafts related-work style surveys, making it closer to a harness entry.
- [Flextron: Many-in-One Flexible Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10260): Trains a flexible LLM whose activated components can be adjusted at inference to trade quality, latency, and resource use within one model.
- [MobileAIBench: Benchmarking LLMs and LMMs for On-Device Use Cases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10290): Benchmarks mobile LLM and LMM use cases under on-device constraints, so it is primarily an evaluation row rather than a model recipe.
- [LieRE: Generalizing Rotary Position Encodings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10322): Generalizes RoPE with Lie-group rotations so positional encodings can better fit high-dimensional modalities beyond one-dimensional text.
- [Efficient Prompting for LLM-Based Generative Internet of Things](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10382): Studies prompt compression and selection for locally deployed open-source LLMs in generative IoT settings with latency and security constraints.
- [What is the Visual Cognition Gap between Humans and Multimodal LLMs?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10424): Uses matrix-reasoning style tasks to compare human visual working memory with MLLM visual cognition, making the row evaluation-oriented.
- [Unveiling the Ignorance of MLLMs: Seeing Clearly, Answering Incorrectly](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10638): Constructs diagnostics showing MLLMs can perceive visual content yet answer incorrectly, separating perception success from reasoning failure.
- [CoLoR-Filter: Conditional Loss Reduction Filtering for Targeted Language Model Pre-training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10670): Selects pretraining data by estimating conditional loss reduction for target tasks with an empirical Bayes style filtering rule.
- [Invited: New Solutions on LLM Acceleration, Optimization, and Application](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10903): Surveys acceleration, optimization, and deployment solutions for LLMs rather than contributing a single new model mechanism.
- [Eliminating Biased Length Reliance of Direct Preference Optimization via Down-Sampled KL Divergence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10957): Modifies DPO with down-sampled KL divergence to reduce verbosity and length bias during preference optimization.
- [Adaptive Query Rewriting: Aligning Rewriters through Marginal Probability of Conversational Answers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10991): Trains conversational query rewriters with marginal answer probability, aligning rewrites to downstream answer likelihood without heavy annotations.
- [Are Large Language Models a Good Replacement of Taxonomies?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11131): Tests whether LLMs can replace schema taxonomies for nuanced long-tail knowledge, focusing on taxonomy reasoning rather than new training.
- [RePrompt: Planning by Automatic Prompt Engineering for Large Language Models Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11132): Automatically searches and revises prompts for LLM agents through planning feedback, which is mainly an agent-harness optimization method.
- [Breaking Boundaries: Investigating the Effects of Model Editing on Cross-linguistic Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11139): Evaluates how knowledge-editing methods affect multilingual LLM behavior across English and non-English models.
- [GoldCoin: Grounding Large Language Models in Privacy Laws via Contextual Integrity Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11149): Grounds privacy-law reasoning in contextual integrity theory so LLMs judge information flows by actors, attributes, transmission principles, and norms.
- [MMNeuron: Discovering Neuron-Level Domain-Specific Interpretation in Multimodal Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11193): Identifies domain-specific neurons in MLLMs and studies how visual features projected into word-embedding space activate modality and domain behavior.
- [In-Context Editing: Learning Knowledge from Self-Induced Distributions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11194): Introduces Consistent In-Context Editing, optimizing toward self-induced contextual distributions so models absorb new facts without full fine-tuning.
- [Self-training Large Language Models through Knowledge Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11275): Uses reference-free consistency to detect unknown samples, self-label them, and selectively self-train LLMs without large labeled datasets.
- [MoE-RBench: Towards Building Reliable Language Models with Sparse Mixture-of-Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11353): Builds a reliability benchmark for sparse MoE LMs and analyzes why MoE fine-tuning can underperform dense counterparts in new domains.
- [Preserving Knowledge in Large Language Model with Model-Agnostic Self-Decompression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11354): Uses model-agnostic self-decompression to preserve language knowledge when LLMs or MLLMs undergo domain post-pretraining or SFT.
- [MetaGPT: Merging Large Language Models Using Model Exclusive Task Arithmetic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11385): Refines task arithmetic for model merging by isolating task-exclusive vectors before combining multitask LLM capabilities.
- [Intrinsic Test of Unlearning Using Parametric Knowledge Traces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11614): Probes parametric knowledge traces inside LLMs to test whether unlearning removes internal knowledge rather than only suppressing outputs.
- [Can LLM be a Personalized Judge?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11657): Evaluates whether LLM-as-judge can reflect individual user preferences, exposing personalization limits in preference evaluation.
- [Optimizing Instructions and Demonstrations for Multi-Stage Language Model Programs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11695): Optimizes prompts for multi-stage LM programs by factorizing downstream metric feedback across modules without gradients or module-level labels.
- [Interactive Evolution: A Neural-Symbolic Self-Training Framework For Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11736): Combines LLM self-training with symbolic rules and iterative evolution to reduce reliance on human-annotated alignment data.
- [DataComp-LM: In search of the next generation of training sets for language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11794): Provides a 240T-token Common Crawl pool, OpenLM recipes, and 53 evaluations for controlled language-model data curation experiments.
- [Iterative Length-Regularized Direct Preference Optimization: A Case Study on Improving 7B Language Models to GPT-4 Level](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11817): Adds length regularization to iterative DPO so online reward-model labeling improves response quality without driving verbosity.
- [WPO: Enhancing RLHF with Weighted Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11827): Weights off-policy preference pairs to correct distribution gaps between the data-generating policy and the policy being optimized.
- [Unveiling Encoder-Free Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11832): Trains VLMs without a separate vision encoder, letting the language model directly process visual token inputs for more flexible multimodal modeling.
- [Initial Investigation of Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks (KANs) as Feature Extractors for IMU Based Human Activity Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11914): Tests KAN layers as nonlinear feature extractors for IMU human-activity recognition, comparing them with conventional neural feature encoders.
- [Prefixing Attention Sinks can Mitigate Activation Outliers for Large Language Model Quantization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12016): Prefixes attention-sink tokens to prevent activation outliers, enabling simpler per-tensor activation quantization for LLMs.
- [Self-MoE: Towards Compositional Large Language Models with Self-Specialized Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12034): Converts one LLM into MiXSE self-specialized experts using self-generated domain data and self-optimized routing.
- [Soft Prompting for Unlearning in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12038): Uses learned soft prompts as a parameter-efficient unlearning mechanism, removing targeted knowledge without updating all model weights.
- [Intermediate Distillation: Data-Efficient Distillation from Black-Box LLMs for Information Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12169): Distills black-box LLM ranking knowledge into retrievers through intermediate supervision signals instead of requiring teacher weights or logits.
- [Time Series Modeling for Heart Rate Prediction: From ARIMA to Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12199): Compares ARIMA, Prophet, and deep time-series models for heart-rate forecasting, making the row a narrow medical time-series application.
- [InterIntent: Investigating Social Intelligence of LLMs via Intention Understanding in an Interactive Game Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12203): Evaluates LLM intention understanding in an interactive game framework, moving beyond static social-intelligence questionnaires.
- [TroL: Traversal of Layers for Large Language and Vision Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12246): Traverses and selects intermediate LLM and vision layers to improve open-source LLVM vision-language performance without simply scaling model size.
- [Defending Against Social Engineering Attacks in the Age of LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12263): Builds SEConvo chat-based social-engineering scenarios and tests LLMs as both attack facilitators and defensive detectors.
- [VoCo-LLaMA: Towards Vision Compression with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12275): Lets the LLM participate in vision-token compression, reducing high-resolution image and video token counts without relying only on external compressors.
- [Abstraction-of-Thought Makes Language Models Better Reasoners](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12442): Introduces Abstraction-of-Thought, a reasoning format that makes models state abstract problem structure before deriving concrete answers.
- [P-React: Synthesizing Topic-Adaptive Reactions of Personality Traits via Mixture of Specialized LoRA Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12548): Uses specialized LoRA experts to synthesize topic-adaptive reactions grounded in Big Five personality traits for personalized LLM behavior.
- [Bridging Local Details and Global Context in Text-Attributed Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12608): Combines local language-model node encoding with global graph aggregation to improve representation learning on text-attributed graphs.
- [Estimating Knowledge in Large Language Models Without Generating a Single Token](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12673): Trains KEEN probes on internal subject representations to predict entity-level QA accuracy and open-ended factuality before the model generates text.
- [LaMDA: Large Model Fine-Tuning via Spectrally Decomposed Low-Dimensional Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12832): Fine-tunes LLMs with spectrally decomposed low-dimensional adapters that freeze a projection path and train a compact square matrix to cut trainable parameters and peak memory.
- [Interpretable Preferences via Multi-Objective Reward Modeling and Mixture-of-Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12845): Builds ArmoRM by training an absolute-rating multi-objective reward model and using a gating network to choose interpretable objectives such as honesty, safety, and verbosity.
- [Reconciling Kaplan and Chinchilla Scaling Laws](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12907): Explains the Kaplan-Chinchilla scaling-law discrepancy through parameter-counting choices and small-scale bias, then reaffirms Chinchilla-style compute-optimal exponents.
- [Tender: Accelerating Large Language Models via Tensor Decomposition and Runtime Requantization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12930): Uses tensor decomposition with power-of-two-separated scales so low-precision LLM inference can accumulate decomposed matrix products without explicit requantization.
- [ChatBug: A Common Vulnerability of Aligned LLMs Induced by Chat Templates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12935): Shows that rigid chat-template formatting creates a safety bypass surface and demonstrates attacks that exploit user prompts not bound by the template.
- [D2O: Dynamic Discriminative Operations for Efficient Generative Inference of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13035): Compresses the KV cache without fine-tuning by dynamically allocating layer-level eviction and compensating token-level context loss during long-sequence generation.
- [Multi-Stage Balanced Distillation: Addressing Long-Tail Challenges in Sequence-Level Knowledge Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13114): Iteratively balances sequence-level distillation data by selecting representative head-domain examples and synthesizing tail-domain examples within a fixed compute budget.
- [When Parts Are Greater Than Sums: Individual LLM Components Can Outperform Full Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13131): Decomposes in-context predictions into attention-head and MLP contributions, then linearly reweights components from a few labeled examples to improve classification.
- [Amphista: Bi-directional Multi-head Decoding for Accelerating LLM Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13170): Extends Medusa-style speculative decoding with an auto-embedding block, bidirectional attention among drafting heads, and staged adaptation layers.
- [AdaMoE: Token-Adaptive Routing with Null Experts for Mixture-of-Experts Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13233): Adds zero-FLOP null experts to MoE routing so each token can adaptively use a different number of real experts under a load-balancing objective.
- [VisualRWKV: Exploring Recurrent Neural Networks for Visual Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13362): Adapts RWKV-style linear recurrence to VLMs with data-dependent recurrence, sandwich prompts, and 2-D image scanning for faster long-context multimodal inference.
- [VELO: A Vector Database-Assisted Cloud-Edge Collaborative LLM QoS Optimization Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13399): Caches similar LLM request results in an edge vector database and optimizes admission and resource choices to reduce cloud-edge response latency and cost.
- [Finding Blind Spots in Evaluator LLMs with Interpretable Checklists](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13439): Introduces FBI, which perturbs answers across factuality, instruction following, coherence, and reasoning to test whether evaluator LLMs detect targeted quality drops.
- [ObscurePrompt: Jailbreaking Large Language Models via Obscure Input](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13662): Iteratively rewrites jailbreak prompts into obscure out-of-distribution text, exploiting fragile alignment boundaries while retaining attack intent.
- [CityGPT: Empowering Urban Spatial Cognition of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13948): Injects urban spatial knowledge through CityInstruction and a city-scale world-model framework so LLMs can reason over urban tasks and geospatial context.
- [ReaL: Efficient RLHF Training of Large Language Models with Parameter Reallocation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14088): Reallocates model parameters across a cluster and searches fine-grained execution plans to improve throughput for the heterogeneous workloads in RLHF.
- [An Investigation of Prompt Variations for Zero-shot LLM-based Rankers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14117): Separates ranking algorithm, model backbone, and prompt wording effects in zero-shot LLM rankers, showing prompt components can dominate observed gains.
- [MACAROON: Training Vision-Language Models To Be Your Engaged Partners](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14137): Trains LVLMs to ask clarification or request extra information for invalid, ambiguous, and personalizable questions using the PIE hierarchy and data.
- [Raising the Bar: Investigating the Values of Large Language Models via Generative Evolving Testing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14230): Proposes GETA, an adaptive value-alignment test generator that evolves prompts instead of relying on a static harmful-content benchmark pool.
- [Mind the Privacy Unit! User-Level Differential Privacy for Language Model Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14322): Compares group privacy and user-wise DP-SGD for LLM fine-tuning, highlighting how example-level privacy can underprotect users with many records.
- [Data-Centric AI in the Age of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14473): Frames LLM research around data curation, data attribution, knowledge transfer, and inference contextualization, arguing for data-centric benchmarks and documentation.
- [Instruction Pre-Training: Language Models are Supervised Multitask Learners](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14491): Synthesizes 200M instruction-response pairs over raw corpora and uses them during pretraining so base LMs learn supervised multitask behavior earlier.
- [rKAN: Rational Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14495): Replaces spline bases in KANs with trainable rational functions based on Pade approximation and rational Jacobi functions for approximation and physics tasks.
- [How Well Do LLMs Represent Values Across Cultures? Empirical Analysis of LLM Responses Based on Hofstede Cultural Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14805): Uses Hofstede-dimension personas and country-linked languages to test whether LLM advice changes appropriately across cultural value profiles.
- [Efficient Continual Pre-training by Mitigating the Stability Gap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14833): Diagnoses the early performance drop in continual pretraining and proposes compute-fixed strategies that speed recovery from the stability gap.
- [Leveraging Passage Embeddings for Efficient Listwise Reranking with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14848): Introduces PE-Rank, feeding passage embeddings as special tokens and constraining decoding to make listwise LLM reranking shorter and faster.
- [Mixture of Attention Spans: Optimizing LLM Inference Efficiency with Heterogeneous Sliding-Window Lengths](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14909): Profiles heads and layers to assign heterogeneous sliding-window lengths, extending effective context while preserving the efficiency of local attention.
- [Steering Without Side Effects: Improving Post-Deployment Control of Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.15518): Trains KL-then-steer models to minimize benign-input divergence before applying steering vectors, reducing jailbreaks while preserving helpful behavior.
- [Large Language Models have Intrinsic Self-Correction Ability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.15673): Identifies zero temperature and fair prompts as key conditions under which LLMs can revise their own answers without external evidence.
- [Unveiling and Harnessing Hidden Attention Sinks: Enhancing Large Language Models without Training through Attention Calibration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.15765): Finds attention sinks beyond the initial token and introduces training-free attention calibration that adjusts inference-time attention distributions per input.
- [RuleR: Improving LLM Controllability by Rule-based Data Recycling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.15938): Recycles existing SFT examples by applying rule-based response edits and appending the corresponding constraints to instructions for controllability training.
- [Modular Pluralism: Pluralistic Alignment via Multi-LLM Collaboration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.15951): Plugs smaller community-specific LMs into a base model so black-box systems can support Overton, steerable, and distributional pluralism.
- [Multi-Objective Linguistic Control of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16229): Fine-tunes LLMs with input controls for multiple linguistic complexity values, improving response complexity control without degrading answer quality.
- [Confidence Regulation Neurons in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16254): Identifies entropy neurons and token-frequency neurons that regulate uncertainty by changing residual-stream scale or shifting logits toward unigram frequencies.
- [DemoRank: Selecting Effective Demonstrations for Large Language Models in Ranking Task](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16332): Trains a demonstration reranker from LLM-scored demonstration lists so ranking-task in-context examples account for dependencies between demonstrations.
- [On the Transformations across Reward Model, Parameter Update, and In-Context Prompt](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16377): Formalizes six transformations among parameter updates, reward models, and in-context prompts, showing how common LLM adaptation tools can emulate one another.
- [Building on Efficient Foundations: Effectively Training LLMs with Structured Feedforward Layers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16450): Studies low-rank and block-diagonal FFN parameterizations from scratch at LLM scale and adds self-guided training for efficient structured layers.
- [OTCE: Hybrid SSM and Attention with Cross Domain Mixture of Experts to construct Observer-Thinker-Conceiver-Expresser](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16495): Combines selective state-space layers, quadratic attention, position injection, and cross-domain MoE experts in the OTCE language-model architecture.
- [Large Vocabulary Size Improves Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16508): Shows larger subword vocabularies improve LLM performance and proposes swapping to a new vocabulary during target-language continual training.
- [LLaMA-MoE: Building Mixture-of-Experts from LLaMA with Continual Pre-Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16554): Converts dense LLaMA feed-forward layers into experts and continually pretrains routing gates, producing sparse models from existing dense checkpoints.
- [Noisy Neighbors: Efficient membership inference attacks against LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16565): Audits LLM memorization by perturbing a target example in embedding space to create noisy neighbors, avoiding shadow-model training.
- [OlympicArena Medal Ranks: Who Is the Most Intelligent AI So Far?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16772): Ranks GPT-4o, Claude 3.5 Sonnet, Gemini 1.5 Pro, and open models on OlympicArena with an Olympic medal-table aggregation.
- [Lottery Ticket Adaptation: Mitigating Destructive Interference in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16797): Finds sparse task subnetworks for adaptation, reducing destructive interference across instruction following, reasoning, math, and summarization tasks.
- [EAGLE-2: Faster Inference of Language Models with Dynamic Draft Trees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16858): Uses calibrated draft confidence to build context-aware dynamic draft trees, improving EAGLE speculative decoding while preserving the output distribution.
- [Large Language Models Assume People are More Rational than We Really are](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.17055): Compares LLM simulations and predictions against human decision data, finding models overfit toward expected-value rational choice.
- [MM-SpuBench: Towards Better Understanding of Spurious Biases in Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.17126): Builds a human-verified MLLM benchmark with nine spurious-correlation types to measure shortcut reliance in multimodal reasoning.
- [Dual-Space Knowledge Distillation for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.17328): Aligns teacher and student output spaces with dual-space distillation and cross-model attention, supporting LLM KD across different vocabularies.
- [Make Some Noise: Unlocking Language Model Parallel Inference Capability through Noisy Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.17404): Replaces standard SFT with noisy denoising training and uses TR-Jacobi decoding to make parallel LLM inference faster without extra draft modules.
- [Tell Me Where You Are: Multimodal LLMs Meet Place Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.17520): Combines VFM retrieval with MLLM pairwise scene descriptions and reasoning to choose visual place-recognition candidates for robot localization.
- [The FineWeb Datasets: Decanting the Web for the Finest Text Data at Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.17557): Curates 15T FineWeb tokens from 96 Common Crawl snapshots, ablates filtering and deduplication choices, and adds FineWeb-Edu for educational text.
- [Knowledge Distillation in Automated Annotation: Supervised Text Classification with LLM-Generated Training Labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.17633): Replicates 14 social-science classification tasks to test when supervised classifiers trained on GPT-4 labels match human-label fine-tuning.
- [Banishing LLM Hallucinations Requires Rethinking Generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.17642): Links hallucination to next-token generalization limits and proposes Lamini-1, which retrieves facts from a large mixture of memory experts.
- [Evaluating Large Language Models with Psychometrics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.17675): Designs a psychometric evaluation suite covering personality, values, emotional intelligence, theory of mind, and self-efficacy across 13 datasets.
- [From Distributional to Overton Pluralism: Investigating Large Language Model Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.17692): Finds alignment shifts responses toward longer aggregated information and shows base models can recover aligned-model outputs with examples and semantic hints.
- [Data curation via joint example selection further accelerates multimodal learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.17711): Introduces JEST, selecting multimodal contrastive-learning batches jointly rather than independently and steering selection with pretrained reference models.
- [Probing the effects of broken symmetries in machine learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.17747): Tests approximate rotational invariance in water simulations and finds broken symmetry has negligible bulk effects but visible extrapolative artifacts.
- [MG-LLaVA: Toward Multi-Granularity Visual Instruction Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.17770): Adds low-resolution, high-resolution, and object-centric visual flows to MLLMs, fusing detail through a Conv-Gate module and detector-derived object features.
- [SigKAN: Signature-Weighted Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks for Time Series](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.17890): Weights KAN outputs with learnable path signatures so the network captures geometric sequence features for time-series approximation and forecasting.
- [MammothModa: Multi-Modal Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.18193): Improves an MLLM with visual attention experts, a visual merger for high-resolution and long-duration features, and curated bilingual multimodal data.
- [Kolmogorov-Arnold Graph Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.18354): Builds GKAN with spline-based edge activation functions, improving graph prediction tasks while making message-passing behavior more interpretable.
- [Towards Open-World Grasping with Large Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.18722): Uses VLMs with segmentation and grasp synthesis for open-ended referring segmentation, grounded grasp planning, and contact-based grasp ranking.
- [Revisiting Backdoor Attacks against Large Vision-Language Models from Domain Shift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.18844): Studies LVLM backdoor generalization under visual and text domain shifts and proposes attribution-guided domain-agnostic triggers.
- [Decoding-Time Language Model Alignment with Multiple Objectives](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.18853): Derives multi-objective decoding, combining next-token predictions from differently aligned models under f-divergence-regularized objectives at inference time.
- [Efficacy of Language Model Self-Play in Non-Zero-Sum Games](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.18872): Fine-tunes language models through filtered behavior cloning in Deal or No Deal self-play, improving cooperative and competitive negotiation rewards.
- [Capturing Minds, Not Just Words: Enhancing Role-Playing Language Models with Personality-Indicative Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.18921): Distills role-playing dialogues from psychological-scale questions so smaller role-playing LMs capture character personality rather than only style.
- [STBench: Assessing the Ability of Large Language Models in Spatio-Temporal Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.19065): Builds STBench with 13 spatio-temporal QA tasks and more than 60,000 examples spanning knowledge, reasoning, computation, and applications.
- [Resolving Discrepancies in Compute-Optimal Scaling of Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.19146): Reproduces Kaplan-style scaling and attributes the mismatch with Chinchilla to last-layer compute, warmup length, and scale-dependent optimizer tuning.
- [Fundamental Problems With Model Editing: How Should Rational Belief Revision Work in LLMs?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.19354): Recasts model editing as belief revision, lists 12 formulation and benchmark problems, and introduces a Wikidata-based Bayesian-agent testbed.
- [Can Large Language Models Generate High-quality Patent Claims?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.19465): Evaluates patent claim generation from descriptions, finding general LLMs outperform patent-specific models and fine-tuning improves feature completeness.
- [Solving Token Gradient Conflict in Mixture-of-Experts for Large Vision-Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.19905): Uses token-level gradients to find conflicting tokens inside LVLM MoE experts and adds a regularizer that reroutes them to reduce interference.
- [ScaleBiO: Scalable Bilevel Optimization for LLM Data Reweighting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.19976): Scales first-order bilevel optimization with LISA to data reweighting for LLMs up to roughly 30B parameters on eight H100 GPUs.
- [LEMoE: Advanced Mixture of Experts Adaptor for Lifelong Model Editing of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.20030): Uses a MoE adaptor with KV-anchor routing and clustering-based edit-order planning to reduce forgetting, routing inconsistency, and order sensitivity in lifelong model editing.
- [LLaVolta: Efficient Multi-modal Models via Stage-wise Visual Context Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.20092): Compresses visual tokens during staged MLLM training, showing large visual redundancy and improving image and video understanding efficiency without test-time information loss.
- [Scaling Synthetic Data Creation with 1,000,000,000 Personas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.20094): Releases Persona Hub, a billion-persona pool that drives synthetic generation of reasoning problems, prompts, knowledge text, game NPCs, and tool functions from diverse perspectives.
- [Queue Management for SLO-Oriented Large Language Model Serving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.00047): Builds QLM, a request-queue scheduler that estimates waiting time across batch and interactive LLM traffic to improve SLO attainment and GPU throughput.
- [LiteSearch: Efficacious Tree Search for LLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.00320): Guides LLM tree search with dynamic node selection and node-level exploration budgets from a value network trained without step annotations, reducing compute on GSM8K and TabMWP.
- [Iterative Nash Policy Optimization: Aligning LLMs with General Preferences via No-Regret Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.00617): Frames preference alignment as a two-player game and optimizes a self-play no-regret objective that avoids per-response win-rate estimation.
- [From Introspection to Best Practices: Principled Analysis of Demonstrations in Multimodal In-Context Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.00902): Perturbs modality content across multimodal ICL tasks to show when image or text demonstrations matter and derive task-specific demonstration strategies.
- [FoldGPT: Simple and Effective Large Language Model Compression Scheme](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.00928): Compresses LLM depth with gated block removal, group parameter sharing, light fine-tuning, and tail-layer distillation for mobile-friendly deployment.
- [Efficient Expert Pruning for Sparse Mixture-of-Experts Language Models: Enhancing Performance and Reducing Inference Costs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.00945): Applies gradient-free evolutionary expert pruning to sparse MoE LMs, reducing total or active experts in Mixtral while preserving or improving task performance.
- [Eliminating Position Bias of Language Models: A Mechanistic Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01100): Attributes document-order bias to causal attention and relative position encodings, then uses PINE to reorder document-level attention for training-free position-invariant inference.
- [Diffusion Forcing: Next-token Prediction Meets Full-Sequence Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01392): Trains sequence diffusion with independent per-token noise levels so a causal model can combine variable-length next-token generation with guided full-sequence diffusion.
- [TimeToM: Temporal Space is the Key to Unlocking the Door of Large Language Models' Theory-of-Mind](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01455): Constructs temporal belief-state chains and a tool-belief solver to convert higher-order beliefs into first-order beliefs for LLM theory-of-mind reasoning.
- [EquiBot: SIM(3)-Equivariant Diffusion Policy for Generalizable and Data Efficient Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01479): Combines SIM(3)-equivariant neural networks with diffusion policies so robot manipulation policies generalize from minutes of demonstrations across object and scene variations.
- [LLM See, LLM Do: Guiding Data Generation to Target Non-Differentiable Objectives](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01490): Studies passive inheritance from synthetic-data generators and uses active inheritance to steer generated training data toward objectives such as lexical diversity or low toxicity.
- [RegMix: Data Mixture as Regression for Language Model Pre-training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01492): Trains many small proxy LMs on candidate data mixtures and uses regression to select a mixture for larger pretraining runs, outperforming hand selection and DoReMi with less compute.
- [Improving Diffusion Inverse Problem Solving with Decoupled Noise Annealing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01521): Introduces DAPS, a decoupled annealing posterior sampler that lets diffusion inverse-problem trajectories vary between steps while annealing toward the true posterior.
- [Black Big Boxes: Tracing Adjective Order Preferences in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.02136): Traces LM adjective-order preferences to training-data frequency, unseen-combination generalization, and contextual word-order cues using learning dynamics and attribution.
- [Synthetic Multimodal Question Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.02233): Uses a retriever, LLM, and LMM to synthesize style- and modality-controlled QA pairs from multimodal documents for MMRAG evaluation.
- [TokenPacker: Efficient Visual Projector for Multimodal LLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.02392): Replaces one-to-one visual projection with a coarse-to-fine region-to-point injector that compresses visual tokens by 75 to 89 percent while retaining fine visual reasoning.
- [Consistency Flow Matching: Defining Straight Flows with Velocity Consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.02398): Enforces velocity-field self-consistency and multi-segment training so flow matching learns straighter sampling paths with faster convergence and better sample quality.
- [Predicting vs. Acting: A Trade-off Between World Modeling & Agent Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.02446): Shows RLHF-aligned agent models lose next-token world-modeling ability because implicit generation blueprints concentrate probability on repeated anchor spans.
- [Magic Insert: Style-Aware Drag-And-Drop](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.02489): Fine-tunes a text-to-image diffusion model with LoRA and target-style CLIP features, then adapts object insertion to stylized domains with the SubjectPlop evaluation dataset.
- [Semantic-Aware Power Allocation for Generative Semantic Communications with Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.03050): Models semantic value and transmission reliability for foundation-model image communication, then optimizes power allocation with constraint decoupling and bisection search.
- [Improved Noise Schedule for Diffusion Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.03297): Designs a diffusion noise schedule by importance-sampling log-SNR near the signal-noise transition, improving convergence and ImageNet generation across prediction targets.
- [On Large Language Models in National Security Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.03453): Analyzes how LLMs can support national-security information processing, wargaming, summarization, and decision support while requiring safeguards for hallucination and adversarial risks.
- [Q-Adapter: Customizing Pre-trained LLMs to New Preferences with Forgetting Mitigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.03856): Customizes pretrained LLMs by learning a residual Q-function adapter from new preference data, preserving original capabilities while shifting toward a new reward signal.
- [LLM-jp: A Cross-organizational Project for the Research and Development of Fully Open Japanese LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.03963): Reports the LLM-jp consortium's open Japanese LLM program, including project organization, activities, and technical details of its released models.
- [SineKAN: Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks using sinusoidal activation functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.04149): Replaces B-spline KAN edge activations with reweighted sine-function grids, improving speed while remaining competitive with spline and Fourier KAN baselines.
- [Mixture of A Million Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.04153): Introduces PEER, a product-key retrieval layer that selects from over a million tiny experts to improve the performance-compute tradeoff of transformer feedforward blocks.
- [Understanding the Gains from Repeated Self-Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.04600): Analyzes repeated self-distillation in linear regression and shows multi-step distillation can reduce excess risk far beyond a single self-distillation pass.
- [AWT: Transferring Vision-Language Models via Augmentation, Weighting, and Transportation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.04603): Adapts VLMs with augmented visual and class-description views, entropy-based weighting, and optimal transport, improving zero-shot and few-shot classification and video recognition.
- [LoRA-GA: Low-Rank Adaptation with Gradient Approximation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.05000): Initializes LoRA so low-rank update gradients align with full fine-tuning at the first step, accelerating convergence and improving T5 and Llama task results.
- [Multimodal Prompt Learning with Missing Modalities for Sentiment Analysis and Emotion Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.05374): Uses generative, missing-signal, and missing-type prompts in a multimodal Transformer to recover missing modality features for sentiment and emotion tasks.
- [Just read twice: closing the recall gap for recurrent language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.05483): Links recurrent LM recall failures to set-disjointness order effects and improves ICL with repeated prompts or non-causal prefix-linear attention.
- [On the Power of Convolution Augmented Transformer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.05591): Adds convolutional filters to attention QKV embeddings, proving and testing gains for associative recall, copying, and length generalization.
- [Saltzer & Schroeder for 2030: Security engineering principles in a world of AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.05710): Reinterprets classic security-engineering principles for AI-generated code and developer use of code-generation tools in secure software work.
- [Multimodal Diffusion Transformer: Learning Versatile Behavior from Multimodal Goals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.05996): Trains a diffusion-policy transformer with latent goal-conditioned state representations and auxiliary objectives to learn long-horizon robot behavior from sparse language labels.
- [iLLM-TSC: Integration reinforcement learning and large language model for traffic signal control policy improvement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.06025): Couples RL traffic-signal control with an LLM reasonableness checker that adjusts decisions under degraded observations or unmodeled events.
- [ANOLE: An Open, Autoregressive, Native Large Multimodal Models for Interleaved Image-Text Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.06135): Fine-tunes Chameleon into an open autoregressive multimodal model for coherent interleaved image-text generation, releasing model, training framework, and instruction data.
- [Multi-Object Hallucination in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.06192): Defines ROPE, a referring-prompt evaluation protocol showing LVLM hallucination worsens when models must focus on multiple objects with class-distribution and salience effects.
- [B'MOJO: Hybrid State Space Realizations of Foundation Models with Eidetic and Fading Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.06324): Builds a hybrid memory architecture that unifies in-context, in-weight, in-state, and retrieval-backed storage, outperforming SSM and hybrid baselines on associative recall and long sequences.
- [Towards Understanding Multi-Task Learning (Generalization) of LLMs via Detecting and Exploring Task-Specific Neurons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.06488): Identifies task-specific neurons with gradient attribution and uses neuron-level continual fine-tuning to reduce forgetting while preserving cross-task generalization.
- [FBI-LLM: Scaling Up Fully Binarized LLMs from Scratch via Autoregressive Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.07093): Trains fully binarized transformer LMs from scratch with autoregressive distillation, releasing 130M, 1.3B, and 7B models and training data.
- [Reuse, Don't Retrain: A Recipe for Continued Pretraining of Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.07263): Provides data-mixture and learning-rate guidelines for continued pretraining, improving a 15B model over continued training on the original pretraining distribution.
- [PaliGemma: A versatile 3B VLM for transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.07726): Describes Google's open 3B SigLIP-Gemma VLM, evaluated on roughly 40 transfer tasks spanning general VLM benchmarks, remote sensing, and segmentation.
- [Fine-Tuning Large Language Models with User-Level Differential Privacy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.07737): Develops scalable user-level differentially private LLM fine-tuning with tight ELS accounting and shows when user-level sampling outperforms example-level clipping.
- [Solving General Natural-Language-Description Optimization Problems with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.07924): Presents OptLLM, which translates natural-language optimization requests into mathematical formulations and solver code while supporting multi-turn refinement and fine-tuned Qwen experiments.
- [MambaVision: A Hybrid Mamba-Transformer Vision Backbone](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.08083): Redesigns Mamba for vision and adds late self-attention blocks in a hierarchical backbone, improving ImageNet, detection, instance segmentation, and semantic segmentation throughput.
- [AddressCLIP: Empowering Vision-Language Models for City-wide Image Address Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.08156): Aligns images with address text and scene captions, then constrains image features by geographic distance for end-to-end city-scale image address localization.
- [GeNet: A Multimodal LLM-Based Co-Pilot for Network Topology and Configuration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.08249): Uses a multimodal LLM to interpret topology diagrams and device configurations so enterprise network engineers can modify network topology and configuration from user intents.
- [DenseFusion-1M: Merging Vision Experts for Comprehensive Multimodal Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.08303): Generates a one-million-image dense-caption dataset by fusing perception experts with an MLLM pivot, improving comprehensive visual perception in downstream MLLMs.
- [FlashAttention-3: Fast and Accurate Attention with Asynchrony and Low-precision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.08608): Speeds Hopper attention with warp-specialized asynchronous compute, interleaved matmul-softmax, and FP8 block quantization, reaching higher utilization with lower FP8 error.
- [Towards Building Specialized Generalist AI with System 1 and System 2 Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.08642): Defines specialized generalist AI and sketches a System 1 and System 2 fusion framework for combining expert-level specialization with broader generality.
- [HiRes-LLaVA: Restoring Fragmentation Input in High-Resolution Large Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.08706): Restores sliced high-resolution patches with a SliceRestore adapter and self-mining sampler, preserving global-local geometry while reducing LVLM training overhead.
- [Model Surgery: Modulating LLM's Behavior Via Simple Parameter Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.08770): Edits a small subset of LLM parameters at inference-level cost to reduce toxicity and jailbreak susceptibility while preserving broad benchmark capabilities.
- [Disassembling Obfuscated Executables with LLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.08924): Builds DisasLLM with an LLM classifier for wrongly decoded assembly instructions and an end-to-end strategy for disassembling executables with junk-byte obfuscation.
- [GAVEL: Generating Games Via Evolution and Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.09388): Trains language-model mutations and recombinations over Ludii game descriptions, using evolutionary search to generate novel playable board games.
- [Foundation models for the electric power grid](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.09434): Argues for GridFM, a graph-based foundation-model concept trained on diverse grid data and topologies for power-grid downstream tasks.
- [Self-Consuming Generative Models with Curated Data Provably Optimize Human Preferences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.09499): Theoretically shows that iterative retraining on user-curated synthetic data can act as implicit preference optimization while also amplifying reward-model bias.
- [GOFA: A Generative One-For-All Model for Joint Graph Language Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.09709): Interleaves GNN layers into a frozen LLM and pretrains graph-level next-word, QA, and structural tasks to create a generative graph foundation model.
- [LeanQuant: Accurate and Scalable Large Language Model Quantization with Loss-error-aware Grid](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.10032): Learns loss-error-aware quantization grids instead of min-max affine grids, scaling accurate LLM post-training quantization up to Llama-3.1 405B.
- [What Makes and Breaks Safety Fine-tuning? A Mechanistic Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.10264): Uses synthetic unsafe-input factors to show safety fine-tuning, DPO, and unlearning push unsafe activations toward MLP-weight null spaces.
- [By My Eyes: Grounding Multimodal Large Language Models with Sensor Data via Visual Prompting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.10385): Converts sensor sequences into task-specific visual prompts for MLLMs, improving nine sensing tasks while cutting token cost versus text prompts.
- [Learning Dynamics of LLM Finetuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.10490): Decomposes step-wise influence during instruction and preference tuning, explaining hallucination reinforcement and DPO overtraining through response-to-response learning dynamics.
- [Learning to Unlearn for Robust Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.10494): Uses meta-learning and gradient harmonization to optimize unlearning so models forget target samples while preserving generalizable knowledge from remaining data.
- [Arena Learning: Build Data Flywheel for LLMs Post-training via Simulated Chatbot Arena](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.10627): Simulates Chatbot Arena battles with AI annotations, predicts Elo rankings through WizardArena, and iteratively trains WizardLM models from battle-derived weaknesses.
- [An Empirical Study of Validating Synthetic Data for Formula Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.10657): Validates synthetic natural-language annotations for spreadsheet formula generation with surrogate accuracy objectives, improving fine-tuned model performance over raw synthetic data.
- [LLM Circuit Analyses Are Consistent Across Training and Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.10827): Tracks circuits across 300B training tokens and 70M to 2.8B decoder-only LMs, finding task algorithms emerge consistently across time and scale.
- [Weighted Grouped Query Attention in Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.10855): Adds learnable key and value head weights to grouped-query attention during fine-tuning, recovering closer multi-head attention quality without inference overhead.
- [Understanding the Importance of Evolutionary Search in Automated Heuristic Design with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.10873): Benchmarks LLM-based evolutionary program search for automated heuristic design across four methods, four problem families, nine LLMs, and repeated runs.
- [SLIP: Securing LLMs IP Using Weights Decomposition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.10886): Splits edge-deployed LLM weights via matrix decomposition between secure and vulnerable compute, protecting model IP with no accuracy loss and low latency overhead.
- [Fine-Tuning and Prompt Optimization: Two Great Steps that Work Better Together](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.10930): Alternates LM weight fine-tuning and prompt-template optimization for modular pipelines, outperforming weight-only or prompt-only optimization in DSPy tasks.
- [AstroMLab 1: Who Wins Astronomy Jeopardy!?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.11194): Evaluates proprietary and open-weight LLMs on 4,425 astronomy multiple-choice questions, analyzing subfield accuracy, calibration, cost trends, and open-model progress.
- [From Low Rank Gradient Subspace Stabilization to Low-Rank Weights: Observations, Theories, and Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.11239): Connects stabilized gradient subspaces to low-rank LLM weights and proposes WeLore for one-shot compression plus memory-efficient fine-tuning.
- [Distractors-Immune Representation Learning with Cross-modal Contrastive Regularization for Change Captioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.11683): Learns stable paired-image representations under viewpoint and illumination distractors, then uses cross-modal contrastive regularization to improve change-caption generation.
- [Data-Juicer Sandbox: A Feedback-Driven Suite for Multimodal Data-Model Co-development](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.11784): Provides a Probe-Analyze-Refine sandbox for multimodal data and model co-development, validating feedback loops on CLIP pretraining, LLaVA-style captioning, and DiT text-to-video.
- [PipeInfer: Accelerating LLM Inference using Asynchronous Pipelined Speculation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.11798): Uses continuous asynchronous speculation and early cancellation to speed pipeline-parallel LLM inference for single requests even when speculation acceptance is low.
- [Preemptive Detection and Correction of Misaligned Actions in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.11843): Presents InferAct, a theory-of-mind belief-reasoning layer that flags and corrects user-intent misaligned agent actions before execution.
- [OmniBind: Large-scale Omni Multimodal Representation via Binding Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.11895): Binds pretrained 3D, audio, image, and language representation spaces with lightweight routers, scaling to 30B omni-representation models from mostly unpaired data.
- [GraphFM: A generalist graph transformer that learns transferable representations across diverse domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.11907): Pretrains a Perceiver-style graph transformer on 152 graph datasets with shared latent tokens, improving transfer across molecular, citation, product, and synthetic graphs.
- [Spectra: Surprising Effectiveness of Pretraining Ternary Language Models at Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.12327): Releases the Spectra suite of float, post-training-quantized, and ternary LMs, showing pretrained ternary models scale better than equal-bit baselines above one billion parameters.
- [PersLLM: A Personified Training Approach for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.12393): Constructs richer personality data with chain-of-thought prompting and anti-induction, then uses automated DPO to make LLM personas more specific and dynamic.
- [ClearCLIP: Decomposing CLIP Representations for Dense Vision-Language Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.12442): Finds CLIP residual connections drive noisy dense segmentation and improves open-vocabulary segmentation by removing final residuals, using self-self attention, and dropping the FFN.
- [Beyond Next Token Prediction: Patch-Level Training for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.12665): Trains LLMs first on aggregated text patches and then finishes with token-level training, halving training cost without losing performance on 370M to 2.7B models.
- [MoME: Mixture of Multimodal Experts for Generalist Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.12709): Combines mixture-of-vision-experts and sparse language experts to reduce task interference in generalist MLLMs while keeping inference cost roughly unchanged.
- [Whispering Experts: Neural Interventions for Toxicity Mitigation in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.12824): Identifies toxicity-discriminating neurons with AUROC scores and scales down their activations, reducing toxic generation across LLM sizes with small perplexity cost.
- [The 2024 Foundation Model Transparency Index](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.12929): Scores 14 foundation-model developers on 100 transparency indicators, finding improved disclosures since 2023 but persistent opacity around copyright, data access, labor, and downstream impact.
- [DropKAN: Regularizing KANs by masking post-activations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.13044): Masks activation outputs inside Kolmogorov-Arnold Network layers, avoiding unstable neuron dropout behavior and improving KAN generalization on tabular datasets.
- [EarthMarker: A Visual Prompting Multimodal Large Language Model for Remote Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.13596): Builds a remote-sensing MLLM that interprets images at image, region, and point levels with boxes and points plus cross-domain learning and RSVP instruction data.
- [Scaling Laws with Vocabulary: Larger Models Deserve Larger Vocabularies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.13623): Derives compute-optimal vocabulary scaling with IsoFLOPs, derivative estimates, and loss fitting, showing larger LLMs benefit from much larger vocabularies.
- [Understanding Reference Policies in Direct Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.13709): Studies DPO's dependence on the reference policy, showing KL strength and reference-model similarity can cap or improve preference-tuning gains.
- [NeuroBind: Towards Unified Multimodal Representations for Neural Signals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.14020): Aligns EEG, fMRI, calcium imaging, and spiking data into pretrained vision-language embedding spaces to transfer high-resource modality models to neuroscience tasks.
- [Not All Noises Are Created Equally:Diffusion Noise Selection and Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.14041): Selects or optimizes diffusion noise by inversion stability, improving SDXL and SDXL-turbo generation quality without model fine-tuning.
- [Impact of Model Size on Fine-tuned LLM Performance in Data-to-Text Generation: A State-of-the-Art Investigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.14088): Compares 12 fine-tuned LLMs across five data-to-text datasets, finding larger models improve readability and informativeness but may reduce faithfulness.
- [Routing Experts: Learning to Route Dynamic Experts in Existing Multi-modal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.14093): Learns example-dependent dynamic expert paths inside existing MLLMs with structure-sparsity regularization, improving efficiency and accuracy without major architecture changes.
- [Mixture of Experts with Mixture of Precisions for Tuning Quality of Service](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.14417): Partially quantizes MoE experts and places them across CPU and GPU to expose throughput-quality configurations for Mixtral serving under resource constraints.
- [Evaluating language models as risk scores](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.14614): Introduces folktexts to generate census-based uncertainty tasks, showing multiple-choice LLM risk scores are predictive but badly miscalibrated.
- [BOND: Aligning LLMs with Best-of-N Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.14622): Distills best-of-N sampling into a policy distribution with Jeffreys divergence and a moving anchor, improving Gemma RLHF results without inference-time sampling overhead.
- [Performance Modeling and Workload Analysis of Distributed Large Language Model Training and Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.14645): Models distributed LLM training and inference across compute, memory, network, and parallelization strategies to expose future bottlenecks and technology-scaling effects.
- [Compact Language Models via Pruning and Knowledge Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.14679): Compresses Nemotron-4 models by combining depth, width, attention, and MLP pruning with distillation retraining, yielding Minitron models with far fewer training tokens.
- [TraveLLM: Could You Plan My Public Transit Alternatives in Face of a Network Disruption?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.14926): Uses LLM reasoning over natural-language trip requests and transit map data to plan disruption-aware public transit alternatives, then tests GPT-4, Claude 3, and Gemini on weather, emergency, and service-availability scenarios.
- [Generalization v.s. Memorization: Tracing Language Models' Capabilities Back to Pretraining Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.14985): Defines distributional memorization through correlations between model output probabilities and pretraining-data frequency, using task-gram counts on Pythia to separate memorization-heavy factual QA from more generalizing translation and reasoning tasks.
- [When Can Transformers Count to n?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.15160): Proves a counting phase transition for transformers: exact token counts are stable when embedding dimension reaches vocabulary size, but non-orthogonal representations make counting numerically unstable when the vocabulary is larger.
- [Discrete Flow Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.15595): Introduces discrete flow matching for high-dimensional discrete data, deriving sampling formulas for probability paths and scaling non-autoregressive models to coding benchmarks such as HumanEval and MBPP.
- [Conditional Language Policy: A General Framework For Steerable Multi-Objective Finetuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.15762): Presents Conditional Language Policy, a multi-objective finetuning framework that learns one steerable language policy whose inference-time controls trade off objectives such as creativity and safety without training separate models.
- [Towards Latent Masked Image Modeling for Self-Supervised Visual Representation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.15837): Analyzes why latent-space masked image modeling collapses or learns weak targets, then fixes joint target optimization, objective design, region correlation, and decoder conditioning to obtain higher-level self-supervised visual representations.
- [SlowFast-LLaVA: A Strong Training-Free Baseline for Video Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.15841): Builds a training-free video LLM baseline with Slow and Fast visual streams, preserving spatial detail at low frame rate while using pooled high-frame-rate features for motion cues under a fixed token budget.
- [DDK: Distilling Domain Knowledge for Efficient Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.16154): Proposes DDK, a distillation framework that reallocates training data across domains according to teacher-student performance gaps, improving small LLMs more stably than uniform distillation or continual pretraining.
- [UniMEL: A Unified Framework for Multimodal Entity Linking with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.16160): Frames multimodal entity linking as an LLM-assisted retrieval and reranking problem, using text-vision augmentation plus parameter-efficient final selection to handle ambiguity, redundancy, and noisy images.
- [VidyaRANG: Conversational Learning Based Platform powered by Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.16209): Describes a retrieval-augmented conversational learning platform where students query private videos and books through LLM-backed interfaces while course access, deployment, and API keys are managed for privacy.
- [PrimeGuard: Safe and Helpful LLMs through Tuning-Free Routing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.16318): Introduces PrimeGuard, a tuning-free inference-time guardrail that routes a request through differently instructed self-instantiations of the same LM to improve safety while preserving helpfulness.
- [PartGLEE: A Foundation Model for Recognizing and Parsing Any Objects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.16696): Presents PartGLEE, a part-level foundation model that uses a Q-Former to model object-part hierarchies for open-world detection, segmentation, grounding, and fine-grained image parsing.
- [Structure-aware Domain Knowledge Injection for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.16724): Introduces StructTuning, which extracts domain taxonomies for structure-aware continual pretraining and supervised finetuning, reducing domain knowledge-injection data needs while matching full-data performance.
- [DYNAMICQA: Tracing Internal Knowledge Conflicts in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.17023): Builds DynamicQA and two conflict measures to study how intra-memory conflict affects whether language models accept contextual facts, especially for temporally changing knowledge.
- [How Good (Or Bad) Are LLMs at Detecting Misleading Visualizations?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.17291): Evaluates LLMs and multimodal LLMs on misleading visualizations, testing whether models can identify deceptive chart design rather than only read surface-level visual content.
- [u-μP: The Unit-Scaled Maximal Update Parametrization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.17465): Extends maximal update parametrization with unit scaling, providing a parametrization that targets stable activations and updates across model widths without per-layer hyperparameter tuning.
- [CMR Scaling Law: Predicting Critical Mixture Ratios for Continual Pre-training of Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.17467): Derives a CMR scaling law for continual pretraining that predicts the critical mixture ratio between target-domain and general data needed to avoid performance degradation.
- [Model Collapse in the Self-Consuming Chain of Diffusion Finetuning: A Novel Perspective from Quantitative Trait Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.17493): Studies diffusion model collapse under self-consuming finetuning through quantitative-trait modeling, explaining how repeated training on generated data shifts trait distributions.
- [KiVA: Kid-inspired Visual Analogies for Testing Large Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.17773): Introduces KiVA, a kid-inspired visual analogy benchmark for large multimodal models that probes relational and analogical reasoning rather than ordinary image recognition.
- [Amortized Posterior Sampling with Diffusion Prior Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.17907): Combines diffusion priors with amortized posterior sampling so inverse-problem solvers can reuse distilled samplers instead of running expensive posterior sampling for each observation.
- [Recursive Introspection: Teaching Language Model Agents How to Self-Improve](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.18219): Fine-tunes agents with iterative failed-attempt correction so they learn how to revise their own responses after unsuccessful executions.
- [LoRA-Pro: Are Low-Rank Adapters Properly Optimized?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.18242): Analyzes LoRA optimization and proposes LoRA-Pro, which adjusts low-rank adapter gradients to better approximate full finetuning updates while retaining parameter efficiency.
- [Trust or Escalate: LLM Judges with Provable Guarantees for Human Agreement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.18370): Develops LLM judges with provable guarantees for agreement with human labels, giving a trust-or-escalate rule for when automated judgments should be accepted or deferred.
- [Physics Informed Kolmogorov-Arnold Neural Networks for Dynamical Analysis via Efficent-KAN and WAV-KAN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.18373): Applies Efficient-KAN and WAV-KAN variants to physics-informed dynamical analysis, comparing Kolmogorov-Arnold network formulations for learning differential-equation dynamics.
- [Right Now, Wrong Then: Non-Stationary Direct Preference Optimization under Preference Drift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.18676): Models preference drift in DPO, showing non-stationary preferences break standard assumptions and proposing a method that tracks changing preference distributions over time.
- [Towards Scalable and Stable Parallelization of Nonlinear RNNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.19115): Designs stable parallelization techniques for nonlinear RNNs, targeting sequence models that retain recurrent expressivity while improving scalability on modern accelerators.
- [LLaVA-Read: Enhancing Reading Ability of Multimodal Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.19185): Improves multimodal reading by training LLaVA-Read on document and text-rich visual data, targeting OCR-heavy comprehension that ordinary MLLMs often miss.
- [On Behalf of the Stakeholders: Trends in NLP Model Interpretability in the Era of LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.19200): Surveys NLP model interpretability in the LLM era through stakeholder needs, organizing methods by who uses explanations and what decisions those explanations support.
- [ML-Mamba: Efficient Multi-Modal Large Language Model Utilizing Mamba-2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.19832): Introduces ML-Mamba, a multimodal LLM architecture that incorporates Mamba-2 sequence modeling for efficient cross-modal understanding.
- [ByteCheckpoint: A Unified Checkpointing System for Large Foundation Model Development](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.20143): Presents ByteCheckpoint, a unified checkpointing system for foundation-model development that coordinates distributed save, load, and recovery under large training workloads.
- [AutoScale: Automatic Prediction of Compute-optimal Data Composition for Training LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.20177): Introduces AutoScale, which predicts compute-optimal pretraining data mixtures from small-scale experiments so LLM training can choose domain composition before full runs.
- [BRIDGE: Bridging Gaps in Image Captioning Evaluation with Stronger Visual Cues](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.20341): Creates BRIDGE for image-captioning evaluation with stronger visual cues, reducing failures where caption metrics reward language plausibility despite weak visual grounding.
- [SynthVLM: High-Efficiency and High-Quality Synthetic Data for Vision Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.20756): Proposes SynthVLM, a synthetic-data pipeline for vision-language models that balances generation efficiency with data quality for multimodal training.
- [MoFO: Momentum-Filtered Optimizer for Mitigating Forgetting in LLM Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.20999): Introduces MoFO, a momentum-filtered optimizer that mitigates forgetting during LLM finetuning by filtering update directions that damage retained capabilities.
- [Adaptive Pre-training Data Detection for Large Language Models via Surprising Tokens](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.21248): Detects whether text appeared in LLM pretraining data through surprising-token signals, offering a data-auditing method for privacy and copyright analysis.
- [Beyond Silent Letters: Amplifying LLMs in Emotion Recognition with Vocal Nuances](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.21315): Combines LLMs with vocal-nuance features for emotion recognition, testing whether speech cues can compensate for missing affective information in text-only inputs.
- [MoMa: Efficient Early-Fusion Pre-training with Mixture of Modality-Aware Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.21770): Presents MoMa, an early-fusion multimodal pretraining approach with modality-aware experts that route different input types through specialized expert modules.
- [Large Language Monkeys: Scaling Inference Compute with Repeated Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.21787): Studies inference-time compute scaling through repeated LLM sampling, showing how pass rates improve with many independent attempts and deriving allocation rules for sampling budgets.
