- [Contrastive Graph Representation Learning with Adversarial Cross-view Reconstruction and Information Bottleneck](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.00295): Combines adversarial cross-view reconstruction with an information bottleneck for contrastive graph representation learning, aiming to learn robust graph embeddings across views.
- [DynamoLLM: Designing LLM Inference Clusters for Performance and Energy Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.00741): Introduces DynamoLLM, a design and simulation framework for LLM inference clusters that jointly analyzes performance and energy efficiency under serving workloads.
- [MiniCPM-V: A GPT-4V Level MLLM on Your Phone](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.01800): Reports MiniCPM-V, a phone-deployable multimodal LLM that targets GPT-4V-level visual understanding with compact model size and mobile-friendly inference.
- [STBLLM: Breaking the 1-Bit Barrier with Structured Binary LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.01803): Develops STBLLM, a structured binary LLM approach that breaks the one-bit quantization barrier by preserving important structure during extreme compression.
- [Cross-layer Attention Sharing for Pre-trained Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.01890): Shares attention components across layers in pretrained LLMs, reducing redundancy while preserving performance through cross-layer attention reuse.
- [Mini-Monkey: Multi-Scale Adaptive Cropping for Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.02034): Introduces Mini-Monkey, which applies multi-scale adaptive cropping to multimodal LLM inputs so small models can inspect high-resolution image regions more effectively.
- [VL-TGS: Trajectory Generation and Selection Using Vision Language Models in Mapless Outdoor Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.02454): Uses vision-language models for trajectory generation and selection in mapless outdoor navigation, connecting visual scene understanding with candidate path choice.
- [UnifiedMLLM: Enabling Unified Representation for Multi-modal Multi-tasks With Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.02503): Builds UnifiedMLLM, a unified representation framework that lets one multimodal LLM handle multiple modalities and tasks through shared representation learning.
- [Self-Taught Evaluators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.02666): Trains evaluators from model-generated data, showing that LLMs can bootstrap judgment models without human labels by iteratively producing and scoring candidate responses.
- [KAN We Improve on HEP Classification Tasks? Kolmogorov–Arnold Networks Applied to an LHC Physics Example](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.02743): Applies Kolmogorov-Arnold networks to LHC high-energy-physics classification, comparing KAN behavior with established classifiers on a concrete scientific dataset.
- [Extend Model Merging from Fine-Tuned to Pre-Trained Large Language Models via Weight Disentanglement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03092): Extends model merging to pretrained LLMs by disentangling weights before merging, rather than limiting the technique to separately finetuned checkpoints.
- [500xCompressor: Generalized Prompt Compression for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03094): Introduces 500xCompressor, a prompt-compression method that aggressively shortens input context while preserving task-relevant information for LLM inference.
- [Scaling LLM Test-Time Compute Optimally can be More Effective than Scaling Model Parameters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03314): Derives test-time compute scaling rules showing when spending inference budget on sampling or search can outperform increasing model parameters.
- [LLaVA-OneVision: Easy Visual Task Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03326): Presents LLaVA-OneVision, a vision-language model designed for easy transfer across single-image, multi-image, and video tasks through unified visual instruction tuning.
- [ULLME: A Unified Framework for Large Language Model Embeddings with Generation-Augmented Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03402): Builds ULLME, a generation-augmented framework for LLM embeddings that uses generative learning signals to improve unified embedding representations.
- [Can DPO Learn Diverse Human Values? A Theoretical Scaling Law](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03459): Analyzes DPO under diverse human values and derives a theoretical scaling law for when preference optimization can learn multiple value distributions.
- [Can LLMs Serve As Time Series Anomaly Detectors?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03475): Tests whether LLMs can act as time-series anomaly detectors, probing how language-model priors transfer to numerical temporal patterns.
- [Empirical Analysis of Large Vision-Language Models against Goal Hijacking via Visual Prompt Injection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03554): Evaluates large vision-language models under visual prompt injection for goal hijacking, measuring whether malicious visual content can override intended instructions.
- [Smaller but Better: Self-Paced Knowledge Distillation for Lightweight yet Effective LCMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03680): Proposes self-paced knowledge distillation for lightweight LCMs, ordering distillation examples by difficulty so smaller models improve without matching full teacher cost.
- [DistTrain: Addressing Model and Data Heterogeneity with Disaggregated Training for Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.04275): Introduces DistTrain, a disaggregated training strategy for multimodal LLMs that handles model and data heterogeneity across training resources.
- [Img-Diff: Contrastive Data Synthesis for Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.04594): Creates Img-Diff, a contrastive multimodal data-synthesis method that generates image differences to strengthen vision-language model training signals.
- [Understanding the Performance and Estimating the Cost of LLM Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.04693): Models the performance and cost tradeoffs of LLM finetuning, helping estimate when finetuning is worthwhile relative to model size, data, and compute budget.
- [Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks for Online Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.04841): Evaluates Kolmogorov-Arnold networks for online reinforcement learning, testing whether spline-based KAN layers improve policy learning and robustness.
- [Gemma Scope: Open Sparse Autoencoders Everywhere All At Once on Gemma 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.05147): Releases Gemma Scope, a large suite of sparse autoencoders trained across Gemma 2 layers and widths for mechanistic interpretability of open LLMs.
- [KIF: Knowledge Identification and Fusion for Language Model Continual Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.05200): Introduces KIF for continual learning, identifying knowledge that should be retained or updated and fusing it to reduce forgetting in language models.
- [VITA: Towards Open-Source Interactive Omni Multimodal LLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.05211): Presents VITA, an open-source interactive omni-multimodal LLM that targets real-time speech, vision, and text interaction in a single model interface.
- [HoME: Hierarchy of Multi-Gate Experts for Multi-Task Learning at Kuaishou](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.05430): Describes HoME, Kuaishou's hierarchy of multi-gate experts for industrial multi-task learning with layered expert routing across many recommendation objectives.
- [LLMServingSim: A HW/SW Co-Simulation Infrastructure for LLM Inference Serving at Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.05499): Introduces LLMServingSim, a hardware-software co-simulation infrastructure for analyzing LLM inference serving at scale before deploying cluster configurations.
- [Efficient Diffusion Transformer with Step-wise Dynamic Attention Mediators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.05710): Improves diffusion transformers with step-wise dynamic attention mediators that adjust attention computation across denoising steps for more efficient generation.
- [Multimodal Large Language Models for Phishing Webpage Detection and Identification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.05941): Applies multimodal LLMs to phishing webpage detection and identification, combining visual page cues with language reasoning over suspicious content.
- [Towards Robust and Parameter-Efficient Knowledge Unlearning for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.06621): Proposes LoKU for parameter-efficient LLM unlearning, combining inverted hinge loss with Fisher-weighted low-rank initialization to remove sensitive knowledge while preserving fluency and reasoning.
- [Large language models can consistently generate high-quality content for election disinformation operations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.06731): Measures LLM compliance and perceived humanness for localized election-disinformation generation, releasing DisElect to quantify how readily models can produce harmful campaign content.
- [Layerwise Recurrent Router for Mixture-of-Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.06793): Introduces RMoE, a layerwise recurrent router that uses GRU state across consecutive MoE layers to improve expert selection, diversity, and parameter efficiency.
- [Language Models as Models of Language](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.07144): Examines language models as empirical models for linguistic theory, arguing that controlled training and causal interventions can inform debates on syntactic acquisition and competence.
- [Can Large Language Models Reason? A Characterization via 3-SAT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.07215): Characterizes LLM reasoning through random 3-SAT phase transitions, showing models fail on intrinsically harder instances while external solvers substantially improve results.
- [Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks (KAN) for Time Series Classification and Robust Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.07314): Benchmarks KAN, MLP, and hybrid networks on 128 time-series datasets, finding KAN-level robustness advantages tied to lower Lipschitz constants.
- [Graph Triple Attention Network: A Decoupled Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.07654): Proposes DeGTA, a graph triple attention network that decouples positional, structural, and attribute attention across local and global interactions for interpretable graph transformers.
- [FuseChat: Knowledge Fusion of Chat Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.07990): Introduces FuseChat, which first aligns diverse chat LLMs through lightweight pairwise fusion and token alignment, then merges target models in parameter space.
- [I-SHEEP: Self-Alignment of LLM from Scratch through an Iterative Self-Enhancement Paradigm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.08072): Presents I-SHEEP, an iterative self-enhancement paradigm where LLMs generate their own alignment data over repeated rounds to self-align from scratch.
- [BAM! Just Like That: Simple and Efficient Parameter Upcycling for Mixture of Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.08274): Introduces BAM for MoE upcycling, reusing both FFN and attention parameters from dense expert models through Branch-Attend-Mix and parallel attention computation.
- [5%>100%: Breaking Performance Shackles of Full Fine-Tuning on Visual Recognition Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.08345): Proposes Mona, a multi-cognitive visual adapter with vision-friendly filters and scaled normalization that outperforms full finetuning across detection, segmentation, and classification tasks.
- [MIA-Tuner: Adapting Large Language Models as Pre-training Text Detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.08661): Introduces MIA-Tuner, an instruction-based membership-inference method that makes LLMs act as internal pretraining-data detectors and evaluates them on the updated WIKIMIA-24 benchmark.
- [Rethinking Generative Semantic Communication for Multi-User Systems with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.08765): Rethinks multi-user generative semantic communication by using an LLM as a shared knowledge base for task decomposition, semantic representation specification, and semantic translation.
- [xGen-MM (BLIP-3): A Family of Open Large Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.08872): Releases the BLIP-3 framework with curated multimodal datasets, a training recipe, model architectures, and 4B and 14B LMM checkpoints for single-image, multi-image, and interleaved image-text inputs.
- [CyberPal.AI: Empowering LLMs with Expert-Driven Cybersecurity Instructions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.09304): Builds SecKnowledge, a cybersecurity instruction dataset generated from expert knowledge, and fine-tunes CyberPal.AI models evaluated with SecKnowledge-Eval and public security benchmarks.
- [Threshold Filtering Packing for Supervised Fine-Tuning: Training Related Samples within Packs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.09327): Packs SFT examples by threshold-filtering context-related samples with diversity, reducing cross-sequence contamination and improving GSM8K, HumanEval, and fairness benchmark results.
- [Out-of-distribution generalization via composition: A lens through induction heads in Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.09503): Studies hidden-rule OOD generalization and finds Transformers can compose two attention layers through a shared bridge representation to support induction-head rule learning.
- [Does Thought Require Sensory Grounding? From Pure Thinkers to Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.09605): Argues that sensory grounding is not a principled prerequisite for thought while using language-model results to discuss which cognitive capacities grounding may still enhance.
- [MoDeGPT: Modular Decomposition for Large Language Model Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.09632): Compresses Transformer blocks by decomposing module-level matrix pairs with Nystrom, CR, and SVD approximations, avoiding recovery fine-tuning while improving throughput.
- [Are Large Language Models More Honest in Their Probabilistic or Verbalized Confidence?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.09773): Compares token-probability confidence with verbalized confidence for factual knowledge boundaries, finding probabilistic signals better calibrated but still validation-set dependent.
- [Performance Law of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.09895): Proposes an empirical law that predicts MMLU from architecture hyperparameters and training-data size, giving a performance-level alternative to loss-only scaling estimates.
- [Personalizing Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback with Variational Preference Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.10075): Models pluralistic RLHF with user-specific latent variables that condition reward models and policies, enabling subgroup-aware preference learning without extra per-user labels.
- [SMILE: Zero-Shot Sparse Mixture of Low-Rank Experts Construction From Pre-Trained Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.10174): Converts pretrained source models into a sparse mixture of low-rank experts without data or additional training, using expanded dimensions to reduce parameter interference.
- [Transformers to SSMs: Distilling Quadratic Knowledge to Subquadratic Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.10189): Introduces MOHAWK, distilling Transformer knowledge into SSM and hybrid Mamba models by matching mixing matrices, hidden states, and end-to-end predictions.
- [Goldfish: Monolingual Language Models for 350 Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.10441): Releases more than 1,000 small monolingual models for 350 languages, showing 125M-parameter specialists can beat large multilingual models on low-resource grammar and perplexity tests.
- [LeCov: Multi-level Testing Criteria for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.10474): Defines nine LLM testing criteria over attention, feed-forward neurons, and uncertainty, then uses them for test prioritization and coverage-guided defect exploration.
- [Minor SFT loss for LLM fine-tune to increase performance and reduce model deviation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.10642): Proposes MinorSFT, an SFT loss and discrepancy metric designed to improve downstream fine-tuning while reducing drift from the original LLM.
- [HMoE: Heterogeneous Mixture of Experts for Language Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.10681): Uses MoE experts with different parameter capacities and a training objective that favors smaller experts when possible, improving specialization and activated-parameter efficiency.
- [Kilometer-scale convection-allowing model emulation using generative diffusion modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.10958): Presents StormCast, an autoregressive diffusion emulator for NOAA HRRR 3 km convection models that predicts 99 atmospheric variables from synoptic conditioning.
- [Scaling Law with Learning Rate Annealing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11029): Fits full language-model loss curves with a scaling law based on training-step area under the learning-rate curve and annealing area, predicting losses across schedulers.
- [Do Neural Scaling Laws Exist on Graph Self-Supervised Learning?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11243): Benchmarks graph self-supervised learning across model and data scales, finding downstream performance fails to scale even as SSL loss decreases.
- [Sycophancy in vision-language models: A systematic analysis and an inference-time mitigation framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11261): Evaluates LVLM susceptibility to leading visual-language prompts and mitigates it with query neutralization, sycophancy-aware contrastive decoding, and adaptive logit refinement.
- [FedMoE: Personalized Federated Learning via Heterogeneous Mixture of Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11304): Personalizes federated LLM fine-tuning by selecting client-specific sub-MoEs from activation patterns and aggregating them with modular expert updates.
- [SarcasmBench: Towards Evaluating Large Language Models on Sarcasm Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11319): Evaluates eleven LLMs and eight PLMs on six sarcasm datasets, showing current LLM prompting trails supervised sarcasm detectors and that CoT prompting is less effective.
- [Design Principle Transfer in Neural Architecture Search via Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11330): Uses an LLM to infer reusable neural-architecture design principles, refine them with new search results, and narrow transferable NAS search spaces.
- [Epistemic Injustice in Generative AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11441): Formalizes generative algorithmic epistemic injustice across testimonial, hermeneutical, and access harms, then sketches design and resistance strategies for knowledge ecosystems.
- [SCANS: Mitigating the Exaggerated Safety for LLMs via Safety-Conscious Activation Steering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11491): Identifies refusal steering vectors and safety-critical layers, then steers hidden-state transitions to reduce false refusals while preserving harmful-query defenses.
- [LLM Pruning and Distillation in Practice: The Minitron Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11796): Compresses Llama 3.1 8B and Mistral NeMo 12B with depth and width pruning followed by distillation and alignment, producing open Minitron checkpoints.
- [How Susceptible are LLMs to Influence in Prompts?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11865): Tests Llama, Mixtral, and Falcon variants with external predictions and explanations in prompts, showing models are easily swayed even by low-quality explanations.
- [Exploring Large Language Models for Feature Selection: A Data-centric Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.12025): Separates LLM feature selection into numerical data-driven and text-based semantic methods, finding text-based selection more robust across classification and regression tasks.
- [S-EPOA: Overcoming the Indivisibility of Annotations with Skill-Driven Preference-Based Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.12130): Adds unsupervised skill pretraining and skill-space query selection to preference-based RL, improving robustness and learning efficiency in manipulation and locomotion tasks.
- [PolyRouter: A Multi-LLM Querying System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.12320): Routes each query to a suitable expert LLM through TO-Router, targeting better cost, latency, and quality tradeoffs than single-model serving.
- [Enhancing Multi-hop Reasoning through Knowledge Erasure in Large Language Model Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.12456): Introduces KELE, which erases residual single-hop knowledge while injecting updated facts so edited LLMs handle multi-hop reasoning more reliably.
- [Show-o: One Single Transformer to Unify Multimodal Understanding and Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.12528): Combines autoregressive modeling with discrete diffusion in one Transformer for VQA, text-to-image generation, inpainting, extrapolation, and mixed-modality generation.
- [Jamba-1.5: Hybrid Transformer-Mamba Models at Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.12570): Releases 12B-active and 94B-active hybrid Transformer-Mamba MoE instruction models with 256K context and ExpertsInt8 quantization for efficient long-context inference.
- [A Percolation Model of Emergence: Analyzing Transformers Trained on a Formal Language](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.12578): Links Transformer emergence on a context-sensitive formal language to learning underlying grammar structures, modeled as a percolation phase transition.
- [Semantic Alignment for Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.12867): Introduces SAM, using bidirectional semantic guidance during multi-image visual-token extraction and the MmLINK dataset to improve group captioning and storytelling.
- [Power Scheduler: A Batch Size and Token Number Agnostic Learning Rate Scheduler](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.13359): Derives a power-law relation among optimal learning rate, batch size, and token count, then combines Power Scheduler with muP for transferable pretraining hyperparameters.
- [On the Robustness of Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks: An Adversarial Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.13809): Tests KANs under white-box and black-box adversarial attacks, finding large KANs more robust than comparable networks while smaller KANs are inconsistent.
- [MobileQuant: Mobile-friendly Quantization for On-device Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.13933): Jointly optimizes post-training weight transformations and activation ranges for integer-only LLM quantization compatible with mobile NPUs.
- [LLMs as Zero-shot Graph Learners: Alignment of GNN Representations with LLM Token Embeddings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.14512): Aligns GNN representations to LLM token embeddings and projects them into graph tokens, enabling zero-shot node classification and link prediction without tuning the LLM.
- [Training-Free Activation Sparsity in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.14690): Applies TEAL magnitude-based activation sparsity across hidden states without training, reaching 40 to 50 percent sparsity with sparse-kernel decoding speedups.
- [Diffusion Models Are Real-Time Game Engines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.14837): Shows diffusion models acting as real-time game engines, a concrete world-modeling capability.
- [Advancing Adversarial Suffix Transfer Learning on Aligned Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.14866): Speeds adversarial suffix search with DeGCG and i-DeGCG, separating behavior-agnostic pre-search from behavior-specific post-search to improve transfer attacks across models and data.
- [BaichuanSEED: Sharing the Potential of ExtensivE Data Collection and Deduplication by Introducing a Competitive Large Language Model Baseline](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15079): Opens a broad-collection and reweighting data pipeline, then validates it by pretraining BaichuanSEED 7B on 3T processed tokens plus simple SFT.
- [Can Unconfident LLM Annotations Be Used for Confident Conclusions?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15204): Proposes Confidence-Driven Inference, combining LLM labels, confidence indicators, and targeted human annotation to produce valid estimates with fewer human labels.
- [The Mamba in the Llama: Distilling and Accelerating Hybrid Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15237): Distills Llama-style Transformers into hybrid linear-RNN models by reusing attention projection weights and adds hardware-aware speculative decoding for faster generation.
- [SkillMimic: Learning Basketball Interaction Skills from Demonstrations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15270): Learns a unified imitation reward from human-object interaction demonstrations so one policy can acquire and transition among diverse basketball skills.
- [Divide, Conquer and Combine: A Training-Free Framework for High-Resolution Image Perception in Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15556): Introduces HR-Bench for 4K and 8K images and DC2, a training-free patch description and recombination framework for high-resolution MLLM perception.
- [LLaVA-MoD: Making LLaVA Tiny via MoE Knowledge Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15881): Distills large MLLMs into a small sparse-MoE LLaVA model using mimic distillation followed by DPO preference distillation with the teacher as reference.
- [Eagle: Exploring The Design Space for Multimodal LLMs with Mixture of Encoders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15998): Systematically ablates MLLM mixtures of vision encoders and resolutions, finding simple visual-token concatenation plus pre-alignment competitive with more complex fusion.
- [Law of Vision Representation in MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.16357): Defines an Alignment and Correspondence score that linearly predicts MLLM performance across vision representations, enabling vision-tower selection without repeatedly tuning the LLM.
- [Iterative Graph Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.16667): Uses VLM-generated logical graphs and reference answers to expose LLM rule-alignment gaps, then iteratively fine-tunes on helper-generated aligned responses.
- [Smaller, Weaker, Yet Better: Training LLM Reasoners via Compute-Optimal Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.16737): Shows weaker cheaper models can generate more compute-optimal synthetic reasoning data than stronger expensive models, improving distillation, self-improvement, and weak-to-strong training.
- [The future of open human feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.16961): Analyzes how an open human-feedback ecosystem could borrow peer-production and citizen-science practices while handling quality, incentives, privacy, and governance challenges.
- [VQ4DiT: Efficient Post-Training Vector Quantization for Diffusion Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.17131): Quantizes Diffusion Transformer weights with candidate assignment sets plus zero-data block-wise calibration, reaching 2-bit compression with acceptable image quality.
- [Joint Estimation and Prediction of City-wide Delivery Demand: A Large Language Model Empowered Graph-based Learning Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.17258): Combines spatiotemporal graph message passing with LLM-derived geospatial text embeddings to estimate, predict, and transfer delivery-demand models across cities.
- [Wait, that's not an option: LLMs Robustness with Incorrect Multiple-Choice Options](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.00113): Evaluates multiple-choice questions with no valid answer, showing post-training alignment can make models choose invalid options instead of refusing.
- [Sequence to Sequence Reward Modeling: Improving RLHF by Language Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.00162): Replaces scalar preference targets with sequence-to-sequence language feedback for reward modeling, reducing refusal and length-bias failures in RLHF without extra annotations.
- [A Hybrid Transformer-Mamba Network for Single Image Deraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.00410): Builds TransMamba with spectral-banded Transformer blocks, bidirectional Mamba state-space modules, dual-branch fusion, and spectral coherence loss for image deraining.
- [DataSculpt: Crafting Data Landscapes for LLM Post-Training through Multi-objective Partitioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.00997): Organizes long-context post-training data through coarse semantic clustering and fine multi-objective packing over relevance, homogeneity, integrity, and efficiency.
- [Duplex: A Device for Large Language Models with Mixture of Experts, Grouped Query Attention, and Continuous Batching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.01141): Designs a co-processing device that routes high-arithmetic-intensity LLM layers to xPU and low-intensity MoE or attention work to Logic-PIM.
- [Target-Driven Distillation: Consistency Distillation with Target Timestep Selection and Decoupled Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.01347): Improves few-step diffusion generation with target timestep selection, decoupled guidance for inference-time scale tuning, and optional non-equidistant sampling.
- [CHESS: Optimizing LLM Inference via Channel-Wise Thresholding and Selective Sparsification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.01366): Speeds edge LLM inference by assigning feed-forward thresholds per activation channel and selectively sparsifying attention layers with sparse kernels.
- [Imitating Language via Scalable Inverse Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.01369): Reformulates inverse soft-Q learning as a temporal-difference-regularized extension of MLE, improving SFT diversity and task performance on fixed language datasets.
- [Interpreting and Improving Large Language Models in Arithmetic Calculation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.01659): Identifies a small set of transferable attention heads and MLPs involved in arithmetic operands and operators, then improves math by selectively fine-tuning them.
- [Empirical evidence of Large Language Model's influence on human spoken communication](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.01754): Uses causal inference over YouTube talks and podcasts to detect post-ChatGPT increases in words preferentially generated by ChatGPT.
- [FC-KAN: Function Combinations in Kolmogorov-Arnold Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.01763): Extends KANs by combining B-splines, wavelets, radial basis functions, and related transformations through element-wise operations on low-dimensional data.
- [OLMoE: Open Mixture-of-Experts Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.02060): Releases OLMoE-1B-7B, a fully open sparse MoE language model pretrained on 5T tokens with open weights, data, code, logs, and instruct tuning.
- [Pooling And Attention: What Are Effective Designs For LLM-Based Embedding Models?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.02727): Compares LLM embedding pooling and attention designs under the same base model and data, then introduces multi-layer trainable pooling for retrieval and similarity tasks.
- [Masked Diffusion Models are Secretly Time-Agnostic Masked Models and Exploit Inaccurate Categorical Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.02908): Shows masked diffusion models are equivalent to time-agnostic masked models, introduces a faster first-hitting sampler, and exposes categorical-sampling numerical artifacts.
- [KAN See in the Dark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.03404): Applies KAN blocks and frequency-domain perception to low-light image enhancement, targeting nonlinear illumination and noise relationships with more interpretable transformations.
- [Fine-tuning large language models for domain adaptation: exploration of training strategies, scaling, model merging and synergistic capabilities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.03444): Compares CPT, SFT, DPO, ORPO, scaling, and model merging for materials-domain LLM adaptation, finding merged models can gain capabilities absent from either parent.
- [Attention heads of large language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.03752): Surveys attention-head roles through knowledge recalling, in-context identification, latent reasoning, and expression preparation, plus methods for discovering and evaluating special heads.
- [AnyMatch - Efficient Zero-Shot Entity Matching with a Small Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.04073): Fine-tunes a small transfer-learning model for zero-shot entity matching using difficult-pair selection, attribute-level examples, and label-imbalance control.
- [Towards Automated Knowledge Transfer in Evolutionary Multitasking via Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.04270): Uses LLMs in the SKTD framework to synthesize knowledge-transfer modules for evolutionary multitask optimization across heterogeneous task settings.
- [VILA-U: a Unified Foundation Model Integrating Visual Understanding and Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.04429): Unifies visual understanding and image generation in one autoregressive next-token framework with aligned discrete visual tokens and text tokens.
- [How Does Code Pretraining Affect Language Model Task Performance?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.04556): Runs controlled pretraining mixtures showing code improves structured-output and math tasks while potentially hurting syntax, morphology, and world-knowledge tasks.
- [BPE Gets Picky: Efficient Vocabulary Refinement During Tokenizer Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.04599): Refines BPE vocabularies during tokenizer training to remove under-trained tokens and improve vocabulary efficiency without harming compression.
- [POINTS: Improving Your Vision-language Model with Affordable Strategies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.04828): Trains a 9B VLM with ablated affordable strategies, including perplexity-filtered pretraining data and model-soup visual instruction tuning.
- [Improving Pretraining Data Using Perplexity Correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.05816): Selects pretraining documents by estimating correlations between LLM perplexity on web text and downstream benchmark performance, avoiding new large pretraining runs.
- [MMEvol: Empowering Multimodal Large Language Models with Evol-Instruct](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.05840): Evolves multimodal instruction data through fine-grained perception, cognitive reasoning, and interaction transformations to improve MLLMs across 13 vision-language tasks.
- [FLoRA: Federated Fine-Tuning Large Language Models with Heterogeneous Low-Rank Adaptations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.05976): Fixes noisy LoRA aggregation in federated LLM fine-tuning with stacking-based aggregation that supports heterogeneous adapter ranks across clients.
- [STUN: Structured-Then-Unstructured Pruning for Scalable MoE Pruning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.06211): Prunes MoE LLMs by first exploiting expert similarity for scalable expert pruning, then applying unstructured pruning to preserve performance at high sparsity.
- [Unveiling Markov heads in Pretrained Language Models for Offline Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.06985): Identifies last-token-focused Markov heads in pretrained decision transformers and adds GPT2-DTMA with Mixture of Attention to improve long-horizon offline RL.
- [Cross-Refine: Improving Natural Language Explanation Generation by Learning in Tandem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.07123): Uses paired generator and critic LLMs so explanations are iteratively revised from feedback rather than emitted in a single pass.
- [Gated Slot Attention for Efficient Linear-Time Sequence Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.07146): Extends bounded-memory ABC attention with a GLA-style gate, giving recurrent inference while improving recall-heavy sequence modeling.
- [Synthetic continued pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.07431): Converts scarce domain documents into synthetic paraphrased and question-answer pretraining text so models can absorb rare facts more data-efficiently.
- [Source2Synth: Synthetic Data Generation and Curation Grounded in Real Data Sources](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.08239): Generates and filters instruction data from user-provided source corpora, grounding synthetic examples in real documents rather than free-form model invention.
- [What Should We Engineer in Prompts? Training Humans in Requirement-Driven LLM Use](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.08775): Introduces ROPE, a human-training paradigm that teaches prompt writers to specify task requirements explicitly instead of relying on generic prompt tricks.
- [Adjoint Matching: Fine-tuning Flow and Diffusion Generative Models with Memoryless Stochastic Optimal Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.08861): Frames reward fine-tuning for flow and diffusion models as stochastic optimal control and derives Adjoint Matching with a required memoryless noise schedule.
- [Rethinking KenLM: Good and Bad Model Ensembles for Efficient Text Quality Filtering in Large Web Corpora](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.09613): Trains good-data and bad-data KenLM ensembles so web-corpus filtering can score both high-quality language patterns and low-quality artifacts.
- [Famba-V: Fast Vision Mamba with Cross-Layer Token Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.09808): Speeds Vision Mamba training by fusing similar tokens across layers instead of applying a uniform token-reduction rule everywhere.
- [StruEdit: Structured Outputs Enable the Fast and Accurate Knowledge Editing for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.10132): Edits LLM knowledge by turning reasoning traces into structured outputs, locating outdated facts, and revising only the affected reasoning components.
- [Quantile Regression for Distributional Reward Models in RLHF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.10164): Replaces scalar reward prediction with Quantile Reward Models that estimate a distribution over human preferences for RLHF.
- [Cognitive Kernel: An Open-source Agent System towards Generalist Autopilots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.10277): Releases an autopilot-oriented agent system with perception, action, memory, and coordination components for completing tasks end to end.
- [Towards semantic versioning of open pre-trained language model releases on hugging face](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.10472): Audits Hugging Face pretrained-model releases and proposes semantic versioning practices to make model dependency changes more traceable.
- [Flash STU: Fast Spectral Transform Units](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.10489): Interleaves spectral state-space layers with sliding-window attention to scale language models with near-linear sequence complexity.
- [Kolmogorov-Arnold Transformer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.10594): Replaces transformer MLP blocks with KAN layers and adds scaling fixes so Kolmogorov-Arnold networks can train inside large transformer architectures.
- [Model-in-the-Loop (MILO): Accelerating Multimodal AI Data Annotation with LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.10702): Puts LLMs into multimodal annotation workflows as pre-labelers and quality assistants while preserving human annotator oversight.
- [Unveiling and Mitigating Bias in Large Language Model Recommendations: A Path to Fairness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.10825): Measures demographic and cultural biases in LLM music, song, and book recommendations and tests mitigation strategies for fairer suggestions.
- [Diversify and Conquer: Diversity-Centric Data Selection with Iterative Refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.11378): Selects instruction-tuning data by iteratively balancing global diversity with example quality instead of ranking samples only by local scores.
- [NVLM: Open Frontier-Class Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.11402): Releases NVLM 1.0 and compares decoder-only, cross-attention, and hybrid multimodal LLM designs while preserving strong text-only performance after multimodal training.
- [Hardware Acceleration of Kolmogorov-Arnold Network (KAN) for Lightweight Edge Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.11418): Designs lookup-table-based hardware acceleration for B-spline functions in KANs to make lightweight edge inference practical.
- [From Lists to Emojis: How Format Bias Affects Model Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.11704): Shows reward models and human evaluators can over-reward surface formats such as lists, links, bold text, and emojis, creating exploitable alignment artifacts.
- [LLMs + Persona-Plug = Personalized LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.11901): Adds a lightweight Persona-Plug module that conditions a shared LLM on user-specific preferences without fine-tuning a separate model per user.
- [Reinforcement Learning with Lie Group Orientations for Robotics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.11935): Modifies RL policy inputs and outputs to respect Lie-group orientation geometry, improving robot learning without heavy architectural changes.
- [LifeGPT: topology-agnostic generative pretrained transformer model for cellular automata](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.12182): Trains a decoder-only transformer to model cellular automata dynamics across grid topologies and boundary conditions without hard-coded topology.
- [Exploring and Enhancing the Transfer of Distribution in Knowledge Distillation for Autoregressive Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.12512): Analyzes why reverse-KL distillation misses teacher distribution behavior and proposes improved objectives for autoregressive student models.
- [Scaling FP8 training to trillion-token LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.12517): Demonstrates trillion-token FP8 LLM training and traces long-run instability to SwiGLU outlier amplification, motivating stabilizing design changes.
- [Scaling Smart: Accelerating Large Language Model Pre-training with Small Model Initialization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.12903): Initializes large LLMs from pretrained smaller models so pretraining starts from transferred structure instead of random weights.
- [RRM: Robust Reward Model Training Mitigates Reward Hacking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.13156): Trains reward models to separate prompt-dependent preferences from prompt-independent artifacts such as length and format, reducing reward hacking.
- [Exploring Scaling Laws for Local SGD in Large Language Model Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.13198): Empirically maps when local SGD matches standard distributed LLM training across compute, model size, multi-cluster, and edge scenarios.
- [CITI: Enhancing Tool Utilizing Ability in Large Language Models without Sacrificing General Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.13202): Improves tool-use tuning with instructions that preserve general LLM capabilities instead of overfitting to narrow tool-call patterns.
- [Recent Advancement of Emotion Cognition in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.13354): Surveys LLM emotion cognition across emotion classification, emotionally rich response generation, and Theory-of-Mind-style evaluation.
- [SURf: Teaching Large Vision-Language Models to Selectively Utilize Retrieved Information](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.14083): Fine-tunes LVLMs with self-reflection so they can use helpful retrieved visual-text evidence while ignoring irrelevant or misleading references.
- [ERABAL: Enhancing Role-Playing Agents through Boundary-Aware Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.14710): Builds role-specific boundary dialogues and alignment training to keep role-playing LLMs consistent under subtle character-related queries.
- [Phantom of Latent for Large Language and Vision Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.14713): Expands latent hidden dimensions inside attention and applies Phantom Optimization to create compact LLVMs that compete with larger models.
- [DiSPo: Diffusion-SSM based Policy Learning for Coarse-to-Fine Action Discretization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.14719): Combines diffusion policy learning with Mamba state-space modeling so coarse demonstrations can generate actions at variable control scales.
- [Multi-Modal Generative AI: Multi-modal LLM, Diffusion and Beyond](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.14993): Surveys multimodal LLMs, diffusion models, and unified understanding-generation designs including autoregressive, diffusion, dense, and MoE choices.
- [Inference-Friendly Models With MixAttention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.15012): Combines sliding-window attention with cross-layer KV-cache sharing to reduce memory and latency while retaining short- and long-context quality.
- [Don’t Use LLMs to Make Relevance Judgments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.15133): Argues from TREC-style evaluation practice that LLM-generated relevance labels are not a reliable substitute for trained human assessors.
- [Harmonic Path Integral Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.15166): Derives a neural-network-free sampling method by mapping stochastic optimal control to an imaginary-time harmonic oscillator bridge.
- [Archon: An Architecture Search Framework for Inference-Time Techniques](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.15254): Searches over LLMs and inference-time techniques such as repeated sampling and revision to optimize accuracy under token-budget constraints.
- [MiSS: Revisiting the Trade-off in LoRA with an Efficient Shard-Sharing Structure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.15371): Shares trainable matrix shards across LoRA-style updates to improve the performance, memory, and serving trade-off of PEFT.
- [Beyond Turn-Based Interfaces: Synchronous LLMs as Full-Duplex Dialogue Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.15594): Adds real-time clock conditioning and synthetic spoken-dialogue training so Llama-style models can handle overlapping full-duplex conversation.
- [Empirical Insights on Fine-Tuning Large Language Models for Question-Answering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.15825): Shows that small QA SFT sets can activate pretrained knowledge, with performance depending on how memorized the fine-tuning examples are.
- [MonoFormer: One Transformer for Both Diffusion and Autoregression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.16280): Shares one transformer backbone between autoregressive text generation and diffusion image generation by switching attention masks.
- [Training Language Models to Win Debates with Self-Play Improves Judge Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.16636): Uses self-play debate training to produce stronger arguments that improve LLM judge accuracy on long-context reading-comprehension questions.
- [Mitigating Covariate Shift in Imitation Learning for Autonomous Vehicles Using Latent Space Generative World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.16663): Uses latent generative world models and a multi-view transformer encoder to train driving policies that recover from out-of-distribution perturbations.
- [GraphLoRA: Structure-Aware Contrastive Low-Rank Adaptation for Cross-Graph Transfer Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.16670): Adapts pretrained GNNs across graph domains with structure-aware MMD alignment, low-rank graph adapters, and regularization against forgetting.
- [Harnessing Diversity for Important Data Selection in Pretraining Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.16986): Introduces Quad, a pretraining-data selector that combines influence-style quality scoring with diversity to avoid redundant high-score samples.
- [Molmo and PixMo: Open Weights and Open Data for State-of-the-Art Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.17146): Releases open Molmo VLMs and PixMo datasets with detailed captions, free-form image QA, and 2D pointing data collected without proprietary VLM distillation.
- [Post-hoc Reward Calibration: A Case Study on Length Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.17407): Calibrates reward-model scores after training by estimating and removing length-bias terms without collecting new preference data.
- [HDFlow: Enhancing LLM Complex Problem-Solving with Hybrid Thinking and Dynamic Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.17433): Combines fast direct reasoning with dynamically generated slow workflows that decompose problems and call specialized LLM or symbolic tools.
- [MaskLLM: Learnable Semi-Structured Sparsity for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.17481): Learns N:M sparsity masks with Gumbel-Softmax distributions, enabling transferable semi-structured pruning for LLM inference.
- [A Scalable Data-Driven Framework for Systematic Analysis of SEC 10-K Filings Using Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.17581): Applies LLM scoring pipelines to SEC 10-K filings to compare company performance, strategy, sustainability, innovation, and workforce signals.
- [MIO: A Foundation Model on Multimodal Tokens](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.17692): Trains an autoregressive any-to-any foundation model over discrete speech, text, image, and video tokens with causal multimodal modeling.
- [Self-supervised Preference Optimization: Enhance Your Language Model with Preference Degree Awareness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.17791): Adds a self-supervised preference-degree loss to DPO-style alignment so pairwise training reflects graded preference strength.
- [PEDRO: Parameter-Efficient Fine-tuning with Prompt DEpenDent Representation MOdification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.17834): Generates prompt-conditioned representation-modification vectors inside transformer layers for efficient multi-tenant PEFT.
- [Infering Alt-text For UI Icons With Large Language Models During App Development](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.18060): Generates mobile-icon alt text from partial UI metadata such as class, resource ID, bounds, OCR text, and surrounding hierarchy.
- [EgoLM: Multi-Modal Language Model of Egocentric Motions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.18127): Projects egocentric video and sensor inputs into an LLM latent space for joint motion tracking, motion generation, and language understanding.
- [Data-Prep-Kit: getting your data ready for LLM application development](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.18164): Provides a scalable open-source toolkit of natural-language and code transforms for preparing LLM development data from local to cluster scale.
- [Policy Maps: Tools for Guiding the Unbounded Space of LLM Behaviors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.18203): Introduces policy maps and Policy Projector for interactively defining regions of LLM behavior and rule-based output interventions.
- [Do We Need Domain-Specific Embedding Models? An Empirical Investigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.18511): Builds FinMTEB and tests whether finance-specific embedding models outperform general embedding models trained on broad corpora.
- [Emu3: Next-Token Prediction is All You Need](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.18869): Tokenizes images, text, and video into a shared discrete space and trains one transformer with next-token prediction for perception and generation.
- [Exploring LLM-Driven Explanations for Quantum Algorithms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.19028): Compares GPT-3.5, Llama 2, and TinyLlama explanations of quantum algorithms and studies prompt style, consistency, and description-improvement use.
- [HybridFlow: A Flexible and Efficient RLHF Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.19256): Combines single-controller and multi-controller execution to express RLHF dataflow while reducing dispatch overhead in distributed post-training.
- [CLIP-MoE: Towards Building Mixture of Experts for CLIP with Diversified Multiplet Upcycling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.19291): Fine-tunes complementary CLIP variants with diversified multiplet upcycling and merges them into a dynamic CLIP-MoE.
- [The Crucial Role of Samplers in Online Direct Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.19605): Analyzes DPO convergence under sampling choices and introduces an online sampler with posterior and logit-mixing improvements.
- [Hyper-Connections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.19606): Replaces residual connections with depth-adaptive hyper-connections that mitigate gradient vanishing and representation collapse in dense and sparse LLM pretraining.
- [Hybrid Mamba for Few-Shot Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.19613): Designs cross-Mamba support-query fusion to replace quadratic cross-attention in few-shot segmentation.
- [Balancing Cost and Effectiveness of Synthetic Data Generation Strategies for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.19759): Compares answer augmentation, question rephrasing, and new-question synthesis under seed-data and query-budget constraints for LLM fine-tuning.
- [Calibrating Language Models with Adaptive Temperature Scaling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.19817): Predicts token-level temperature parameters from features to repair calibration degradation after RLHF.
- [RouterDC: Query-Based Router by Dual Contrastive Learning for Assembling Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.19886): Trains an encoder-based LLM router with dual contrastive losses so queries can be assigned among multiple strong models.
- [Scaling Optimal LR Across Token Horizons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.19913): Establishes a scaling law for transferring optimal learning rates from short LLM pretraining runs to longer token horizons.
- [GUNDAM: Aligning Large Language Models with Graph Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.20053): Aligns LLMs to graph structure with graph-understanding tasks that emphasize topology rather than only textual node attributes.
- [VMAD: Visual-Enhanced Multimodal Large Language Model for Zero-Shot Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.20146): Adapts MLLMs for industrial anomaly detection with visual enhancement for fine-grained defect recognition and open-ended anomaly descriptions.
- [Scaling Proprioceptive-Visual Learning with Heterogeneous Pre-trained Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.20537): Pretrains HPT policy trunks across heterogeneous robot embodiments by tokenizing distinct proprioceptive and visual inputs into a shared representation.
- [MM1.5: Methods, Analysis & Insights from Multimodal LLM Fine-tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.20566): Develops MM1.5 dense and MoE MLLMs with data-centric OCR, synthetic-caption, and visual-instruction mixtures, plus video and mobile-UI variants.
- [ACE: All-round Creator and Editor Following Instructions via Diffusion Transformer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.00086): Uses a Long-context Condition Unit and diffusion transformer training to unify multi-condition visual creation and editing tasks.
- [DreamStruct: Understanding Slides and User Interfaces via Synthetic Data Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.00201): Generates labeled slide and UI datasets with code synthesis, reducing human annotation for element recognition, description, and content classification.
- [Comprehensive Performance Modeling and System Design Insights for Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.00273): Models transformer training performance across parallelism strategies, accelerator capacity, bandwidth, and interconnect needs for LLM and scientific foundation-model workloads.
- [Ask, Pose, Unite: Scaling Data Acquisition for Close Interactions with Vision Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.00309): Uses LVLM-derived contact maps to create pseudo-ground-truth human mesh pairs and train diffusion contact priors for close-interaction HME.
- [Uncertainty-aware Reward Model: Teaching Reward Models to Know What is Unknown](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.00847): Models reward uncertainty with a probabilistic value head and ensemble disagreement, improving RewardBench and making best-of-n, iterative DPO, and PPO alignment more reliable.
- [MAP: Unleashing Hybrid Mamba-Transformer Vision Backbone’s Potential with Masked Autoregressive Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.00871): Uses Masked Autoregressive Pretraining to combine MAE-style reconstruction with autoregressive prediction for hybrid Mamba-Transformer vision backbones, improving both 2D and 3D recognition results.
- [Generative AI and Perceptual Harms: Who's Suspected of using LLMs?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.00906): Studies perceptual harm from suspected LLM use through three online experiments on freelance-writer profiles, showing that AI-use suspicion can reduce writing evaluations and hiring outcomes.
- [Approximately Aligned Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01103): Introduces AprAD, a speculative-decoding-inspired constrained generation method that trades distribution distortion against resampling cost for long outputs with hard alignment constraints.
- [Softmax Is Not Enough (for Sharp Out-of-distribution)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01104): Proves that softmax circuits struggle to approximate sharp functions as input size grows, and tests adaptive temperature as an inference-time patch for sharper size generalization.
- [ConServe: Fine-Grained GPU Harvesting for LLM Online and Offline Co-Serving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01228): Builds ConServe, an LLM co-serving system with token-level scheduling, layer-wise preemption, and incremental KV-cache management to harvest GPU idle cycles for offline work while protecting online latency.
- [HelpSteer2-Preference: Complementing Ratings with Preferences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01257): Adds preference labels and justifications to HelpSteer2 ratings, then compares Bradley-Terry and regression reward models and combines them for Nemotron reward and instruction tuning.
- [Speculative Coreset Selection for Task-Specific Fine-tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01296): Introduces STAFF, which scores candidate fine-tuning examples with a smaller same-family model and verifies regions on the target LLM to reduce coreset-selection overhead.
- [The Labyrinth of Links: Navigating the Associative Maze of Multi-modal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01417): Defines annotation-free association tasks for MLLMs, covering single-step, synchronous, and asynchronous links between observations and prior concepts, and compares open and closed models against humans.
- [DLP-LoRA: Efficient Task-Specific LoRA Fusion with a Dynamic, Lightweight Plugin for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01497): Introduces DLP-LoRA, a small MLP plugin that fuses multiple LoRA adapters at sentence level with top-p routing, improving composite-task adaptation while limiting inference overhead.
- [Fira: Can We Achieve Full-rank Training of LLMs Under Low-rank Constraint?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01623): Proposes Fira, a low-rank optimizer framework that preserves low-rank memory use while approximating full-rank training through norm-based scaling and a norm-growth limiter.
- [U-shaped and Inverted-U Scaling behind Emergent Abilities of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01692): Explains emergent abilities through U-shaped scaling on hard questions and inverted-U behavior on easy questions, then uses Slice-and-Sandwich to predict emergence thresholds.
- [LASeR: Learning to Adaptively Select Reward Models with Multi-Armed Bandits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01735): Frames reward-model choice as a multi-armed bandit in LASeR, selecting task-appropriate reward models during iterative LLM training to improve reasoning, instruction following, and long-context generation.
- [Leopard: A Vision Language Model For Text-Rich Multi-Image Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01744): Presents Leopard, a text-rich multi-image VLM with about one million instruction examples and adaptive high-resolution image encoding for slides, documents, and web snapshots.
- [Not All LLM Reasoners Are Created Equal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01748): Measures compositional grade-school math reasoning by chaining paired word problems, showing a reasoning gap when the second answer depends on the first.
- [Quantifying Generalization Complexity for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01769): Introduces Scylla, a dynamic evaluation framework that separates memorization from generalization with in-distribution and shifted task variants.
- [Plug-and-Play Controllable Generation for Discrete Masked Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02143): Uses importance sampling to control discrete masked-model generation without task-specific fine-tuning, gradients, or conditional-score training.
- [EmbedLLM: Learning Compact Representations of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02223): Learns compact vector embeddings of Hugging Face LLMs through an encoder-decoder setup, enabling model routing and benchmark-performance forecasting without repeated inference.
- [Theoretical Insights into Fine-Tuning Attention Mechanism: Generalization and Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02247): Analyzes attention fine-tuning and finds value matrices matter more than key matrices, with customized learning rates for query, key, and value weights improving convergence.
- [Encryption-Friendly LLM Architecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02486): Modifies Transformer components with LoRA fine-tuning and Gaussian kernels for homomorphic-encryption-friendly private adaptation, reporting 6.94x fine-tuning and 2.3x inference speedups.
- [GUD: Generation with Unified Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02667): Unifies diffusion design choices over representation basis, prior covariance, and component-wise noise schedules, bridging diffusion and autoregressive generation through soft-conditioning models.
- [FAN: Fourier Analysis Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02675): Builds Fourier Analysis Networks by embedding the Fourier principle into neural layers, improving periodicity modeling while retaining general-purpose MLP-like applicability.
- [Selective Attention Improves Transformer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02703): Adds a parameter-free selective-attention modification that downweights unnecessary context tokens, improving language modeling while shrinking attention memory for long contexts.
- [LLMs Know More Than They Show: On the Intrinsic Representation of LLM Hallucinations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02707): Finds truthfulness signals concentrated in specific internal tokens, shows detectors do not generalize universally, and predicts likely error types from LLM representations.
- [Justice or Prejudice? Quantifying Biases in LLM-as-a-Judge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02736): Quantifies LLM-as-a-judge biases from length, position, and preference factors, showing how judge models can distort benchmark evaluation and supervised reward signals.
- [Revisit Large-Scale Image-Caption Data in Pre-training Multimodal Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02740): Revisits image-caption pretraining data by separating caption rewriting and filtering effects, showing how caption quality and diversity change multimodal foundation-model performance.
- [Neutral residues: revisiting adapters for model extension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02744): Reworks adapters so added residual blocks output near zero on the original domain, improving new-language adaptation while preserving English performance.
- [Contrastive Localized Language-Image Pre-Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02746): Introduces CoLLIP, a localized language-image pretraining objective that aligns region-level visual evidence with text rather than relying only on global contrastive pairs.
- [Theoretically Grounded Framework for LLM Watermarking: A Distribution-Adaptive Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02890): Jointly optimizes watermarking and detection under false-positive and distortion constraints, deriving distribution-adaptive watermark schemes.
- [Leveraging Model Guidance to Extract Training Data from Personalized Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03039): FineXtract extrapolates personalized diffusion checkpoints to recover likely fine-tuning images and expose data-leakage risks.
- [CommonIT: Commonality-Aware Instruction Tuning for Large Language Models via Data Partitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03077): Partitions instruction data by commonality in CommonIT, sampling shared and specialized examples to improve LLM instruction tuning without treating all prompts as equally useful.
- [Scaling Parameter-Constrained Language Models with Quality Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03083): Studies parameter-constrained language models and shows that higher-quality data can recover scaling gains when model size is fixed, using controlled pretraining comparisons.
- [ARB-LLM: Alternating Refined Binarizations for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03129): Introduces a 1-bit post-training quantization recipe with alternating refined binarization, calibration-aware variants, and column-group bitmaps to reduce LLM weight quantization error.
- [Surgical, Cheap, and Flexible: Mitigating False Refusal in Language Models via Single Vector Ablation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03415): Extracts and ablates a false-refusal direction from language-model activations, reducing safe-request refusals while preserving safety and general capabilities.
- [Steering Large Language Models between Code Execution and Textual Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03524): Finds steering vectors that shift LLM behavior between code execution and textual reasoning, providing activation-level control over when models externalize computation.
- [Understanding Large Language Models in Your Pockets: Performance Study on COTS Mobile Devices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03613): Adds a model-side method, training recipe, architecture, or data method for other capability coverage.
- [Unraveling Cross-Modality Knowledge Conflicts in Large Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03659): Defines cross-modality parametric knowledge conflict in LVLMs, detects visual-textual answer mismatches, and mitigates them with dynamic contrastive decoding or prompts.
- [Teuken-7B-Base & Teuken-7B-Instruct: Towards European LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03730): Reports Teuken-7B, a European multilingual LLM trained from scratch with balanced language data and instruction tuning for European-language coverage.
- [Unsupervised Human Preference Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03731): Uses a small local preference-agent model to generate natural-language rules that steer a larger frozen foundation model toward an individual user's preferences.
- [Khattat: Enhancing Readability and Concept Representation of Semantic Typography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03748): Contributes a model-side method relevant to general foundation-model methods.
- [Denoising with a Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03755): Extends joint-embedding predictive architectures with denoising, training representations to recover clean latent targets from corrupted inputs instead of reconstructing pixels or tokens directly.
- [Hidden in Plain Text: Emergence & Mitigation of Steganographic Collusion in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03768): Elicits LLM-generated linguistic steganography with gradient-based and in-context reinforcement learning, showing hidden collusion can emerge from misspecified rewards and resist paraphrasing oversight.
- [Determine-Then-Ensemble: Necessity of Top-k Union for Large Language Model Ensembling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03777): Shows LLM ensembling needs a top-k union over candidate tokens before reranking, preventing individual models from hiding useful alternatives during ensemble decoding.
- [DaWin: Training-free Dynamic Weight Interpolation for Robust Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03782): Introduces DaWin, a training-free adaptation method that dynamically interpolates model weights across domain-specialized checkpoints for robust test-time transfer.
- [GraphRouter: A Graph-based Router for LLM Selections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03834): Routes queries to LLMs with a graph over tasks, queries, and models so selection can generalize to new tasks and balance quality against cost.
- [Can Language Models Reason about Individualistic Human Values and Preferences?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03868): Contributes model-side other methods, such as training, architecture, post-training, synthetic-data, verifier, or world-model techniques.
- [Scaling Graph Neural Networks to Large Proteins](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03921): Optimizes GNN force-field models and benchmarks for large biomolecular systems, targeting efficient molecular-dynamics simulation on protein-scale graphs.
- [LLM-TOPLA: Efficient LLM Ensemble by Maximising Diversity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03953): Prunes LLM ensembles using a focal-diversity metric and learns to resolve output inconsistencies, improving constrained and generative tasks with smaller sub-ensembles.
- [LoRTA: Low Rank Tensor Adaptation of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.04060): Uses low-rank tensor adaptation in LoRTA, replacing independent LoRA matrices with tensor-structured updates to share adaptation factors across layers and tasks.
- [Toxic Subword Pruning for Dialogue Response Generation on Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.04155): Prunes BPE subwords contained in toxic words from trained LLM tokenizers, reducing toxic dialogue generation without weight updates and sometimes improving response diversity.
- [Learning on LoRAs: GL-Equivariant Processing of Low-Rank Weight Spaces for Large Finetuned Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.04207): Treats LoRA weights as inputs for downstream prediction or edit generation, developing symmetry-aware invariant and equivariant models over low-rank weight spaces.
- [QKAN: quantum Kolmogorov-Arnold networks with applications in machine learning and multivariate state preparation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.04435): Defines quantum Kolmogorov-Arnold networks with recursive block encodings, applying them to quantum learning models and multivariate quantum state preparation.
- [TimeBridge: Non-Stationarity Matters for Long-term Time Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.04442): Introduces TimeBridge, a time-series forecasting architecture that models non-stationarity through multi-scale temporal pattern bridging for long-horizon prediction.
- [G\"odel Agent: A Self-Referential Agent Framework for Recursive Self-Improvement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.04444): Builds a self-referential agent that searches and rewrites its own design space for recursive self-improvement beyond fixed human-authored pipelines.
- [Large Language Model Inference Acceleration: A Comprehensive Hardware Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.04466): Surveys LLM inference acceleration from a hardware perspective, organizing bottlenecks and optimizations across compute, memory, communication, quantization, and serving systems.
- [SITCOM: Step-wise Triple-Consistent Diffusion Sampling for Inverse Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.04479): Solves diffusion inverse problems with step-wise triple-consistent sampling, enforcing measurement, forward-diffusion, and backward-diffusion consistency at each reverse step.
- [Upsample or Upweight? Balanced Training on Heavily Imbalanced Datasets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.04579): Analyzes low-resource data balancing, proving upsampling and loss upweighting diverge under stochastic gradients and giving regime-dependent guidance for multilingual LM training.
- [The LLM Effect: Are Humans Truly Using LLMs, or Are They Being Influenced By Them Instead?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.04699): Runs human-subject experiments on the LLM Effect, separating actual LLM use from social influence when people judge whether text was AI-assisted.
- [Strong Model Collapse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.04840): Formalizes strong model collapse, showing how repeated training on model-generated data can drive generative models toward degraded distributions under stronger assumptions.
- [VLM2Vec: Training Vision-Language Models for Massive Multimodal Embedding Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05160): Trains VLM2Vec to turn vision-language models into universal multimodal embedding models for image-text, text-image, and interleaved multimodal retrieval tasks.
- [Warmup-Stable-Decay Learning Rates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05192): explains warmup-stable-decay schedules through a river-valley loss-landscape view.
- [Cookbook: A framework for improving LLM generative abilities via programmatic data generating templates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05224): Introduces Cookbook, a programmatic template framework that generates targeted training data to improve LLM generative abilities through controllable data recipes.
- [Falcon Mamba: The First Competitive Attention-free 7B Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05355): Reports Falcon Mamba, an attention-free 7B language model based on Mamba sequence modeling that competes with Transformer LLMs at long-context inference.
- [Model-GLUE: Democratized LLM Scaling for A Large Model Zoo in the Wild](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05357): Benchmarks model merging, MoE, and stacking over a heterogeneous LLM zoo, then derives a clustering-and-aggregation guideline for scaling from existing models.
- [Can LLMs Understand Time Series Anomalies?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05440): Tests zero-shot and few-shot anomaly detection hypotheses for LLMs on time-series inputs, finding that image-style representations work better than textualized series.
- [Adaptation Odyssey in LLMs: Why Does Additional Pretraining Sometimes Fail to Improve?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05581): Studies why additional pretraining can fail, tracing adaptation problems to data mixture, optimization, and capability interference across continued-pretraining stages.
- [Scaling Laws Across Model Architectures: A Comparative Analysis of Dense and MoE Models in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05661): Compares dense and MoE LLM scaling, finding power-law loss scaling transfers to MoE while MoE models generalize better under the same training compute.
- [PostCast: Generalizable Postprocessing for Precipitation Nowcasting via Unsupervised Blurriness Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05805): Adds PostCast, an unsupervised blurriness-modeling postprocessor that sharpens precipitation nowcasting outputs and generalizes across forecasting backbones.
- [Time Transfer: On Optimal Learning Rate and Batch Size In The Infinite Data Limit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05838): Studies learning-rate and batch-size scaling in the infinite-data limit, showing optimal batch size tracks a growing critical batch size and transfers under muP scaling.
- [EMMA: Empowering Multi-modal Mamba with Structural and Hierarchical Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05938): Builds EMMA by adding structural and hierarchical alignment to multimodal Mamba, improving cross-modal representation learning in a sequence-state architecture.
- [PDF-WuKong: A Large Multimodal Model for Efficient Long PDF Reading with End-to-End Sparse Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05970): Builds a long-PDF MLLM with a sparse sampler over text and image representations, selecting query-relevant paragraphs or diagrams for document question answering.
- [Aria: An Open Multimodal Native Mixture-of-Experts Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05993): Releases an open native multimodal MoE model trained through four stages for language, multimodal, long-context, and instruction-following capabilities.
- [Training-free LLM-generated Text Detection by Mining Token Probability Sequences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06072): Introduces Lastde, a training-free detector that mines local and global token-probability time-series statistics to identify LLM-generated text across domains and models.
- [Treat Visual Tokens as Text? But Your MLLM Only Needs Fewer Efforts to See](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06169): Streamlines LLaVA visual computation with neighbor-aware visual-token attention, inactive-head pruning, and selective layer dropping to reduce MLLM inference cost.
- [RelitLRM: Generative Relightable Radiance for Large Reconstruction Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06231): Introduces RelitLRM, a large reconstruction model that generates relightable radiance fields so reconstructed 3D assets can be rendered under new illumination.
- [MC-MoE: Mixture Compressor for Mixture-of-Experts LLMs Gains More](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06270): Compresses MoE LLMs without retraining by combining expert-importance and token-importance signals with pre-loading mixed-precision quantization.
- [Sketch to Adapt: Fine-Tunable Sketches for Efficient LLM Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06364): Adapts LLMs through fine-tunable compressed sketches of model weights, avoiding low-rank adapter assumptions while improving memory and training efficiency.
- [TorchTitan: One-stop PyTorch native solution for production ready LLM pre-training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06511): Provides a PyTorch-native distributed pretraining stack for composing and comparing large-scale LLM training techniques across thousands of accelerators.
- [MotionRL: Align Text-to-Motion Generation to Human Preferences with Multi-Reward Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06513): Fine-tunes text-to-motion generators with multi-reward reinforcement learning over text adherence, motion quality, and human-preference objectives, giving controllable Pareto tradeoffs.
- [Rodimus*: Breaking the Accuracy-Efficiency Trade-Off with Efficient Attentions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06577): Combines recurrent linear attention, data-dependent tempered selection, and sliding-window shared-key attention to lower per-token memory while preserving LLM accuracy.
- [Learning Evolving Tools for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06617): Trains LLMs to learn evolving tools, updating tool representations and usage policies as tool APIs or task requirements change.
- [Towards Universality: Studying Mechanistic Similarity Across Language Model Architectures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06672): Compares mechanistic features across language-model architectures to test whether circuits and representations are universal or architecture-specific.
- [Break the Visual Perception: Adversarial Attacks Targeting Encoded Visual Tokens of Large Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06699): Attacks encoded visual tokens in LVLMs by disrupting feature representations, token relationships, and semantics, producing transferable adversarial images across shared encoders.
- [MatMamba: A Matryoshka State Space Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06718): Introduces MatMamba, a Matryoshka state-space model whose nested channels allow elastic submodel extraction while preserving Mamba-style sequence efficiency.
- [Compositional Entailment Learning for Hyperbolic Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06912): Uses compositional entailment learning in hyperbolic vision-language models, aligning image-text representations through hierarchy-aware entailment relations.
- [Self-Boosting Large Language Models with Synthetic Preference Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06961): Uses self-generated prompts and progressively improved responses to create synthetic preference data, enabling iterative self-alignment without human preference labels.
- [Data Selection via Optimal Control for Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.07064): Formulates LM pretraining data selection as an optimal-control problem and approximates Pontryagin conditions to choose CommonCrawl data that improves scaling efficiency.
- [Towards Interpreting Visual Information Processing in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.07149): Uses token ablations and vocabulary-space analysis in LLaVA to show how visual-token object information becomes interpretable and feeds final-token predictions.
- [Deciphering Cross-Modal Alignment in Large Vision-Language Models with Modality Integration Rate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.07167): Defines Modality Integration Rate as a pre-SFT metric for LVLM pretraining quality, linking inter-modal distribution distance to later benchmark performance.
- [One Initialization to Rule them All: Fine-tuning via Explained Variance Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.07170): Proposes EVA, initializing parameter-efficient fine-tuning updates from explained-variance directions so adapters start in high-impact representation subspaces.
- [IterGen: Iterative Semantic-aware Structured LLM Generation with Backtracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.07295): Adds grammar-guided structured generation with forward and backward moves, letting users backtrack and refine partially generated LLM outputs.
